Plans for review · 12 Oct 2026 · kern

kern Build Plans

Implementation plans for the first build, M0 to M2, written from the approved spec. Each milestone is its own plan with test-first tasks; the shared contract fixes the names, signatures and exit codes all three use. Everything moves to docs/plans/ in the kern repo at M0 Task 1.

M0 · 9 tasksRepo, licences, lint, routing evals, CI · 4 to 6 h
M1 · 18 tasksKernel, hooks, modes, guards G1 to G6, G13 to G16 · 10 to 14 h
M2 · 24 tasks11 skills, 3 agents, worktree and check scripts, G18, pilot · 19 to 25 h

kern M0 Implementation Plan

For agentic workers: REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (- [ ]) syntax for tracking.

Goal: Stand up the kern repo as a valid Claude Code plugin and marketplace with licensing, lint, routing evals and CI, all green on a placeholder skill.

Architecture: One git repo at ~/Code/projects/kern that is both the plugin (.claude-plugin/plugin.json) and its own marketplace (.claude-plugin/marketplace.json, source: "./"). Dev tooling is zero-dependency Node ESM (scripts/*.mjs, tested with node --test) plus bash (scripts/fetch-upstream, tested with bats). Upstream sources that later milestones port from are cloned at pinned commits into a gitignored .upstream/.

Tech Stack: Node 22 (built-in node:test, no npm dependencies), bash 3.2+ (macOS default) and POSIX tools, bats-core, shellcheck, git, gh, Claude Code 2.1.296 (claude plugin validate --strict).

Spec: docs/specs/2026-10-12-kern-design.md (sections 2, 3.4, 11, 12, 14) and docs/specs/2026-10-12-kern-m1-m2-skills.md. Shared names and signatures: docs/plans/contract.md. Executors read the spec, the contract and this plan.

Global Constraints

  • Plugin name kern; skills invoked as /kern:<skill> (D-1).
  • Claude Code only (D-3). Minimum Claude Code version for development: 2.1.295 (hook onFailure needs it); pinned in CI: 2.1.296.
  • kern's own licence: MIT. Copyright line: Copyright (c) 2026 Mustafa Mohamed.
  • Sources and pins: superpowers 6.4.1, MIT, 2025 Jesse Vincent, commit 5bf4e78011075bcfc0dc295f0724994cd123ee71; cursor/plugins pstack/ 0.15.17, MIT, 2026 Lauren Tan, 11713c6; mattpocock/skills 1.3.1, MIT, 2026 Matt Pocock, 49dd158; addyosmani/agent-skills 0.6.12, MIT, 2025 Addy Osmani, 1be8e34; caveman 3.2.0, Apache-2.0, 2026 Julius Brussee, installed cache.
  • "Caveman", "ultracave" and "megacave" are trademarks: never a name, file name, mode or skill in kern. The mode is terse.
  • NOTICE reproduces caveman's NOTICE content (Apache-2.0 section 4d). Files modified from an Apache-2.0 source carry a header line saying they were changed. Scripts adapted from any source carry a one-line source comment. SKILL.md files carry none; the NOTICE map covers them.
  • Description limits: auto skills at most 300 chars with a trigger phrase, at most 1,500 chars total; path and slash skills at most 120 chars; agents at most 150 chars; auto skills plus agents at most 2,500 chars.
  • Lint rule 8: no machine paths or personal names in shipped files (skills/, agents/, kernel/, modes/, hooks/).
  • Lint rule 11: no long dashes (U+2013, U+2014) and no curly quotes (U+2018, U+2019, U+201C, U+201D) in shipped files, README.md and CHANGELOG.md.
  • Versioning: semver; Keep-a-Changelog with an Unreleased section; plugin.json, marketplace.json and the newest CHANGELOG release agree.
  • Routing evals: every auto skill has a case file with at least 3 positive prompts, 2 negative prompts and 1 behavioural eval; the rank-1 rate floor only rises.
  • Commits in this repo: subject M0: <imperative summary>, 72 chars or fewer; body says why; no attribution trailers; stage named paths only (git add -- <paths>), never -A. Pushes and the GitHub repo creation happen only after the user says yes.
  • Prose in every file follows unslop: answer first, no filler, no long dashes, no curly quotes.

Review Focus

  1. A skill file with Windows line endings. A SKILL.md saved with \r\n must parse the same as \n; otherwise lint reports a missing frontmatter on a valid file. Pinned in Task 5, test parses CRLF frontmatter.
  2. A description that is quoted, or carries a colon. description: "Use when: x" must lint by its unquoted text and length. Pinned in Task 5, test strips quotes and keeps colons in values.
  3. An eval prompt that shares no vocabulary with any skill. The runner must report "shares no vocabulary", not crash on a zero vector or divide by zero. Pinned in Task 7, test zero-score prompt is an error, not a crash.
  4. A CHANGELOG with no release yet, only Unreleased. check-version must compare against the manifests' version and fail with a clear message, not throw. Pinned in Task 6, test no release heading is a clear failure.
  5. fetch-upstream re-run on a tree already at the pin, or with a missing commit. A re-run must not re-clone; a missing commit exits 3 and leaves no half-filled directory. Pinned in Task 2, tests second run is a no-op and missing commit exits 3 and removes the partial clone.

Port-task convention (used from M1 on; stated here so all three plans share it)

A prose file ported from an upstream source names the source file and line range under .upstream/<source>/, lists the required headings in order, states the adaptation rules (no source tags, no personal names or machine paths, kern vocabulary, unslop rules, no long dashes or curly quotes), gives the full text of anything that is a contract (frontmatter, ## Steps, kernel text, mode rules, injected lines, agent return formats), and ends with a check command: lint, grep for each required heading, and a size bound with wc -c.

  • Step 5: Write README.md
# kern

A personal Claude Code plugin that routes work through a small always-on kernel, enforces safety with guard hooks, and defers to each project's own rules and skills.

## Install

1. `gh auth login`
2. In Claude Code: `/plugin marketplace add <owner>/kern`, then `/plugin install kern@kern` at user scope.
3. Optional: restore `~/.claude/kern.yml`. Per project, run `/kern:help setup` when the defaults are not enough.

## Develop

Clone to the path named by `kern_repo` in `~/.claude/kern.yml` and run `claude --plugin-dir <path>`. Run `scripts/ci-local` before pushing.

## Documents

- Design: `docs/specs/2026-10-12-kern-design.md`
- Skill specs for M1 and M2: `docs/specs/2026-10-12-kern-m1-m2-skills.md`
- Plans: `docs/plans/`
  • Step 6: Verify and commit

Run: git -C "$KR" status --porcelain | sort Expected: ?? .gitignore, ?? README.md, ?? docs/.

git -C "$KR" commit -m "M0: start the kern repo with its spec, plans and research notes" -m "The spec and plans lived in a temporary scratchpad; the repo is now their durable home."

Task 2: fetch-upstream

Files:

  • Create: scripts/fetch-upstream
  • Test: tests/scripts/fetch-upstream.bats

Interfaces:

  • Consumes: nothing from earlier tasks.
  • Produces: bash scripts/fetch-upstream [key...] fills .upstream/<key>/ for keys superpowers pstack pocock addy caveman local (all when none given). Env overrides for tests: KERN_UPSTREAM_DIR (destination), KERN_UPSTREAM_<KEY>_URL, KERN_UPSTREAM_<KEY>_SHA, KERN_UPSTREAM_<KEY>_SRC (copy source), with <KEY> upper case. Exit 0 all fetched; 3 a pinned commit or copy source is missing.
  • Step 1: Write the failing test
#!/usr/bin/env bats
# tests/scripts/fetch-upstream.bats

setup() {
  ROOT="$(cd "$BATS_TEST_DIRNAME/../.." && pwd)"
  export KERN_UPSTREAM_DIR="$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/up"
  REMOTE="$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/remote"
  git init -q -b main "$REMOTE"
  git -C "$REMOTE" -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -q --allow-empty -m one
  SHA1=$(git -C "$REMOTE" rev-parse HEAD)
  git -C "$REMOTE" -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -q --allow-empty -m two
  export KERN_UPSTREAM_ADDY_URL="$REMOTE" KERN_UPSTREAM_ADDY_SHA="$SHA1"
}

@test "clones at the pinned commit" {
  run bash "$ROOT/scripts/fetch-upstream" addy
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]
  [ "$(git -C "$KERN_UPSTREAM_DIR/addy" rev-parse HEAD)" = "$SHA1" ]
}

@test "second run is a no-op" {
  bash "$ROOT/scripts/fetch-upstream" addy
  run bash "$ROOT/scripts/fetch-upstream" addy
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]
  [[ "$output" == *"addy: already at"* ]] || false
}

@test "missing commit exits 3 and removes the partial clone" {
  export KERN_UPSTREAM_ADDY_SHA=0000000000000000000000000000000000000000
  run bash "$ROOT/scripts/fetch-upstream" addy
  [ "$status" -eq 3 ]
  [ ! -e "$KERN_UPSTREAM_DIR/addy" ]
}

@test "copy source is copied" {
  mkdir -p "$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/cave"; echo hi > "$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/cave/NOTICE"
  export KERN_UPSTREAM_CAVEMAN_SRC="$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/cave"
  run bash "$ROOT/scripts/fetch-upstream" caveman
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]
  [ "$(cat "$KERN_UPSTREAM_DIR/caveman/NOTICE")" = hi ]
}

@test "missing copy source exits 3" {
  export KERN_UPSTREAM_CAVEMAN_SRC="$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/nope"
  run bash "$ROOT/scripts/fetch-upstream" caveman
  [ "$status" -eq 3 ]
}

@test "a failing git source is not swallowed" {
  export KERN_UPSTREAM_ADDY_URL="$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/no-such-remote"
  run bash "$ROOT/scripts/fetch-upstream" addy
  [ "$status" -eq 3 ]
}

@test "unknown key exits 2" {
  run bash "$ROOT/scripts/fetch-upstream" nonsense
  [ "$status" -eq 2 ]
}
  • Step 2: Run it to verify it fails

Run: bats tests/scripts/fetch-upstream.bats Expected: every test fails with No such file or directory for scripts/fetch-upstream.

  • Step 3: Write scripts/fetch-upstream
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Fills .upstream/ with the pinned source trees that kern files are ported from. Dev-only; hooks never run it.
set -euo pipefail

root=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)
dest="${KERN_UPSTREAM_DIR:-$root/.upstream}"
mkdir -p "$dest"

pin() { # key -> "url sha" for git sources
  case "$1" in
    superpowers) echo "https://github.com/obra/superpowers.git 5bf4e78011075bcfc0dc295f0724994cd123ee71" ;;
    pstack) echo "https://github.com/cursor/plugins.git 11713c6" ;;
    pocock) echo "https://github.com/mattpocock/skills.git 49dd158" ;;
    addy) echo "https://github.com/addyosmani/agent-skills.git 1be8e34" ;;
    *) return 1 ;;
  esac
}

override() { # key suffix default
  local var
  var="KERN_UPSTREAM_$(printf '%s' "$1" | tr '[:lower:]' '[:upper:]')_$2"
  printf '%s' "${!var:-$3}"
}

clone_at() { # key url sha
  local key=$1 url=$2 sha=$3 dir="$dest/$1"
  if [ -d "$dir/.git" ]; then
    local want
    want=$(git -C "$dir" rev-parse --verify --quiet "$sha^{commit}" || true)
    if [ -n "$want" ] && [ "$(git -C "$dir" rev-parse HEAD)" = "$want" ]; then
      echo "$key: already at $sha"
      return 0
    fi
  fi
  rm -rf "$dir"
  if ! git clone --quiet "$url" "$dir"; then
    rm -rf "$dir"
    echo "$key: clone failed: $url" >&2
    return 3
  fi
  if ! git -C "$dir" checkout --quiet --detach "$sha" 2>/dev/null; then
    rm -rf "$dir"
    echo "$key: commit $sha not found in $url" >&2
    return 3
  fi
  echo "$key: $url @ $sha"
}

copy_from() { # key src...
  local key=$1 dir="$dest/$1"
  shift
  rm -rf "$dir"
  mkdir -p "$dir"
  local src
  for src in "$@"; do
    if [ ! -e "$src" ]; then
      rm -rf "$dir"
      echo "$key: source missing: $src" >&2
      return 3
    fi
    cp -R "$src" "$dir"/
  done
  echo "$key: copied"
}

fetch() {
  local key=$1 url sha
  case "$key" in
    superpowers|pstack|pocock|addy)
      read -r url sha <<<"$(pin "$key")"
      url=$(override "$key" URL "$url")
      sha=$(override "$key" SHA "$sha")
      local rc=0
      clone_at "$key" "$url" "$sha" || rc=$?
      if [ "$rc" -ne 0 ] && [ "$key" = superpowers ] && [ -z "${KERN_UPSTREAM_SUPERPOWERS_URL:-}" ]; then
        echo "superpowers: falling back to the installed cache" >&2
        copy_from superpowers "$HOME/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/superpowers/6.4.1/."
        return
      fi
      return "$rc"
      ;;
    caveman)
      local src
      src=$(override caveman SRC "$HOME/.claude/plugins/cache/caveman/caveman/3.2.0")
      copy_from caveman "$src/."
      ;;
    local)
      local src
      src=$(override local SRC "")
      if [ -n "$src" ]; then
        copy_from local "$src/."
      else
        copy_from local "$HOME/.claude/skills/recall" "$HOME/.claude/skills/unslop" "$HOME/.claude/skills/no-comments" "$HOME/.claude/agents/comment-sicko.md"
      fi
      ;;
    *)
      echo "unknown source: $key (use superpowers pstack pocock addy caveman local)" >&2
      exit 2
      ;;
  esac
}

keys=("$@")
[ ${#keys[@]} -gt 0 ] || keys=(superpowers pstack pocock addy caveman local)
status=0
for k in "${keys[@]}"; do
  fetch "$k" || status=$?
done
exit "$status"

Note: copy_from superpowers "$cache/." copies the directory's contents; cp -R "$src" "$dir"/ with a trailing /. copies contents, and without it copies the named directory into $dir, which is what the local key wants (.upstream/local/recall/...).

  • Step 1: Write the failing test
// tests/licensing.test.mjs
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { readFileSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';

const read = (p) => readFileSync(new URL(`../${p}`, import.meta.url), 'utf8');

function upstreamRows() {
  return read('UPSTREAM.md').split('\n')
    .filter((l) => /^\| [a-z]+ \|/.test(l))
    .map((l) => l.split('|').slice(1, -1).map((c) => c.trim()));
}

test('kern itself is MIT', () => {
  assert.match(read('LICENSE'), /^MIT License\n\nCopyright \(c\) 2026 Mustafa Mohamed/);
});

test('every upstream source has a licence file and a NOTICE entry', () => {
  const rows = upstreamRows();
  assert.deepEqual(rows.map((r) => r[0]).sort(), ['addy', 'caveman', 'pocock', 'pstack', 'superpowers']);
  const notice = read('NOTICE');
  for (const [key, , , , copyright, , url] of rows) {
    assert.ok(existsSync(new URL(`../licenses/${key}.LICENSE`, import.meta.url)), `licenses/${key}.LICENSE`);
    assert.ok(notice.includes(url), `NOTICE names ${url}`);
    assert.ok(notice.includes(copyright), `NOTICE carries "${copyright}"`);
  }
});

test('NOTICE reproduces caveman NOTICE verbatim', () => {
  assert.ok(read('NOTICE').includes(read('licenses/caveman.NOTICE').trim()));
});

test('caveman licence is Apache-2.0', () => {
  assert.match(read('licenses/caveman.LICENSE'), /Apache License\s+Version 2\.0, January 2004/);
});

test('NOTICE has a file map section', () => {
  assert.match(read('NOTICE'), /^## File map$/m);
});

  • Step 5: Write UPSTREAM.md
# Upstream sources

kern ports text and ideas from these sources. `scripts/fetch-upstream` clones each at its pin into `.upstream/<key>/`. Review upstream changes quarterly: `git -C .upstream/<key> fetch && git -C .upstream/<key> log --oneline <commit>..origin/HEAD`.

| Key | Source | Version | Licence | Copyright | Commit | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Task 5: Skill lint (rules 1, 2, 3, 4, 8, 10, 11)

Files:

  • Create: scripts/lib/frontmatter.mjs, scripts/lib/lint-rules.mjs, scripts/lint-skills.mjs, scripts/lint-exemptions.json
  • Test: tests/lint-rules.test.mjs

Interfaces:

  • Consumes: skills/*/SKILL.md, agents/*.md (none yet).
  • Produces (M1 and M2 add rules through these):
    • parseFrontmatter(src: string) -> { data: Record<string,string>, body: string, error: string|null }
    • loadModel(root: string) -> { root, skills: Skill[], agents: Agent[], exemptions: Exemption[], shipped: string[], kernelText: string, cases: Record<string, object> } where Skill = { name, dir, file, tier: 'auto'|'path'|'slash', data, body, error } (data is the parsed frontmatter; tier is auto without disable-model-invocation: true, path when kernel/kernel.md has a row reading Read {KERN}/<name>/SKILL.md, else slash), Agent = { name, base, file, data, body, error }, Exemption = { rule: number, path: string, reason: string }, shipped is every file under the shipped dirs (absolute paths), kernelText is kernel/kernel.md or '', and cases maps an evals/cases/*.json basename (no extension) to its parsed JSON or { parseError }.
    • RULES: Array<{ id: number, check(model) -> Finding[] }> where Finding = { rule: number, path: string, level: 'error'|'warn', message: string }
    • runLint(model) -> Finding[] applies every rule, then drops findings matched by an exemption (same rule and path).
    • CLI node scripts/lint-skills.mjs [root] prints <level> <path>: rule <n>: <message> per finding and exits 1 when any error remains.
    • Adding a rule later: append { id, check } to RULES in lint-rules.mjs and add tests to tests/lint-rules.test.mjs.
  • Step 1: Write the failing tests
// tests/lint-rules.test.mjs
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { parseFrontmatter } from '../scripts/lib/frontmatter.mjs';
import { loadModel, runLint } from '../scripts/lib/lint-rules.mjs';

function repo(files) {
  const root = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'kern-lint-'));
  for (const [p, content] of Object.entries(files)) {
    mkdirSync(join(root, p, '..'), { recursive: true });
    writeFileSync(join(root, p), content);
  }
  return root;
}
const skill = (name, fm, body = '\n# x\n\n## Steps\n\n1. a\n') => `---\nname: ${name}\n${fm}\n---\n${body}`;
const lint = (files) => runLint(loadModel(repo(files)));
const rules = (findings) => findings.filter((f) => f.level === 'error').map((f) => f.rule);

test('parses CRLF frontmatter', () => {
  const r = parseFrontmatter('---\r\nname: a\r\ndescription: Use when x.\r\n---\r\nbody');
  assert.equal(r.error, null);
  assert.equal(r.data.name, 'a');
  assert.equal(r.body.trim(), 'body');
});

test('strips quotes and keeps colons in values', () => {
  const r = parseFrontmatter('---\nname: a\ndescription: "Use when: x happens"\n---\n');
  assert.equal(r.data.description, 'Use when: x happens');
});

test('block scalars are an error', () => {
  const r = parseFrontmatter('---\nname: a\ndescription: >\n  folded\n---\n');
  assert.match(r.error, /single-line/);
});

test('tiers: auto, path when a kernel row routes to it, else slash', () => {
  const manual = 'description: d\ndisable-model-invocation: true';
  const root = repo({
    'skills/a/SKILL.md': skill('a', 'description: Use when x.'),
    'skills/p/SKILL.md': skill('p', manual),
    'skills/s/SKILL.md': skill('s', manual),
    'kernel/kernel.md': '| New behaviour | Read {KERN}/p/SKILL.md |\n',
  });
  const tiers = Object.fromEntries(loadModel(root).skills.map((x) => [x.name, x.tier]));
  assert.deepEqual(tiers, { a: 'auto', p: 'path', s: 'slash' });
});

test('a clean auto skill passes', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(lint({ 'skills/a/SKILL.md': skill('a', 'description: Use when x happens.') }), []);
});

test('rule 1: missing frontmatter, name mismatch, non-kebab dir', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'skills/a/SKILL.md': '# no frontmatter' })), [1]);
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'skills/a/SKILL.md': skill('b', 'description: Use when x.') })), [1]);
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'skills/Bad_Dir/SKILL.md': skill('Bad_Dir', 'description: Use when x.') })), [1]);
});

test('rule 2: unknown skill key and ignored plugin-agent keys', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'skills/a/SKILL.md': skill('a', 'description: Use when x.\ncolour: red') })), [2]);
  const agent = '---\nname: r\ndescription: d\npermissionMode: plan\n---\nbody\n';
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'agents/r.md': agent })), [2]);
});

test('rule 3: auto limit and trigger, manual limit, agent limit, totals', () => {
  const long = 'Use when ' + 'x'.repeat(300);
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'skills/a/SKILL.md': skill('a', `description: ${long}`) })), [3]);
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'skills/a/SKILL.md': skill('a', 'description: Does things.') })), [3]);
  const manual = `description: ${'y'.repeat(121)}\ndisable-model-invocation: true`;
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'skills/a/SKILL.md': skill('a', manual) })), [3]);
  const agent = `---\nname: r\ndescription: ${'z'.repeat(151)}\n---\nbody\n`;
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'agents/r.md': agent })), [3]);
  const files = {};
  for (let i = 0; i < 6; i++) files[`skills/s${i}/SKILL.md`] = skill(`s${i}`, `description: Use when ${'w'.repeat(280)}`);
  assert.ok(rules(lint(files)).includes(3), 'auto total over 1,500');
});

test('rule 4: referenced files must exist', () => {
  const body = '\n## Steps\n\n1. Read `references/missing.md` and `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/kernel/none.md`.\n';
  const f = lint({ 'skills/a/SKILL.md': skill('a', 'description: Use when x.', body) });
  assert.deepEqual(rules(f), [4, 4]);
});

test('rule 8: machine paths and listed names in shipped files', () => {
  process.env.KERN_LINT_NAMES = 'Zebediah';
  const body = '\n## Steps\n\n1. Open /Users/someone/x and ask Zebediah.\n';
  const f = lint({ 'skills/a/SKILL.md': skill('a', 'description: Use when x.', body) });
  delete process.env.KERN_LINT_NAMES;
  assert.deepEqual(rules(f), [8, 8]);
});

test('rule 10: exemption needs a reason, and suppresses its finding', () => {
  const bad = { 'skills/a/SKILL.md': skill('a', 'description: Does things.'), 'scripts/lint-exemptions.json': '[{"rule":3,"path":"skills/a/SKILL.md","reason":""}]' };
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint(bad)), [3, 10], 'an exemption without a reason suppresses nothing');
  const ok = { ...bad, 'scripts/lint-exemptions.json': '[{"rule":3,"path":"skills/a/SKILL.md","reason":"fixture"}]' };
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint(ok)), []);
});

test('rule 11: long dashes and curly quotes', () => {
  const body = '\n## Steps\n\n1. Do this — then “that”.\n';
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'skills/a/SKILL.md': skill('a', 'description: Use when x.', body) })), [11, 11]);
});
  • Step 2: Run them to verify they fail

Run: node --test tests/lint-rules.test.mjs Expected: FAIL with Cannot find module '.../scripts/lib/frontmatter.mjs'.

  • Step 3: Write scripts/lib/frontmatter.mjs
// Minimal frontmatter reader for kern files: single-line `key: value` pairs only.
export function parseFrontmatter(src) {
  const text = src.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n');
  const m = text.match(/^---\n([\s\S]*?)\n---[ \t]*(?:\n|$)/);
  if (!m) return { data: {}, body: text, error: 'no frontmatter block' };
  const data = {};
  for (const line of m[1].split('\n')) {
    if (!line.trim() || line.trimStart().startsWith('#')) continue;
    const kv = line.match(/^([A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]*):[ \t]*(.*)$/);
    if (!kv) return { data, body: text.slice(m[0].length), error: `unparsed frontmatter line (use single-line values): ${line}` };
    let value = kv[2].trim();
    if (value === '>' || value === '|' || value === '>-' || value === '|-') {
      return { data, body: text.slice(m[0].length), error: `key ${kv[1]} uses a block scalar; use single-line values` };
    }
    if ((value.startsWith('"') && value.endsWith('"')) || (value.startsWith("'") && value.endsWith("'"))) value = value.slice(1, -1);
    data[kv[1]] = value;
  }
  return { data, body: text.slice(m[0].length), error: null };
}
  • Step 4: Write scripts/lib/lint-rules.mjs
// kern lint rules (design spec 11.2). Pure checks over a loaded model; the CLI is scripts/lint-skills.mjs.
import { readFileSync, readdirSync, existsSync, statSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, relative } from 'node:path';
import { parseFrontmatter } from './frontmatter.mjs';

export const SKILL_KEYS = new Set(['name', 'description', 'when_to_use', 'argument-hint', 'arguments', 'disable-model-invocation', 'user-invocable', 'allowed-tools', 'disallowed-tools', 'model', 'effort', 'context', 'agent', 'background', 'hooks', 'paths', 'shell', 'metadata', 'license', 'compatibility']);
export const AGENT_KEYS = new Set(['name', 'description', 'tools', 'disallowedTools', 'model', 'effort', 'color', 'maxTurns', 'skills', 'memory', 'background', 'isolation']);
export const AGENT_KEYS_IGNORED_IN_PLUGINS = new Set(['permissionMode', 'hooks', 'mcpServers', 'initialPrompt']);
export const SHIPPED_DIRS = ['skills', 'agents', 'kernel', 'modes', 'hooks'];
export const LIMITS = { auto: 300, manual: 120, agent: 150, autoTotal: 1500, autoPlusAgents: 2500 };
const KEBAB = /^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$/;
const TRIGGER = /\buse (this )?when\b|\buse (before|after|during)\b/i;
const MACHINE_PATH = /(\/Users\/[A-Za-z]|\/home\/[a-z]|[A-Z]:\\Users\\|~\/Code\/)/;
const LONG_DASH = /[–—]/g;
const CURLY = /[‘’“”]/g;

export const rel = (model, p) => relative(model.root, p);

function walk(dir) {
  if (!existsSync(dir)) return [];
  const out = [];
  for (const name of readdirSync(dir)) {
    const p = join(dir, name);
    if (statSync(p).isDirectory()) out.push(...walk(p));
    else out.push(p);
  }
  return out;
}

export function loadModel(root) {
  const skills = [];
  const skillsDir = join(root, 'skills');
  if (existsSync(skillsDir)) {
    for (const dir of readdirSync(skillsDir)) {
      const file = join(skillsDir, dir, 'SKILL.md');
      if (!existsSync(file)) continue;
      const fm = parseFrontmatter(readFileSync(file, 'utf8'));
      skills.push({ name: fm.data.name, dir, file, data: fm.data, body: fm.body, error: fm.error });
    }
  }
  const kernelPath = join(root, 'kernel', 'kernel.md');
  const kernelText = existsSync(kernelPath) ? readFileSync(kernelPath, 'utf8') : '';
  const routed = new Set([...kernelText.matchAll(/Read \{KERN\}\/([a-z0-9-]+)\/SKILL\.md/g)].map((m) => m[1]));
  for (const s of skills) s.tier = s.data['disable-model-invocation'] !== 'true' ? 'auto' : routed.has(s.name) ? 'path' : 'slash';
  const cases = {};
  const casesDir = join(root, 'evals', 'cases');
  if (existsSync(casesDir)) {
    for (const f of readdirSync(casesDir).filter((n) => n.endsWith('.json'))) {
      try { cases[f.replace(/\.json$/, '')] = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(casesDir, f), 'utf8')); }
      catch (e) { cases[f.replace(/\.json$/, '')] = { parseError: e.message }; }
    }
  }
  const agents = [];
  const agentsDir = join(root, 'agents');
  if (existsSync(agentsDir)) {
    for (const f of readdirSync(agentsDir).filter((n) => n.endsWith('.md'))) {
      const file = join(agentsDir, f);
      const fm = parseFrontmatter(readFileSync(file, 'utf8'));
      agents.push({ name: fm.data.name, base: f.replace(/\.md$/, ''), file, data: fm.data, body: fm.body, error: fm.error });
    }
  }
  const exFile = join(root, 'scripts', 'lint-exemptions.json');
  const exemptions = existsSync(exFile) ? JSON.parse(readFileSync(exFile, 'utf8')) : [];
  const shipped = SHIPPED_DIRS.flatMap((d) => walk(join(root, d)));
  return { root, skills, agents, exemptions, shipped, kernelText, cases };
}

export const err = (rule, path, message) => ({ rule, path, level: 'error', message });
export const warn = (rule, path, message) => ({ rule, path, level: 'warn', message });

export const RULES = [
  { id: 1, check(model) {
    const out = [];
    for (const s of model.skills) {
      const p = rel(model, s.file);
      if (s.error) { out.push(err(1, p, s.error)); continue; }
      if (!KEBAB.test(s.dir)) out.push(err(1, p, `directory ${s.dir} is not kebab case`));
      else if (s.name !== s.dir) out.push(err(1, p, `name ${s.name} does not equal directory ${s.dir}`));
    }
    for (const a of model.agents) {
      const p = rel(model, a.file);
      if (a.error) { out.push(err(1, p, a.error)); continue; }
      if (a.name !== a.base) out.push(err(1, p, `name ${a.name} does not equal file name ${a.base}`));
    }
    return out;
  } },
  { id: 2, check(model) {
    const out = [];
    for (const s of model.skills) {
      for (const k of Object.keys(s.data)) if (!SKILL_KEYS.has(k)) out.push(err(2, rel(model, s.file), `unknown frontmatter key ${k}`));
    }
    for (const a of model.agents) {
      for (const k of Object.keys(a.data)) {
        if (AGENT_KEYS_IGNORED_IN_PLUGINS.has(k)) out.push(err(2, rel(model, a.file), `${k} is ignored for plugin agents; use tools, disallowedTools and kern guards`));
        else if (!AGENT_KEYS.has(k)) out.push(err(2, rel(model, a.file), `unknown agent key ${k}`));
      }
    }
    return out;
  } },
  { id: 3, check(model) {
    const out = [];
    let autoTotal = 0;
    let agentTotal = 0;
    for (const s of model.skills) {
      if (s.error) continue;
      const d = s.data.description || '';
      const p = rel(model, s.file);
      if (!d) { out.push(err(3, p, 'description missing')); continue; }
      if (s.tier === 'auto') {
        autoTotal += d.length;
        if (d.length > LIMITS.auto) out.push(err(3, p, `auto description ${d.length} chars, limit ${LIMITS.auto}`));
        if (!TRIGGER.test(d)) out.push(err(3, p, 'auto description needs a trigger such as "Use when" or "Use before"'));
      } else if (d.length > LIMITS.manual) out.push(err(3, p, `description ${d.length} chars, limit ${LIMITS.manual}`));
    }
    for (const a of model.agents) {
      const d = a.data.description || '';
      agentTotal += d.length;
      if (d.length > LIMITS.agent) out.push(err(3, rel(model, a.file), `agent description ${d.length} chars, limit ${LIMITS.agent}`));
    }
    if (autoTotal > LIMITS.autoTotal) out.push(err(3, 'skills', `auto descriptions total ${autoTotal} chars, limit ${LIMITS.autoTotal}`));
    if (autoTotal + agentTotal > LIMITS.autoPlusAgents) out.push(err(3, 'skills', `auto plus agent descriptions total ${autoTotal + agentTotal} chars, limit ${LIMITS.autoPlusAgents}`));
    return out;
  } },
  { id: 4, check(model) {
    const out = [];
    for (const s of model.skills) {
      const p = rel(model, s.file);
      const local = [...s.body.matchAll(/`((?:references|templates|scripts|prompts)\/[\w./-]+)`|\]\(((?:references|templates|scripts|prompts)\/[\w./-]+)\)/g)].map((m) => m[1] || m[2]);
      for (const r of local) if (!existsSync(join(model.root, 'skills', s.dir, r))) out.push(err(4, p, `referenced file missing: ${r}`));
      const rooted = [...s.body.matchAll(/\$\{CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT\}\/([\w./-]+)/g)].map((m) => m[1].replace(/[.,;:]+$/, ''));
      for (const r of rooted) if (!existsSync(join(model.root, r))) out.push(err(4, p, `referenced file missing: \${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/${r}`));
    }
    return out;
  } },
  { id: 8, check(model) {
    const out = [];
    const names = (process.env.KERN_LINT_NAMES || '').split(',').map((n) => n.trim()).filter(Boolean);
    const namesFile = join(model.root, '.lint-names');
    if (existsSync(namesFile)) names.push(...readFileSync(namesFile, 'utf8').split('\n').map((n) => n.trim()).filter(Boolean));
    const nameRes = names.map((n) => new RegExp(`\\b${n.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&')}\\b`, 'i'));
    for (const f of model.shipped) {
      const text = readFileSync(f, 'utf8');
      const p = rel(model, f);
      if (MACHINE_PATH.test(text)) out.push(err(8, p, 'machine-specific path'));
      for (const re of nameRes) if (re.test(text)) out.push(err(8, p, `personal name matches ${re.source}`));
    }
    return out;
  } },
  { id: 10, check(model) {
    return model.exemptions.filter((e) => !e.reason || !String(e.reason).trim()).map((e) => err(10, 'scripts/lint-exemptions.json', `exemption for rule ${e.rule} on ${e.path} has no reason`));
  } },
  { id: 11, check(model) {
    const out = [];
    const files = [...model.shipped, join(model.root, 'README.md'), join(model.root, 'CHANGELOG.md')].filter((f) => existsSync(f) && /\.(md|sh|json|yml)$|\/hooks\/[a-z-]+$/.test(f));
    for (const f of files) {
      const text = readFileSync(f, 'utf8');
      const p = rel(model, f);
      if ((text.match(LONG_DASH) || []).length) out.push(err(11, p, 'long dash; use a period or a comma'));
      if ((text.match(CURLY) || []).length) out.push(err(11, p, 'curly quotes; use straight quotes'));
    }
    return out;
  } },
];

export function runLint(model) {
  const findings = RULES.flatMap((r) => r.check(model));
  const valid = model.exemptions.filter((e) => e.reason && String(e.reason).trim());
  return findings.filter((f) => f.rule === 10 || !valid.some((e) => e.rule === f.rule && e.path === f.path));
}
  • Step 5: Write scripts/lint-skills.mjs and scripts/lint-exemptions.json
#!/usr/bin/env node
// CLI over scripts/lib/lint-rules.mjs. Exit 1 when any error remains.
import { resolve } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { loadModel, runLint } from './lib/lint-rules.mjs';

const root = resolve(process.argv[2] || fileURLToPath(new URL('..', import.meta.url)));
const findings = runLint(loadModel(root));
for (const f of findings) console.log(`${f.level} ${f.path}: rule ${f.rule}: ${f.message}`);
const errors = findings.filter((f) => f.level === 'error').length;
console.log(errors ? `lint: ${errors} error(s)` : 'lint: clean');
process.exit(errors ? 1 : 0);

Task 6: Version agreement and releases (rule 14)

Files:

  • Create: scripts/check-version.mjs, scripts/release.mjs
  • Test: tests/versions.test.mjs

Interfaces:

  • Consumes: .claude-plugin/plugin.json, .claude-plugin/marketplace.json, CHANGELOG.md.
  • Produces:
    • checkVersions(root) -> string[] (problems; empty means agreement).
    • checkUnreleased(root, changedFiles: string[]) -> string[] (problems when shipped paths changed and ## [Unreleased] has no bullet).
    • bump(version, part: 'major'|'minor'|'patch') -> string.
    • release(root, part, date) -> string (new version; rewrites both manifests and CHANGELOG; never commits or tags).
    • CLIs: node scripts/check-version.mjs [--pr <base-ref>]; node scripts/release.mjs <major|minor|patch> prints git tag v<x.y.z>.

// tests/versions.test.mjs
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { checkVersions, checkUnreleased } from '../scripts/check-version.mjs';
import { bump, release } from '../scripts/release.mjs';

function repo({ plugin = '0.1.0', market = '0.1.0', changelog }) {
  const root = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'kern-ver-'));
  mkdirSync(join(root, '.claude-plugin'));
  writeFileSync(join(root, '.claude-plugin/plugin.json'), JSON.stringify({ name: 'kern', version: plugin }, null, 2) + '\n');
  writeFileSync(join(root, '.claude-plugin/marketplace.json'), JSON.stringify({ name: 'kern', plugins: [{ name: 'kern', version: market, source: './' }] }, null, 2) + '\n');
  writeFileSync(join(root, 'CHANGELOG.md'), changelog ?? '# Changelog\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n## [0.1.0] - 2026-10-12\n\n- First.\n');
  return root;
}

  assert.deepEqual(checkVersions(repo({})), []);
});

  assert.equal(checkVersions(repo({ market: '0.2.0' })).length, 1);
});

  assert.match(problems[0], /no release heading/);
});

test('shipped change needs an Unreleased bullet', () => {

  assert.deepEqual(checkUnreleased(root, ['README.md']), []);
  writeFileSync(join(root, 'CHANGELOG.md'), '# Changelog\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n- Add a.\n\n## [0.1.0] - 2026-10-12\n\n- First.\n');
  assert.deepEqual(checkUnreleased(root, ['skills/a/SKILL.md']), []);
});

test('bump follows semver', () => {
  assert.equal(bump('0.1.9', 'patch'), '0.1.10');
  assert.equal(bump('0.1.9', 'minor'), '0.2.0');
  assert.equal(bump('0.1.9', 'major'), '1.0.0');
});

test('release moves Unreleased bullets under the new version', () => {
  const root = repo({});

  • Step 3: Write scripts/check-version.mjs
#!/usr/bin/env node
// Lint rule 14: manifests and the newest CHANGELOG release agree; shipped changes add an Unreleased line.
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

const SHIPPED = /^(skills|agents|kernel|modes|hooks)\//;
const json = (root, p) => JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, p), 'utf8'));

export function unreleasedBullets(changelog) {
  const m = changelog.match(/^## \[Unreleased\]\n([\s\S]*?)(?=^## \[|(?![\s\S]))/m);
  return m ? m[1].split('\n').filter((l) => /^- /.test(l)) : [];
}

export function checkVersions(root) {
  const problems = [];
  const plugin = json(root, '.claude-plugin/plugin.json').version;
  const market = json(root, '.claude-plugin/marketplace.json').plugins?.[0]?.version;
  const changelog = readFileSync(join(root, 'CHANGELOG.md'), 'utf8');
  const release = changelog.match(/^## \[(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)\]/m)?.[1];
  if (plugin !== market) problems.push(`plugin.json ${plugin} != marketplace.json ${market}`);
  if (!release) problems.push(`CHANGELOG.md has no release heading; expected ## [${plugin}] - <date>`);
  else if (release !== plugin) problems.push(`newest CHANGELOG release ${release} != plugin.json ${plugin}`);
  return problems;
}

export function checkUnreleased(root, changedFiles) {
  if (!changedFiles.some((f) => SHIPPED.test(f))) return [];
  const changelog = readFileSync(join(root, 'CHANGELOG.md'), 'utf8');
  return unreleasedBullets(changelog).length ? [] : ['shipped files changed but ## [Unreleased] has no bullet'];
}

if (process.argv[1] && resolve(process.argv[1]) === fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) {
  const root = fileURLToPath(new URL('..', import.meta.url));
  const problems = checkVersions(root);
  const i = process.argv.indexOf('--pr');
  if (i > 0) {
    const base = process.argv[i + 1];
    const changed = execFileSync('git', ['-C', root, 'diff', '--name-only', `${base}...HEAD`], { encoding: 'utf8' }).split('\n').filter(Boolean);
    problems.push(...checkUnreleased(root, changed));
  }
  for (const p of problems) console.log(`error: ${p}`);
  console.log(problems.length ? 'versions: FAILED' : 'versions: agree');
  process.exit(problems.length ? 1 : 0);
}
  • Step 4: Write scripts/release.mjs
#!/usr/bin/env node
// Bumps both manifests and the CHANGELOG. Prints the tag command; never commits, tags or pushes.
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { unreleasedBullets } from './check-version.mjs';

export function bump(version, part) {
  const [ma, mi, pa] = version.split('.').map(Number);
  if (part === 'major') return `${ma + 1}.0.0`;
  if (part === 'minor') return `${ma}.${mi + 1}.0`;
  if (part === 'patch') return `${ma}.${mi}.${pa + 1}`;
  throw new Error(`unknown part ${part}; use major, minor or patch`);
}

export function release(root, part, date) {
  const pluginPath = join(root, '.claude-plugin/plugin.json');
  const marketPath = join(root, '.claude-plugin/marketplace.json');
  const logPath = join(root, 'CHANGELOG.md');
  const plugin = JSON.parse(readFileSync(pluginPath, 'utf8'));
  const market = JSON.parse(readFileSync(marketPath, 'utf8'));
  const log = readFileSync(logPath, 'utf8');
  const bullets = unreleasedBullets(log);
  if (!bullets.length) throw new Error('Unreleased is empty; add a line before releasing');
  const next = bump(plugin.version, part);
  plugin.version = next;
  market.plugins[0].version = next;
  const updated = log.replace(/^## \[Unreleased\]\n[\s\S]*?(?=^## \[)/m, `## [Unreleased]\n\n## [${next}] - ${date}\n\n${bullets.join('\n')}\n\n`);
  writeFileSync(pluginPath, JSON.stringify(plugin, null, 2) + '\n');
  writeFileSync(marketPath, JSON.stringify(market, null, 2) + '\n');
  writeFileSync(logPath, updated);
  return next;
}

if (process.argv[1] && resolve(process.argv[1]) === fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) {
  const root = fileURLToPath(new URL('..', import.meta.url));
  const next = release(root, process.argv[2], new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10));
  console.log(`released ${next}; review the diff, commit, then run: git tag v${next}`);
}

Task 7: Routing evals (tier 1)

Files:

  • Create: scripts/lib/rank.mjs (ported), scripts/route-eval.mjs, evals/cases/hello.json, evals/distractors.json, evals/baseline.json, evals/fixtures/hello/README.md

  • Modify: NOTICE (file map line)

Interfaces:

  • Consumes: loadModel(root) from Task 5 (auto skills are tier === 'auto').
  • Produces:
    • rank.mjs: tokenize(text) -> string[], buildCorpus(docs: {name, text}[]) -> Corpus, rank(prompt, corpus) -> {name, score}[] (descending), similarity(nameA, nameB, corpus) -> number.
    • route-eval.mjs: targets(root) -> {name, text, kind: 'skill'|'distractor'}[], owners first and distractors last (M2 adds kind: 'row' for kernel rows, placed before the distractors), evaluate(root, { minRank1 }) -> { errors: string[], warnings: string[], rank1Rate: number|null }; CLI node scripts/route-eval.mjs [--update-baseline] exits 1 on any error.
    • Case file shape (addy's): { "skill_name": "<name>", "trigger": { "positive": [{ "prompt", "top_k" }], "negative": [{ "prompt", "owner"? }] }, "evals": [{ "id", "kind": "execution"|"dialogue", "prompt", "expected_output", "expectations": [], "files"? }] }.
    • evals/baseline.json: { "min_rank1": <integer percent> }; the run fails below it; --update-baseline raises it to the current rate and never lowers it.
  • Step 1: Write the failing tests

    mkdirSync(join(root, p, '..'), { recursive: true });

  }
  return root;
}

});

  • Step 3: Port the ranking functions into scripts/lib/rank.mjs

Port STOP, stem, tokenize, termFreq (lines 62-102) and vec, cosine (lines 121-139) verbatim from .upstream/addy/scripts/run-evals.js, converted to ESM exports. Skip lines 104-119: addy's buildCorpus takes skills, and kern's version below takes {name, text} documents.

// Adapted from addyosmani/agent-skills scripts/run-evals.js (MIT): the tier 2 text pipeline.

// <lines 62-102 and 121-139 of .upstream/addy/scripts/run-evals.js go here unchanged except `function` becomes `export function` for tokenize, termFreq, vec, cosine>

export function buildCorpus(docs) {
  const map = new Map();
  for (const d of docs) {
    const nameTokens = tokenize(d.name.replace(/-/g, ' '));
    map.set(d.name, termFreq([...nameTokens, ...nameTokens, ...tokenize(d.text)]));
  }
  const df = new Map();
  for (const tf of map.values()) for (const term of tf.keys()) df.set(term, (df.get(term) || 0) + 1);
  const n = map.size;
  const idf = (term) => Math.log(1 + n / (1 + (df.get(term) || 0)));
  return { docs: map, idf };
}

export function rank(prompt, corpus) {
  const pv = vec(termFreq(tokenize(prompt)), corpus.idf);
  const scores = [];
  for (const [name, tf] of corpus.docs) scores.push({ name, score: cosine(pv, vec(tf, corpus.idf)) });
  scores.sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score);
  return scores;
}

export function similarity(a, b, corpus) {
  return cosine(vec(corpus.docs.get(a), corpus.idf), vec(corpus.docs.get(b), corpus.idf));
}

The angle-bracket comment is an executor instruction: replace it with the copied lines. Check: grep -c 'export function' scripts/lib/rank.mjs prints 7 (tokenize, termFreq, vec, cosine, buildCorpus, rank, similarity), and the tokenizer check [ 'conflict', 'branch' ] when given 'conflicts branching'; run it as node -e "import('./scripts/lib/rank.mjs').then(m => console.log(m.tokenize('conflicts branching')))".

  • Step 4: Write scripts/route-eval.mjs
#!/usr/bin/env node
// Tier 1 routing evals (design spec 11.1). Adapted from addyosmani/agent-skills scripts/run-evals.js (MIT), deterministic tier only.
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync, readdirSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { loadModel } from './lib/lint-rules.mjs';
import { buildCorpus, rank, similarity } from './lib/rank.mjs';

const MIN_POSITIVE = 3;
const MIN_NEGATIVE = 2;
const MIN_EVALS = 1;
const COLLISION_WARN = 0.5;
const COLLISION_ERROR = 0.75;

export function targets(root) {
  const skills = loadModel(root).skills.filter((s) => s.tier === 'auto' && s.data.description)
    .map((s) => ({ name: s.name, text: s.data.description, kind: 'skill' }));
  const dPath = join(root, 'evals', 'distractors.json');
  const distractors = existsSync(dPath) ? JSON.parse(readFileSync(dPath, 'utf8')).map((d) => ({ name: d.name, text: d.description, kind: 'distractor' })) : [];
  return [...skills, ...distractors];
}

function loadCases(root) {
  const dir = join(root, 'evals', 'cases');
  if (!existsSync(dir)) return [];
  return readdirSync(dir).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.json')).map((f) => {
    try { return { file: f, data: JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dir, f), 'utf8')) }; }
    catch (e) { return { file: f, parseError: e.message }; }
  });
}

export function evaluate(root, { minRank1 }) {
  const all = targets(root);
  const owners = all.filter((t) => t.kind !== 'distractor');
  const corpus = buildCorpus(all);
  const names = new Set(all.map((t) => t.name));
  const cases = loadCases(root);
  const errors = [];
  const warnings = [];
  let positives = 0;
  let rank1 = 0;

  for (const t of owners) if (!cases.some((c) => c.file === `${t.name}.json`)) errors.push(`${t.name}: no eval case file (evals/cases/${t.name}.json)`);

  for (const c of cases) {
    if (c.parseError) { errors.push(`${c.file}: invalid JSON: ${c.parseError}`); continue; }
    const d = c.data;
    const expected = c.file.replace(/\.json$/, '');
    if (d.skill_name !== expected) errors.push(`${c.file}: skill_name "${d.skill_name}" does not match the file name`);
    if (!owners.some((t) => t.name === expected)) { errors.push(`${c.file}: no such auto skill or kernel row`); continue; }
    for (const ev of d.evals || []) {
      const ok = Number.isInteger(ev.id) && typeof ev.prompt === 'string' && typeof ev.expected_output === 'string' && Array.isArray(ev.expectations) && ev.expectations.length > 0;
      if (!ok) errors.push(`${c.file}: eval id=${ev.id} does not match the evals schema`);
      const kind = ev.kind || 'execution';
      if (!['execution', 'dialogue'].includes(kind)) errors.push(`${c.file}: eval id=${ev.id} has unknown kind "${kind}"`);
      if (kind === 'execution' && !(Array.isArray(ev.files) && ev.files.length)) errors.push(`${c.file}: eval id=${ev.id} needs files[]`);
      for (const f of ev.files || []) if (!existsSync(join(root, 'evals', 'fixtures', f))) errors.push(`${c.file}: eval id=${ev.id} fixture not found: evals/fixtures/${f}`);
    }
    for (const p of d.trigger?.positive || []) {
      positives++;
      const topK = p.top_k || 3;
      const r = rank(p.prompt, corpus);
      const idx = r.findIndex((x) => x.name === expected);
      const hit = r[idx];
      if (idx === 0 && hit.score > 0) rank1++;
      if (!hit || hit.score === 0) errors.push(`${expected}: description shares no vocabulary with "${p.prompt}"`);
      else if (idx >= topK) errors.push(`${expected}: positive prompt ranked #${idx + 1} (need top ${topK}): "${p.prompt}"; top: ${r.slice(0, 3).map((x) => `${x.name} ${x.score.toFixed(2)}`).join(', ')}`);
    }
    for (const n of d.trigger?.negative || []) {
      const r = rank(n.prompt, corpus);
      if (r[0].name === expected && r[0].score > 0) errors.push(`${expected}: ranked #1 for negative prompt "${n.prompt}"`);
      if (n.owner) {
        if (!names.has(n.owner)) errors.push(`${c.file}: negative names unknown owner "${n.owner}"`);
        else {
          const oi = r.findIndex((x) => x.name === n.owner);
          const si = r.findIndex((x) => x.name === expected);
          if (r[oi].score === 0 || oi > si) errors.push(`${expected}: owner ${n.owner} does not outrank it for "${n.prompt}"`);
        }
      }
    }
    const pc = (d.trigger?.positive || []).length;
    const nc = (d.trigger?.negative || []).length;
    const ec = (d.evals || []).length;
    if (pc < MIN_POSITIVE || nc < MIN_NEGATIVE || ec < MIN_EVALS) errors.push(`${expected}: below required minimums (${pc}/${nc}/${ec}; need ${MIN_POSITIVE}/${MIN_NEGATIVE}/${MIN_EVALS})`);
  }

  // targets() puts owners first and distractors last, so all[i] === owners[i] for i < owners.length.
  for (let i = 0; i < owners.length; i++) {
    for (let j = i + 1; j < all.length; j++) {
      const a = owners[i].name;
      const b = all[j].name;
      const sim = similarity(a, b, corpus);
      if (sim >= COLLISION_ERROR) errors.push(`collision: ${a} and ${b} are ${(sim * 100).toFixed(0)}% similar`);
      else if (sim >= COLLISION_WARN) warnings.push(`overlap: ${a} and ${b} are ${(sim * 100).toFixed(0)}% similar`);
    }
  }

  const rank1Rate = positives ? Math.floor((rank1 / positives) * 100) : null;
  if (minRank1 !== null && rank1Rate !== null && rank1Rate < minRank1) errors.push(`rank-1 rate ${rank1Rate}% is below the baseline ${minRank1}%`);
  return { errors, warnings, rank1Rate };
}

if (process.argv[1] && resolve(process.argv[1]) === fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) {
  const root = fileURLToPath(new URL('..', import.meta.url));
  const basePath = join(root, 'evals', 'baseline.json');
  const baseline = JSON.parse(readFileSync(basePath, 'utf8'));
  const r = evaluate(root, { minRank1: baseline.min_rank1 });
  for (const w of r.warnings) console.log(`warn: ${w}`);
  for (const e of r.errors) console.log(`error: ${e}`);
  console.log(`rank-1 rate: ${r.rank1Rate ?? 'n/a'}% (baseline ${baseline.min_rank1}%)`);
  if (process.argv.includes('--update-baseline') && !r.errors.length && r.rank1Rate !== null && r.rank1Rate > baseline.min_rank1) {
    writeFileSync(basePath, JSON.stringify({ min_rank1: r.rank1Rate }, null, 2) + '\n');
    console.log(`baseline raised to ${r.rank1Rate}%`);
  }
  console.log(r.errors.length ? 'route-eval: FAILED' : 'route-eval: passed');
  process.exit(r.errors.length ? 1 : 0);
}
  • Step 5: Write the eval data

evals/cases/hello.json:

{

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Runs the CI job's steps locally, in the same order, before a push.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
node --test 'tests/**/*.test.mjs'
node scripts/lint-skills.mjs
node scripts/check-version.mjs
node scripts/route-eval.mjs

bats tests/scripts

name: ci
on:
  push:
  pull_request:
  workflow_dispatch:

jobs:
  checks:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: '22'
      - name: Unit tests
        run: node --test 'tests/**/*.test.mjs'
      - name: Lint skills
        run: node scripts/lint-skills.mjs
      - name: Versions agree
        run: node scripts/check-version.mjs
      - name: Unreleased line for shipped changes
        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'
        run: node scripts/check-version.mjs --pr origin/${{ github.base_ref }}
      - name: Routing evals
        run: node scripts/route-eval.mjs
      - name: Install Claude Code
        run: npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code@2.1.296
      - name: Validate plugin
        run: claude plugin validate . --strict
      - name: Install bats and shellcheck
        run: sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y bats shellcheck
      - name: Script tests
        run: bats tests/scripts
      - name: Shellcheck
        run: shellcheck scripts/fetch-upstream scripts/ci-local

kern M1 Implementation Plan

For agentic workers: REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (- [ ]) syntax for tracking.

Goal: Ship kern's kernel, mode switching, autopilot grant, settings layer, ten guards and the three M1 skills, all tested on macOS bash 3.2 and Linux bash 5.

Architecture: Four bash hooks (session-start, subagent-start, prompt-submit, pre-tool-use) share five sourced libraries under hooks/lib/. session-start renders kernel/kernel.md with the project settings summary; prompt-submit applies /kern:mode and /kern:autopilot and writes per-session state; pre-tool-use sources every hooks/guards/g*.sh, collects votes and prints the strictest. Skills are prose; scripts/kern-* give skills the same settings and repo facts the hooks use.

Tech Stack: bash 3.2+ and POSIX tools (awk, sed, grep, git), optional gh and gitleaks; bats-core for hook tests; Node 22 node:test for lint rules; shellcheck; Claude Code 2.1.296.

Spec: docs/specs/2026-10-12-kern-design.md (sections 4 to 8, 11.2, 14) and docs/specs/2026-10-12-kern-m1-m2-skills.md (sections A, B, E). Shared names: docs/plans/contract.md. M0 must be complete: this plan extends M0's scripts/lib/lint-rules.mjs, tests/lint-rules.test.mjs, scripts/ci-local and .github/workflows/ci.yml.

Every command runs from the repo root, ~/Code/projects/kern, unless a step says otherwise.

Global Constraints

  • Kernel delivery: SessionStart target 4,000 chars (lint warning), lint error at 9,000, Claude Code's cap 10,000 per string. SubagentStart at most 1,200 chars.
  • Kernel section budgets (design 4.2): header 100, precedence 420, routing 1,100, autonomy 650, evidence 330, context 125, reply 225, learning 160, paths 250, settings summary 600; total 3,960.
  • M1 routing rows: technical-writing and context only. Every other row ships with its route (M2, M3, M6).
  • Hooks use bash, POSIX tools and git only. gh, jq and gitleaks are used when present, never required. jq is never used, so behaviour does not change with it.
  • Hooks are bash 3.2 compatible (macOS /bin/bash): no associative arrays, no ${var,,}, no mapfile, no ;&. A function that votes must never run in a pipeline subshell; loops read from here-documents instead.
  • Plugin hooks use exec form: "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/<name>"].
  • PreToolUse decisions are hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecision (allow, ask, deny) with permissionDecisionReason. A hook that cannot parse its input returns ask, or deny under autopilot. It never allows on a parse failure. The dispatcher entry uses "onFailure": "block".
  • Policy keys: commit.policy, push.policy, pr.policy, tracker.policy, review.post_policy, each ask (default), auto or deny. Floor actions have no key and never loosen: merge, production deploy, data deletion, force-push to a shared branch, messaging people.
  • Autopilot is on only when no settings layer says autopilot: denied and the session state says autopilot: on, which only the hook writes from a command the user typed.
  • Session state: ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}/sessions/<session_id>.state, flat key: value, mode 0600, atomic write, symlinks refused. (Skills spec B1 wins over the design's .json.)
  • startup, clear and fork reset the mode to settings defaults with autopilot off; compact and resume keep it.
  • Settings and .kern/ resolve from the main worktree root, the first worktree line of git worktree list --porcelain, never from the current directory.
  • Description limits: path and slash skills at most 120 chars. A frontmatter value that contains : is quoted, so YAML parsers read it.
  • Shipped files (skills/, agents/, kernel/, modes/, hooks/): no machine paths, no personal names (lint rule 8), no long dashes or curly quotes (lint rule 11), and never the words caveman, ultracave or megacave as a name.
  • modes/terse.md is written for kern. Caveman 3.2.0 (Apache-2.0) is credited in NOTICE as the source of the idea; no caveman text is copied.
  • Commits: subject M1: <imperative summary>, 72 chars or fewer; body says why; no attribution trailers; git add -- <paths> only. Pushes happen only when the user says so.
  • Port tasks follow the port-task convention in the M0 plan: source file and lines under .upstream/<source>/, required headings, adaptation rules, full text of every contract part, and a check command.

Review Focus

  1. A git command hidden in a chain, a $( ), backticks or bash -c '...'. The user expects G1 to see cd x && git stash and bash -c 'git switch main' the same as a bare command. Pinned: Task 10 test a chained or substituted main-checkout command is still seen; Task 9 gitctx.bats tests the splitter.
  2. A session running inside a linked worktree. Settings, .kern/ and the main-checkout test must resolve from the main worktree root, or a worktree session sees no settings and every guard misjudges "main checkout". Pinned: Task 3 test a linked worktree reads the main checkout's untracked settings; Task 4 test kern-dir creates WORK/sub at the main root; Task 10 test the same commands in a linked worktree: no vote.
  3. A prompt Claude Code starts itself that contains /kern:autopilot on: a background subagent report, a cross-session message, a scheduled task, or a CronCreate Claude writes. None may switch autopilot on. Pinned: Task 8 test wrapped prompts never switch; Task 9 test a scheduled prompt or relayed message carrying /kern:autopilot is denied.
  4. A very large tool input, such as a 200 KB Write. The guard dispatcher parses every Write; a quadratic parser would stall each one. Pinned: Task 2 test a 200 KB value parses in under 5 seconds.
  5. gh missing, offline or slow during the PR duplicate check. The guard must not allow a PR it could not check, and must not hang the session. Pinned: Task 11 test gh failing means dedup unknown; Task 9 test with_timeout returns promptly when the command finishes.

Interfaces M2 relies on

  • Kernel route row (contract): | <when, observable> | Read {KERN}/<skill>/SKILL.md | in kernel/kernel.md under ## Routing; a kernel file route reads Read {ROOT}/kernel/<file>.md. Lint rule 6 resolves both and requires the target's first H2 to be ## Steps followed by a numbered list. M2 also replaces the routing sentence "Non-trivial work with no match: write a short plan as a todo list first." with "Several matches: define and diagnose before build. Non-trivial work with no match: plan."
  • Settings summary (settings_summary in hooks/lib/render.sh), at most 600 chars, lines in this order, each present only when non-empty: Main worktree: <path> (or Not a git repository: user settings only.), Settings files: <list>, Set: key=value; ..., Overrides (follow the project skill, say so in one line): <job> -> project skill <name>; ..., Routes: <name> -> <value>; ..., Ignored, floor actions have no key: <keys>, Unknown keys, ignored: <keys>. Overrides cover every directory under skills/ except mode, autopilot, help and hello, so each M2 skill gets defer detection with no hook change.
  • Mode line: kern mode: voice=<normal|terse> surface=<chat|auto|html> autopilot=<on|off>, printed by mode_line SID in hooks/lib/render.sh. session-start prints it in the kernel header; prompt-submit prints it on every /kern:mode or /kern:autopilot turn, outside the default preset, and under autopilot.
  • Votes (hooks/lib/decide.sh): vote allow|ask|deny G<n> "<reason>"; strictest wins (deny, ask, allow); the reason shown is the winning level's reasons joined by ; with the prefix kern . fail_closed "<reason>" votes ask as G0. Under autopilot a final ask becomes deny.
  • Adding a guard (M2 adds g18): create hooks/guards/g18-<name>.sh defining guard_g18(); the dispatcher sources every hooks/guards/g*.sh and calls guard_<id> where <id> is the file name up to the first -. Variables a guard reads: TOOL, TOOL_INPUT (raw JSON of tool_input), AGENT (agent_type, empty in the main session), CWD, SID, KERN_MAIN, AUTOPILOT (1 or 0). Helpers: ti_get KEY, git_words, git_sub, gh_sub, cmd_head, _shell_words, word_has, word_value, in_main_checkout, current_branch, branch_protected, base_branch_name, push_targets, with_timeout, settings_get, policy_for, list_has. Test it with run_guard "<payload>" g18 from tests/helpers/common.bash.
  • Test helpers (tests/helpers/common.bash): kern_root, make_repo DIR, kern_env DIR, payload EVENT FIELDS, bash_payload CMD, run_guard PAYLOAD [GUARDS], decision_of OUTPUT, set_autopilot on|off, fake_gh. Run bats through tests/run-bats.
  • Spike record: docs/spikes/m1-payloads.md holds the line agent_type in subagent PreToolUse: <value or absent>.

  • Produces: docs/spikes/m1-payloads.md with one line per finding in the form <question>: <answer>, including exactly agent_type in subagent PreToolUse: <value or absent> (M2 Task 7 reads it). Fixtures tests/fixtures/payloads/{expansion-mode,expansion-autopilot,submit-slash-mode,submit-task-notification,subagent-start,pretooluse-subagent,sessionstart-clear}.json, each one JSON object with session_id replaced by test-session and the throwaway repo path replaced by /tmp/kern-fixture.   • Step 1: Build the spike plugin and a throwaway repo
SPIKE=$(mktemp -d)/kern-spike
mkdir -p "$SPIKE/plugin/.claude-plugin" "$SPIKE/plugin/hooks" "$SPIKE/plugin/skills/mode" "$SPIKE/plugin/skills/autopilot" "$SPIKE/plugin/agents"
cat >"$SPIKE/plugin/.claude-plugin/plugin.json" <<'JSON'
{ "name": "kern", "version": "0.0.0", "description": "Payload spike for kern M1. Delete after use." }
JSON
cat >"$SPIKE/plugin/hooks/log.sh" <<'SH'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Spike logger: appends CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT and the hook's stdin to payloads.log, one line each.
in=$(cat)
printf '%s\t%s\n' "${CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT-unset}" "$(printf '%s' "$in" | tr '\n' ' ')" >>"$(dirname "$0")/payloads.log"
case "$in" in
  *SubagentStart*) printf '{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"SubagentStart","additionalContext":"SPIKE-MARKER-7731"}}\n' ;;
esac
exit 0
SH
cat >"$SPIKE/plugin/hooks/hooks.json" <<'JSON'
{ "hooks": {
  "SessionStart": [{ "matcher": "", "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/log.sh"] }] }],
  "UserPromptSubmit": [{ "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/log.sh"] }] }],
  "UserPromptExpansion": [{ "matcher": "", "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/log.sh"] }] }],
  "SubagentStart": [{ "matcher": "", "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/log.sh"] }] }],
  "PreToolUse": [{ "matcher": "", "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/log.sh"] }] }]
} }
JSON
for s in mode autopilot; do
  printf -- '---\nname: %s\ndescription: Spike skill %s.\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\nReply with one line: spike %s skill loaded, arguments: $ARGUMENTS\n' "$s" "$s" "$s" >"$SPIKE/plugin/skills/$s/SKILL.md"
done
printf -- '---\nname: investigator\ndescription: Spike agent that runs one read-only command.\ntools: Read, Bash\n---\n\nRun the command you are given and report its output. Also report any context line that starts with SPIKE-MARKER.\n' >"$SPIKE/plugin/agents/investigator.md"
chmod +x "$SPIKE/plugin/hooks/log.sh"
git init -q "$SPIKE/repo" && git -C "$SPIKE/repo" commit -q --allow-empty -m init
mkdir -p "$SPIKE/repo/.claude"
printf '{ "enabledPlugins": { "superpowers@claude-plugins-official": false, "caveman@caveman": false } }\n' >"$SPIKE/repo/.claude/settings.local.json"
echo "SPIKE=$SPIKE"

Expected: the last line prints the spike directory. Keep it for the next steps.

  • Step 5: Apply the decision each finding drives

The hooks in Tasks 6 to 9 already handle every documented shape; these branches only add what a finding requires. Record the branch taken under each line of the record as Decision: ....

  1. Expansion fires (yes): the primary switch path works; keep the UserPromptSubmit envelope path as the fallback. Expansion does not fire (no): the envelope or raw path is the only path; nothing to change, because Task 8 tests both.
  2. command_name is kern:mode, /kern:mode or mode: Task 8's prompt-submit accepts all three (mode only with command_source plugin and a prompt starting /mode or /kern:mode). Any other value: add it to the case "${NAME#/}" in line of hooks/prompt-submit in Task 8 and add a test with the recorded fixture.
  3. UserPromptSubmit prompt is the envelope (<command-message>...<command-name>/kern:mode</command-name>...) or the raw /kern:mode terse: covered by Task 8. Something else while Expansion also did not fire: stop and tell the user that autopilot cannot be switched on safely with this Claude Code version, and wait for a decision before Task 8.
  4. Background report wrapper tag is one of <task-notification, <cross-session-message, <scheduled-task, <teammate-message, <channel-message: covered. Another tag: add it to KERN_WRAPPER_TAGS in hooks/prompt-submit (Task 8) and add it to the test wrapped prompts never switch.
  5. SubagentStart context reached the subagent (yes): keep JSON additionalContext. No: record it in the M2 risk list; execute-plan briefs will carry the subagent kernel text instead (M2 Task 7 reads this line).
  6. agent_type in subagent PreToolUse is recorded verbatim for M2's G18.
  7. Headless CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT starts with sdk: the reminder skip in Task 8 works as written. Another value <v>: in Task 8 change case "${CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT-}" in sdk*) exit 0 ;; esac to case "${CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT-}" in sdk* | <v>) exit 0 ;; esac, and change the autopilot-line condition [ "${CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT#sdk}" = "${CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT-}" ] to case "${CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT-}" in sdk* | <v>) false ;; *) true ;; esac.
  8. /clear session id: recorded only. State resets on source: clear either way.

tests/helpers/common.bash:

# shellcheck shell=bash
# Shared bats helpers for kern hook and script tests.

# kern_root: absolute path of the kern repo (two levels above this file).
kern_root() {
  cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../.." && pwd -P
}

# make_repo DIR: a git repo on main with user config, one commit (README.md), and a bare origin
# at DIR.origin with main pushed and refs/remotes/origin/HEAD set.
make_repo() {
  local dir="$1"
  git init -q -b main "$dir"
  git -C "$dir" config user.name "Test User"
  git -C "$dir" config user.email "test@example.com"
  git -C "$dir" config commit.gpgsign false
  printf 'readme\n' >"$dir/README.md"
  git -C "$dir" add README.md
  git -C "$dir" commit -q -m "init"
  git init -q --bare -b main "$dir.origin"
  git -C "$dir" remote add origin "$dir.origin"
  git -C "$dir" push -q origin main
  git -C "$dir" remote set-head origin main >/dev/null
}

# kern_env DIR: isolates HOME and plugin data under the test tmpdir and points CWD at DIR.
kern_env() {
  export HOME="$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/home"
  export CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA="$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/data"
  export CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT
  CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT="$(kern_root)"
  mkdir -p "$HOME/.claude" "$CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA"
  export CWD="$1"
}

# payload EVENT JSON_FIELDS: a hook input object with session_id test-session and cwd $CWD.
# JSON_FIELDS is the inside of an object, for example '"tool_name":"Bash","tool_input":{...}'.
payload() {
  printf '{"session_id":"test-session","cwd":"%s","hook_event_name":"%s",%s}' "$CWD" "$1" "$2"
}

# bash_payload COMMAND: a PreToolUse payload for the Bash tool. COMMAND must not hold a double quote
# or a backslash; tests that need them write the JSON by hand.
bash_payload() {
  payload PreToolUse "\"tool_name\":\"Bash\",\"tool_input\":{\"command\":\"$1\"}"
}

# run_guard PAYLOAD [GUARDS]: runs hooks/pre-tool-use with PAYLOAD on stdin.
run_guard() {
  local guards="${2-}"
  run env KERN_GUARDS="$guards" bash "$(kern_root)/hooks/pre-tool-use" <<<"$1"
}

# decision_of OUTPUT: allow|ask|deny|none.
decision_of() {
  local d
  d=$(printf '%s' "$1" | sed -n 's/.*"permissionDecision":"\([a-z]*\)".*/\1/p')
  printf '%s\n' "${d:-none}"
}

# set_autopilot on|off: writes the session state the hooks read (tests only; G16 guards real sessions).
set_autopilot() {
  mkdir -p "$CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA/sessions"
  printf 'voice: normal\nsurface: auto\nautopilot: %s\nsince: 2026-10-12T00:00:00Z\n' "$1" \
    >"$CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA/sessions/test-session.state"
}

# fake_gh: puts a stub `gh` first on PATH. FAKE_GH_STATES (comma list) answers `pr list`,
# FAKE_GH_RC its exit code, FAKE_GH_ME answers `api user`, FAKE_GH_AUTHOR answers `pr view`.
fake_gh() {
  mkdir -p "$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/bin"
  cat >"$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/bin/gh" <<'GH'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
case "$*" in
  "pr list"*) [ -n "${FAKE_GH_STATES-}" ] && printf '%s\n' "$FAKE_GH_STATES" | tr ',' '\n'; exit "${FAKE_GH_RC:-0}" ;;
  "api user"*) printf '%s\n' "${FAKE_GH_ME:-me}" ;;
  "pr view"*) printf '%s\n' "${FAKE_GH_AUTHOR:-someone}" ;;
esac
GH
  chmod +x "$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/bin/gh"
  export PATH="$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/bin:$PATH"
}

tests/run-bats:

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Runs bats with /bin/bash first on PATH on macOS, so hooks are tested on the bash 3.2 they meet there.
set -euo pipefail
# bash 3.2 does not fail a test on a false [[ ]] unless it is the test's last command, so every
# [[ ]] assertion must end with || false. Refuse to run while a bare one exists.
root=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)
bare=$(find "$root/tests" -name '*.bats' -exec grep -nHE '^[[:space:]]+\[\[ .* \]\][[:space:]]*$' {} + || true)
if [ -n "$bare" ]; then
  echo "end each [[ ]] assertion with || false:" >&2
  printf '%s\n' "$bare" >&2
  exit 1
fi
if [ "$(uname)" = Darwin ] && [ -x /bin/bash ]; then
  shim=$(mktemp -d)
  trap 'rm -rf "$shim"' EXIT
  ln -s /bin/bash "$shim/bash"
  PATH="$shim:$PATH"
fi
bats "$@"

@test "json_get decodes escapes and ignores a key-like string inside a value" {
  KERN_INPUT='{"description":"has \"command\": inside","command":"git commit -m \"a b\"\nnext \\ end"}'
  run json_get command
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]
  [ "$output" = 'git commit -m "a b"
next \ end' ]
}

@test "json_get: absent key is 1, a non-string value is 1, unterminated input is 2" {
  KERN_INPUT='{"n":5}'
  run json_get missing
  [ "$status" -eq 1 ]
  run json_get n
  [ "$status" -eq 1 ]
  KERN_INPUT='{"a":"open'
  run json_get a
  [ "$status" -eq 2 ]
}

@test "json_get_raw returns objects, arrays and literals with nested braces in strings" {
  KERN_INPUT='{"tool_input":{"command":"echo }","x":[1,{"y":"]"}]},"n":12,"b":true}'
  run json_get_raw tool_input
  [ "$output" = '{"command":"echo }","x":[1,{"y":"]"}]}' ]
  run json_get_raw n
  [ "$output" = 12 ]
  run json_get_raw b
  [ "$output" = true ]
}

@test "json_escape round-trips quotes, backslashes, tabs and newlines" {
  run bash -c ". '$(kern_root)/hooks/lib/json.sh'; printf 'a \"q\"\tb\\\\c\nd' | json_escape"
  [ "$output" = 'a \"q\"\tb\\c\nd' ]
}

@test "json_ok accepts one object with surrounding whitespace and rejects the rest" {
  KERN_INPUT='  {"a":1}
'
  run json_ok
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]
  KERN_INPUT='[1]'
  run json_ok
  [ "$status" -eq 1 ]
  KERN_INPUT=''
  run json_ok
  [ "$status" -eq 1 ]
}

@test "ti_get reads from TOOL_INPUT" {
  TOOL_INPUT='{"file_path":"/a b/c"}'
  run ti_get file_path
  [ "$output" = "/a b/c" ]
}

@test "a 200 KB value parses in under 5 seconds" {
  big=$(head -c 200000 /dev/zero | tr '\0' 'x')
  KERN_INPUT="{\"tool_name\":\"Write\",\"tool_input\":{\"file_path\":\"/f\",\"content\":\"$big\"}}"
  start=$(date +%s)
  run json_get file_path
  [ "$output" = /f ]
  [ $(($(date +%s) - start)) -lt 5 ]
}

  • Step 3: Write hooks/lib/json.sh
# shellcheck shell=bash
# JSON helpers for kern hooks. POSIX awk only, so behaviour is the same with or without jq.
# All functions read the JSON document from $KERN_INPUT.

# Scanner shared by json_get and json_get_raw. Prints the value of the first object key equal to
# `key`. mode=str prints a decoded string value; mode=raw prints the raw JSON text of any value.
# Exit 0 found, 1 absent (or, in str mode, not a string), 2 malformed input.
_KERN_JSON_AWK='
function skip_ws(p) { while (p <= n && index(" \t\r\n", substr(buf, p, 1)) > 0) p++; return p }
function str_end(p,   d) {
  # p is the index just after an opening quote; returns the index of the closing quote, or 0.
  while (p <= n) {
    d = substr(buf, p, 1)
    if (d == "\\") { p += 2; continue }
    if (d == "\"") return p
    p++
  }
  return 0
}
function decode(s) {
  gsub(/\\\\/, "\001", s)
  gsub(/\\"/, "\"", s); gsub(/\\\//, "/", s)
  gsub(/\\n/, "\n", s); gsub(/\\t/, "\t", s); gsub(/\\r/, "\r", s)
  gsub(/\\b/, "\b", s); gsub(/\\f/, "\f", s)
  gsub(/\001/, "\\", s)
  return s
}
function raw_end(p,   c, depth, e) {
  c = substr(buf, p, 1)
  if (c == "\"") { e = str_end(p + 1); return e }
  if (c == "{" || c == "[") {
    depth = 0
    while (p <= n) {
      c = substr(buf, p, 1)
      if (c == "\"") { e = str_end(p + 1); if (!e) return 0; p = e + 1; continue }
      if (c == "{" || c == "[") depth++
      else if (c == "}" || c == "]") { depth--; if (depth == 0) return p }
      p++
    }
    return 0
  }
  while (p <= n && index(",}] \t\r\n", substr(buf, p, 1)) == 0) p++
  return p - 1
}
{ buf = buf (NR > 1 ? "\n" : "") $0 }
END {
  n = length(buf); i = 1
  while (i <= n) {
    c = substr(buf, i, 1)
    if (c != "\"") { i++; continue }
    e = str_end(i + 1)
    if (!e) exit 2
    tok = substr(buf, i + 1, e - i - 1)
    i = e + 1
    k = skip_ws(i)
    if (substr(buf, k, 1) != ":" || tok != key) continue
    k = skip_ws(k + 1)
    if (mode == "str") {
      if (substr(buf, k, 1) != "\"") exit 1
      e = str_end(k + 1)
      if (!e) exit 2
      printf "%s", decode(substr(buf, k + 1, e - k - 1))
      exit 0
    }
    e = raw_end(k)
    if (!e) exit 2
    printf "%s", substr(buf, k, e - k + 1)
    exit 0
  }
  exit 1
}'

# json_get KEY: decoded string value of the first "KEY": whose value is a string.
json_get() {
  printf '%s' "${KERN_INPUT-}" | LC_ALL=C awk -v key="$1" -v mode=str "$_KERN_JSON_AWK"
}

# json_get_raw KEY: raw JSON text of the first "KEY": value (object, array, string or literal).
json_get_raw() {
  printf '%s' "${KERN_INPUT-}" | LC_ALL=C awk -v key="$1" -v mode=raw "$_KERN_JSON_AWK"
}

# json_escape: stdin escaped for the body of a JSON string, without the surrounding quotes.
json_escape() {
  local tab cr
  tab=$(printf '\t'); cr=$(printf '\r')
  LC_ALL=C sed -e 's/\\/\\\\/g' -e 's/"/\\"/g' -e "s/${tab}/\\\\t/g" -e "s/${cr}/\\\\r/g" |
    LC_ALL=C awk 'BEGIN { sep = "" } { printf "%s%s", sep, $0; sep = "\\n" }'
}

# json_ok: exit 0 when $KERN_INPUT is one object: starts with { and ends with } after trimming.
json_ok() {
  local t
  t=$(printf '%s' "${KERN_INPUT-}" | tr -d ' \t\r\n')
  case "$t" in '{'*'}') return 0 ;; *) return 1 ;; esac
}

# ti_get KEY: json_get applied to the hook's tool_input object ($TOOL_INPUT, set by pre-tool-use).
ti_get() {
  KERN_INPUT="${TOOL_INPUT-}" json_get "$1"
}

# shellcheck shell=bash
# kern settings: layered flat `key: value` files resolved from the main worktree root.
# Needs KERN_ROOT (plugin root). Uses KERN_MAIN when set, else resolves it from CWD (or PWD).

# kern_main_root [dir]: the main worktree root (first `worktree` line of the porcelain list).
kern_main_root() {
  local dir="${1:-${CWD:-$PWD}}" line
  line=$(git -C "$dir" worktree list --porcelain 2>/dev/null | sed -n 's/^worktree //p' | head -n 1)
  [ -n "$line" ] || return 2
  printf '%s\n' "$line"
}

# _settings_files: the settings files in precedence order, one per line (existing or not).
_settings_files() {
  local main="${KERN_MAIN-}"
  if [ -z "$main" ]; then main=$(kern_main_root "${CWD:-$PWD}" 2>/dev/null) || main=""; fi
  if [ -n "$main" ]; then
    printf '%s\n' "$main/.claude/kern.local.yml" "$main/.claude/kern.yml"
  fi
  printf '%s\n' "$HOME/.claude/kern.yml"
}

# _settings_lookup FILE KEY: prints the value of KEY in FILE (last occurrence wins). Exit 1 absent.
_settings_lookup() {
  LC_ALL=C awk -v key="$2" -v sq="'" '
    {
      line = $0
      sub(/\r$/, "", line)
      if (line ~ /^[ \t]*#/ || line ~ /^[ \t]*$/) next
      p = index(line, ":")
      if (p == 0) next
      k = substr(line, 1, p - 1)
      gsub(/^[ \t]+|[ \t]+$/, "", k)
      if (k != key) next
      v = substr(line, p + 1)
      out = ""; q = ""; m = length(v)
      for (i = 1; i <= m; i++) {
        c = substr(v, i, 1)
        if (q == "" && (c == "\"" || c == sq)) q = c
        else if (q != "" && c == q) q = ""
        else if (q == "" && c == "#" && (i == 1 || substr(v, i - 1, 1) ~ /[ \t]/)) break
        out = out c
      }
      gsub(/^[ \t]+|[ \t]+$/, "", out)
      f = substr(out, 1, 1); l = substr(out, length(out), 1)
      if (length(out) >= 2 && f == l && (f == "\"" || f == sq)) out = substr(out, 2, length(out) - 2)
      val = out; found = 1
    }
    END { if (found) { print val; exit 0 } exit 1 }' "$1"
}

# settings_get KEY [DEFAULT]: prints the resolved value. Exit 0 found or defaulted, 3 unset.
settings_get() {
  local f v
  while IFS= read -r f; do
    [ -f "$f" ] || continue
    if v=$(_settings_lookup "$f" "$1"); then
      printf '%s\n' "$v"
      return 0
    fi
  done <<EOT
$(_settings_files)
EOT
  if [ $# -ge 2 ]; then
    printf '%s\n' "$2"
    return 0
  fi
  return 3
}

# settings_source KEY: prints the file that sets KEY, or `default`. Exit 0 always.
settings_source() {
  local f
  while IFS= read -r f; do
    [ -f "$f" ] || continue
    if _settings_lookup "$f" "$1" >/dev/null; then
      printf '%s\n' "$f"
      return 0
    fi
  done <<EOT
$(_settings_files)
EOT
  printf 'default\n'
}

# settings_keys FILE: every key set in FILE, one per line.
settings_keys() {
  LC_ALL=C awk '
    { line = $0; sub(/\r$/, "", line) }
    line ~ /^[ \t]*#/ || line ~ /^[ \t]*$/ { next }
    { p = index(line, ":"); if (p == 0) next
      k = substr(line, 1, p - 1); gsub(/^[ \t]+|[ \t]+$/, "", k); print k }' "$1" | sort -u
}

# settings_known_keys: the key column of kernel/settings.md, one per line. A `<...>` part is a wildcard.
settings_known_keys() {
  sed -n 's/^| `\([^`]*\)` |.*/\1/p' "$KERN_ROOT/kernel/settings.md"
}

# settings_unknown_keys: `file<TAB>key` for every key that kernel/settings.md does not list.
settings_unknown_keys() {
  local f k known pat ok
  known=$(settings_known_keys)
  while IFS= read -r f; do
    [ -f "$f" ] || continue
    while IFS= read -r k; do
      [ -n "$k" ] || continue
      ok=1
      while IFS= read -r pat; do
        case "$pat" in
          *'<'*'>'*) case "$k" in "${pat%%<*}"?*) ok=0 ;; esac ;;
          *) [ "$k" = "$pat" ] && ok=0 ;;
        esac
        [ "$ok" = 0 ] && break
      done <<EOT
$known
EOT
      [ "$ok" = 1 ] && printf '%s\t%s\n' "$f" "$k"
    done <<EOT
$(settings_keys "$f")
EOT
  done <<EOT
$(_settings_files)
EOT
}

# policy_for ACTION: ask|auto|deny for commit, push, pr, tracker or review.post.
policy_for() {
  local key v
  case "$1" in
    review.post) key=review.post_policy ;;
    *) key="$1.policy" ;;
  esac
  v=$(settings_get "$key" ask)
  case "$v" in
    auto|deny) printf '%s\n' "$v" ;;
    *) printf 'ask\n' ;;
  esac
}

# kern_enabled: exit 0 unless the resolved `kern` key is `off`.
kern_enabled() {
  [ "$(settings_get kern on)" != off ]
}

# list_has LIST ITEM: exit 0 when the comma-separated LIST contains ITEM (whitespace trimmed).
list_has() {
  local rest="$1," item
  while [ -n "$rest" ]; do
    item=${rest%%,*}; rest=${rest#*,}
    item=$(printf '%s' "$item" | sed 's/^[ \t]*//; s/[ \t]*$//')
    [ -n "$item" ] && [ "$item" = "$2" ] && return 0
  done
  return 1
}
  • Step 4: Write kernel/settings.md
# kern settings

Files, highest first: `.claude/kern.local.yml` (untracked, the default per-project file), `.claude/kern.yml` (shared, committed only if a team wants it), `~/.claude/kern.yml` (user defaults). Project files are read from the main worktree root. Above all files: the user's instruction in this session, then a project skill that owns the job, then project rules (CLAUDE.md, `.claude/rules`, memory), which may tighten and never loosen.

Format: one `key: value` per line. `#` starts a comment at line start or after whitespace outside quotes. Surrounding quotes are stripped; quote a value that ends in a space. Lists are comma-separated. A key with `<...>` in this table stands for a family, such as `overrides.commit`.

Policy keys take `ask` (default), `auto` or `deny`. Floor actions have no key and never loosen: merge, production deploy, data deletion, force-push to a shared branch, messaging people. A key that names a floor action is reported and ignored.

| Key | Default | Values | Read by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `kern` | `on` | `on`, `off` (no routing, no guards in this project) | session-start, subagent-start, prompt-submit, pre-tool-use |
| `kern_repo` | none | path to the kern clone (user file only) | help |
| `work_dir` | `.kern` | relative directory at the main worktree root | kern-dir, all skills |
