# shellcheck shell=bash
# G5 commit: message convention and attribution checks, explicit staging, commit.policy.

guard_g05() {
  [ "$TOOL" = Bash ] || return 0
  local cmd seg parsed dir sub rest
  cmd=$(ti_get command) || return 0
  while IFS= read -r seg; do
    parsed=$(git_sub "$seg") || continue
    dir=${parsed%%	*}
    rest=${parsed#*	}
    sub=${rest%%	*}
    case "$sub" in
      add) _g05_add "$seg" ;;
      commit) _g05_commit "$dir" "$seg" ;;
    esac
  done <<EOT
$(git_words "$cmd")
EOT
}

_g05_broad() {
  vote ask G5 "$1 stages every change; stage named files with git add -- <paths>"
  [ "$AUTOPILOT" = 1 ] && vote deny G5 "$1 under autopilot"
}

_g05_add() {
  local words
  words=$(_shell_words "$1" | awk 'f { print } $0 == "add" { f = 1 }')
  if word_has "$words" -A --all . :/ -u --update '*'; then
    _g05_broad "git add -A"
  fi
}

# _g05_message DIR WORDS: prints the commit message. Exit 1 when it cannot be read statically.
_g05_message() {
  local dir="$1" words="$2" w prev="" msg="" have=0 f ref
  while IFS= read -r w; do
    case "$prev" in
      -m | --message) msg="${msg:+$msg

}$w"; have=1; prev=""; continue ;;
      -F | --file) f="$w"; prev=""; continue ;;
      -C | -c | --reuse-message | --reedit-message) ref="$w"; prev=""; continue ;;
    esac
    case "$w" in
      -m | --message | -F | --file | -C | -c | --reuse-message | --reedit-message) prev="$w" ;;
      --message=*) msg="${msg:+$msg

}${w#--message=}"; have=1 ;;
      --file=*) f="${w#--file=}" ;;
      -m?*) msg="${msg:+$msg

}${w#-m}"; have=1 ;;
      -F?*) f="${w#-F}" ;;
    esac
  done <<EOT
$words
EOT
  if [ -n "${f-}" ]; then
    [ "$f" = - ] && return 1
    case "$f" in /*) ;; *) f="$dir/$f" ;; esac
    [ -f "$f" ] || return 1
    cat "$f"
    return 0
  fi
  if [ "$have" = 1 ]; then
    case "$msg" in *'$('* | *'`'*) return 1 ;; esac
    printf '%s\n' "$msg"
    return 0
  fi
  if [ -n "${ref-}" ]; then
    git -C "$dir" log -1 --format=%B "$ref" 2>/dev/null
    return $?
  fi
  if word_has "$words" --no-edit && word_has "$words" --amend; then
    git -C "$dir" log -1 --format=%B 2>/dev/null
    return $?
  fi
  return 1
}

# _g05_problems DIR MESSAGE: one line per failed check; nothing when the message passes.
_g05_problems() {
  local dir="$1" msg="$2" subject conv idre branch id
  subject=$(printf '%s\n' "$msg" | sed -n '/[^[:space:]]/{p;q;}')
  if [ "$(settings_get commit.attribution none)" = none ] &&
    printf '%s\n' "$msg" | grep -Eiq 'co-authored-by:.*(claude|anthropic)|generated (with|by) .*claude|claude-session:|claude\.ai/code'; then
    echo "attribution trailer present (commit.attribution: none)"
  fi
  conv=$(settings_get commit.convention '')
  if [ -n "$conv" ] && ! printf '%s\n' "$subject" | grep -Eq -- "$conv"; then
    echo "subject does not match commit.convention $conv"
  fi
  idre=$(settings_get commit.id_from_branch '[A-Z][A-Z0-9]+-[0-9]+')
  branch=$(current_branch "$dir")
  id=$(printf '%s\n' "$branch" | grep -Eo -- "$idre" | head -n 1)
  if [ -n "$id" ]; then
    case "$subject" in *"$id"*) ;; *) echo "subject lacks the ticket id $id from branch $branch" ;; esac
  fi
}

_g05_commit() {
  local dir="$1" words msg problems
  words=$(_shell_words "$2" | awk 'f { print } $0 == "commit" { f = 1 }')
  if word_has "$words" -a --all || printf '%s\n' "$words" | grep -Eq '^-[a-zA-Z]*a[a-zA-Z]*$'; then
    _g05_broad "git commit -a"
  fi
  if ! msg=$(_g05_message "$dir" "$words"); then
    vote ask G5 "commit message cannot be checked here; write it to a file and use git commit -F <file>"
    return 0
  fi
  problems=$(_g05_problems "$dir" "$msg")
  if [ -n "$problems" ]; then
    vote deny G5 "$(printf '%s\n' "$problems" | paste -sd ';' - | sed 's/;/; /g')"
    return 0
  fi
  case "$(policy_for commit)" in
    deny) vote deny G5 "commit.policy deny" ;;
    auto) vote allow G5 "commit.policy auto: message checks pass" ;;
    *)
      if [ "$AUTOPILOT" = 1 ]; then
        vote allow G5 "autopilot commit: message checks pass"
      else
        vote ask G5 "commit (message checks pass)"
      fi
      ;;
  esac
}
  • Step 4: Write hooks/guards/g06-secrets.sh
# shellcheck shell=bash
# G6 secrets: scan what a commit or push would publish; refuse never_commit paths.

guard_g06() {
  [ "$TOOL" = Bash ] || return 0
  local cmd seg parsed dir sub rest
  cmd=$(ti_get command) || return 0
  while IFS= read -r seg; do
    parsed=$(git_sub "$seg") || continue
    dir=${parsed%%	*}
    rest=${parsed#*	}
    sub=${rest%%	*}
    rest=${rest#*	}
    case "$sub" in
      commit) _g06_commit "$dir" "$rest" ;;
      push) _g06_push "$dir" ;;
    esac
  done <<EOT
$(git_words "$cmd")
EOT
}

# _g06_added: reads a unified diff, prints `path:line:text` for every added line.
_g06_added() {
  LC_ALL=C awk '
    /^\+\+\+ / { p = substr($0, 5); sub(/^b\//, "", p); next }
    /^@@ / { split($3, a, ","); ln = substr(a[1], 2) + 0; next }
    /^\+/ { print p ":" ln ":" substr($0, 2); ln++; next }
    /^ / { ln++ }'
}

# _g06_scan: reads `path:line:text` lines, prints `path:line <rule>` per hit. Never prints the text.
_g06_scan() {
  local added rule re
  added=$(cat)
  [ -n "$added" ] || return 0
  while IFS='|' read -r rule re; do
    printf '%s\n' "$added" | grep -E -- "$re" | cut -d: -f1,2 | sed "s/\$/ $rule/"
  done <<'EOT'
private-key|-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----
aws-access-key|AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}
github-token|gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}
slack-token|xox[baprs]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}
api-key|sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}
google-api-key|AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}
EOT
}

# _g06_paths NAMES: deny when a newline-separated path matches a never_commit glob.
_g06_paths() {
  local list glob name rest hits=""
  list=$(settings_get never_commit '')
  [ -n "$list" ] || return 0
  while IFS= read -r name; do
    [ -n "$name" ] || continue
    rest="$list,"
    while [ -n "$rest" ]; do
      glob=${rest%%,*}
      rest=${rest#*,}
      glob=$(printf '%s' "$glob" | sed 's/^[[:space:]]*//; s/[[:space:]]*$//')
      [ -n "$glob" ] || continue
      # shellcheck disable=SC2254
      case "$name" in $glob) hits="${hits:+$hits, }$name" ;; esac
    done
  done <<EOT
$1
EOT
  [ -n "$hits" ] && vote deny G6 "never_commit paths staged: $hits"
  return 0
}

_g06_report() {
  local hits="$1" diff="$2"
  if [ -n "$hits" ]; then
    vote deny G6 "possible secret, not committed or pushed: $(printf '%s\n' "$hits" | head -n 5 | paste -sd ';' - | sed 's/;/; /g')"
  elif command -v gitleaks >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    printf '%s\n' "$diff" | gitleaks stdin --no-banner --redact >/dev/null 2>&1
    [ $? -eq 1 ] && vote deny G6 "gitleaks reports a secret in this change; run gitleaks yourself to see where"
  fi
}

_g06_commit() {
  local dir="$1" rest="$2" diff names
  diff=$(git -C "$dir" diff --cached 2>/dev/null)
  names=$(git -C "$dir" diff --cached --name-only 2>/dev/null)
  case " $rest " in
    *" -a "* | *" --all "* | *" -am "*)
      diff="$diff
$(git -C "$dir" diff 2>/dev/null)"
      names="$names
$(git -C "$dir" diff --name-only 2>/dev/null)"
      ;;
  esac
  _g06_paths "$names"
  _g06_report "$(printf '%s\n' "$diff" | _g06_added | _g06_scan)" "$diff"
}

_g06_push() {
  local dir="$1" range base diff
  if git -C "$dir" rev-parse --verify -q '@{u}' >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    range='@{u}..HEAD'
  elif base=$(base_branch_name); then
    range="origin/$base..HEAD"
  else
    return 0
  fi
  diff=$(git -C "$dir" log -p --format= "$range" 2>/dev/null)
  _g06_paths "$(git -C "$dir" diff --name-only "$range" 2>/dev/null)"
  _g06_report "$(printf '%s\n' "$diff" | _g06_added | _g06_scan)" "$diff"
}

#!/usr/bin/env bats
# G13 protected targets, G14 tracker and chat writes, G15 deploy and publish.

load ../helpers/common

setup() {
  R="$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR/repo"
  make_repo "$R"
  mkdir -p "$R/.claude"
  kern_env "$R"
}

g() { run_guard "$(bash_payload "$1")" "$2"; }
mcp() { run_guard "$(payload PreToolUse "\"tool_name\":\"$1\",\"tool_input\":{}")" g14; }

@test "G13: inactive until protected_targets is set" {
  g "scp a.txt v1-host:/data/" g13
  [ "$(decision_of "$output")" = none ]
}

@test "G13: a copy to the target is denied; a copy from it is not" {
  printf 'protected_targets: v1-host, prod-db.internal\n' >"$R/.claude/kern.local.yml"
  g "scp a.txt v1-host:/data/" g13
  [ "$(decision_of "$output")" = deny ]
  g "scp v1-host:/data/a.txt ." g13
  [ "$(decision_of "$output")" = none ]
}

@test "G13: mutating SQL against the target is denied; SELECT is not" {
  printf 'protected_targets: prod-db.internal\n' >"$R/.claude/kern.local.yml"
  g "psql -h prod-db.internal -c 'UPDATE users SET a=1'" g13
  [ "$(decision_of "$output")" = deny ]
  g "psql -h prod-db.internal -c 'SELECT count(*) FROM users'" g13
  [ "$(decision_of "$output")" = none ]
  printf 'DELETE FROM users;\n' >"$R/fix.sql"
  g "psql -h prod-db.internal -f fix.sql" g13
  [ "$(decision_of "$output")" = deny ]
}

@test "G13: a write over ssh to the target is denied in both modes" {
  printf 'protected_targets: v1-host\n' >"$R/.claude/kern.local.yml"
  g "ssh v1-host 'rm -f /data/x'" g13
  [ "$(decision_of "$output")" = deny ]
  set_autopilot on
  g "ssh v1-host 'rm -f /data/x'" g13
  [ "$(decision_of "$output")" = deny ]
}

@test "G14: a tracker write asks, a read does not vote, autopilot denies" {
  mcp mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue
  [ "$(decision_of "$output")" = ask ]
  mcp mcp__claude_ai_Linear__get_issue
  [ "$(decision_of "$output")" = none ]
  set_autopilot on

  • Step 3: Write hooks/guards/g13-protected.sh
# shellcheck shell=bash
# G13 protected targets: no copies to, and no mutating SQL against, anything in protected_targets.
# Reading (SELECT, scp from the target, sftp get) stays allowed.

guard_g13() {
  [ "$TOOL" = Bash ] || return 0
  local list cmd seg rest target
  list=$(settings_get protected_targets '')
  [ -n "$list" ] || return 0
  cmd=$(ti_get command) || return 0
  rest="$list,"
  while [ -n "$rest" ]; do
    target=${rest%%,*}
    rest=${rest#*,}
    target=$(printf '%s' "$target" | sed 's/^[[:space:]]*//; s/[[:space:]]*$//')
    [ -n "$target" ] || continue
    case "$cmd" in *"$target"*) ;; *) continue ;; esac
    while IFS= read -r seg; do
      _g13_segment "$seg" "$target"
    done <<EOT
$(git_words "$cmd")
EOT
    _g13_sql "$cmd" "$target"
  done
}

_g13_segment() {
  local seg="$1" target="$2" head last
  head=$(cmd_head "$seg")
  case "${head%% *}" in
    scp | rsync)
      last=$(_shell_words "$seg" | tail -n 1)
      case "$last" in *"$target"*) vote deny G13 "copy to protected target $target" ;; esac
      ;;
    sftp)
      printf '%s\n' "$seg" | grep -Eiq '(^|[^a-z])(put|rm|rename|mkdir|rmdir|chmod)[[:space:]]' &&
        vote deny G13 "sftp write to protected target $target"
      ;;
    ssh)
      case "$seg" in *"$target"*) ;; *) return 0 ;; esac
      _shell_words "$seg" | paste -sd ' ' - | grep -Eq '(^|[[:space:];])(rm|mv|cp|del|copy|move|rename|mkdir|rmdir|touch|tee|chmod|chown|systemctl|service|kill)([[:space:];]|$)|>' &&
        vote deny G13 "write command over ssh to protected target $target"
      ;;
  esac
}

# _g13_sql CMD TARGET: mutating SQL in the command, or in a file it feeds with -f, --file or <.
_g13_sql() {
  local cmd="$1" target="$2" text f
  text="$cmd"
  for f in $(printf '%s\n' "$cmd" | grep -Eo '(-f|--file=?|<)[[:space:]]*[^[:space:];|&]+\.sql' | sed -E 's/^(-f|--file=?|<)[[:space:]]*//'); do
    case "$f" in /*) ;; *) f="$CWD/$f" ;; esac
    [ -f "$f" ] && text="$text
$(cat "$f")"
  done
  printf '%s\n' "$text" | grep -Eiq '(^|[^a-z_])(insert[[:space:]]+into|update[[:space:]]+[a-z_."`]+[[:space:]]+set|delete[[:space:]]+from|drop[[:space:]]+(table|database|schema|index|view)|alter[[:space:]]+(table|database|schema)|truncate|create[[:space:]]+(table|database|schema|index)|grant[[:space:]]|revoke[[:space:]]|lock[[:space:]]+tables|set[[:space:]]+global|kill[[:space:]]+[0-9])' &&
    vote deny G13 "mutating SQL against protected target $target"
  return 0
}
  • Step 4: Write hooks/guards/g14-tracker.sh
# shellcheck shell=bash
# G14 tracker and chat writes: MCP write tools on tracker or chat servers, and gh issue writes.
# Chat messages are a floor action (messaging people): tracker.policy never makes them auto.

KERN_G14_TRACKERS="linear jira atlassian asana github gitlab notion monday clickup todoist trello shortcut height zendesk"
KERN_G14_CHAT="slack gmail discord teams intercom front mail telegram whatsapp"
KERN_G14_VERBS="create save update delete remove comment post send merge transition move assign archive edit write add set upload submit resolve share invite publish label close reopen mark reply"

guard_g14() {
  case "$TOOL" in
    mcp__*) _g14_mcp ;;
    Bash) _g14_gh ;;
  esac
}

_g14_has_word() {
  local w
  for w in $2; do
    case "$1" in *"$w"*) return 0 ;; esac
  done
  return 1
}

_g14_mcp() {
  local server action kind="" extra
  server=$(printf '%s' "${TOOL#mcp__}" | sed 's/__.*//' | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')
  action=$(printf '%s' "${TOOL##*__}" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]' | tr '-' '_')
  _g14_has_word "$server" "$KERN_G14_CHAT" && kind=chat
  [ -z "$kind" ] && _g14_has_word "$server" "$KERN_G14_TRACKERS" && kind=tracker
  extra=$(settings_get mcp.write_servers '' | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]' | tr ',' ' ')
  [ -z "$kind" ] && [ -n "$extra" ] && _g14_has_word "$server" "$extra" && kind=tracker
  [ -n "$kind" ] || return 0
  _g14_is_write "$action" || return 0
  _g14_decide "$kind" "$TOOL"
}

# _g14_is_write ACTION: any `_`-separated word of ACTION is a write verb.
_g14_is_write() {
  local part
  for part in $(printf '%s' "$1" | tr '_' ' '); do
    case " $KERN_G14_VERBS " in *" $part "*) return 0 ;; esac
  done
  return 1
}

_g14_decide() {
  if [ "$AUTOPILOT" = 1 ]; then
    vote deny G14 "$2 under autopilot: tracker and chat writes stay human"
    return 0
  fi
  if [ "$1" = chat ]; then
    vote ask G14 "$2 sends a message to people"
    return 0
  fi
  case "$(policy_for tracker)" in
    auto) vote allow G14 "tracker.policy auto: $2" ;;
    deny) vote deny G14 "tracker.policy deny" ;;
    *) vote ask G14 "$2 writes to the tracker" ;;
  esac
}

_g14_gh() {
  local cmd seg
  cmd=$(ti_get command) || return 0
  while IFS= read -r seg; do
    case "$(gh_sub "$seg")" in
      "issue create" | "issue comment" | "issue edit" | "issue close" | "issue reopen" | "issue delete" | "issue transfer" | "issue lock" | "label create" | "label edit" | "label delete")
        _g14_decide tracker "gh $(gh_sub "$seg")"
        ;;
    esac
  done <<EOT
$(git_words "$cmd")
EOT
}
  • Step 5: Write hooks/guards/g15-deploy.sh
# shellcheck shell=bash
# G15 deploy and publish: workflow runs, releases, package publishes, infra applies, deploy-branch pushes.

KERN_G15_RE='^(gh (workflow run|release (create|upload|edit|delete))|(npm|pnpm|yarn( npm)?|bun) publish|cargo publish|twine upload|gem push|poetry publish|docker (push|buildx build .*--push)|(terraform|tofu) (apply|destroy|import)|pulumi (up|destroy)|cdk (deploy|destroy)|kubectl (apply|create|delete|replace|patch|rollout|scale|set|edit|annotate|label)|helm (install|upgrade|uninstall|rollback)|(serverless|sls) (deploy|remove)|vercel( .*)? (--prod|deploy)|fly (deploy|apps destroy)|firebase deploy|eb deploy|aws (ecs update-service|lambda update-function-code|cloudformation (deploy|create-stack|update-stack|delete-stack)|s3 (sync|cp|rm|mv) .*s3://))'

guard_g15() {
  [ "$TOOL" = Bash ] || return 0
  local cmd seg words parsed dir rest sub t envs
  cmd=$(ti_get command) || return 0
  envs=$(settings_get deploy.envs '')
  while IFS= read -r seg; do
    words=$(_shell_words "$seg" | awk '!s && /^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*=/ { next } !s && /^(command|exec|time|nohup|sudo|npx)$/ { next } { s = 1; print }' | paste -sd ' ' -)
    if printf '%s\n' "$words" | grep -Eq -- "$KERN_G15_RE"; then
      _g15_vote "$(printf '%s' "$words" | cut -c1-60)"
      continue
    fi
    [ -n "$envs" ] || continue
    parsed=$(git_sub "$seg") || continue
    dir=${parsed%%	*}
    rest=${parsed#*	}
    sub=${rest%%	*}
    rest=${rest#*	}
    [ "$sub" = push ] || continue
    while IFS= read -r t; do
      [ -n "$t" ] && list_has "$envs" "$t" && _g15_vote "push to deploy branch $t"
    done <<EOT
$(push_targets "$dir" "$rest")
EOT
  done <<EOT
$(git_words "$cmd")
EOT
}

_g15_vote() {
  vote ask G15 "deploy or publish: $1"
  [ "$AUTOPILOT" = 1 ] && vote deny G15 "deploy or publish under autopilot: $1"
  return 0
}

  • Step 3: Write skills/mode/SKILL.md
---
name: mode
description: Switch voice (normal, terse) and surface (chat, auto, html) for this session, or show the current mode.
disable-model-invocation: true
argument-hint: "[terse|normal|chat|auto|html|default|status]"
allowed-tools: Read
---

# Mode

kern's prompt hook applies the switch before this skill loads. This skill only reports what the hook did.

## Steps
1. Read the `kern mode:` line the hook injected this turn.
2. No such line: reply "the mode hook did not run; nothing changed" and name the likely cause: bash missing, hooks disabled, or `kern: off` in the project settings. Never write or edit the session state file; guard G16 denies it.
3. `status`, or no argument: report voice, surface and autopilot. For each, give the default and where it comes from, using the Project settings block of the kernel (`mode.voice`, `mode.surface`, `autopilot`).
4. Surface `html` or `auto` while kern has no `visual` skill installed: add one line that visual pages arrive with the visual skill and replies stay in chat until then.
5. Reply in one line. The terse voice governs chat only. Files, commits, PR bodies and pages stay in plain prose.

## Words
`terse` and `normal` set the voice. `chat`, `auto` and `html` set the surface. `default` resets both to the settings defaults. `status` changes nothing. Words combine, as in `/kern:mode html terse`. An unknown word changes nothing.

A phrase that opens the prompt also switches, without this skill: "go terse", "terse mode", "back to normal", "html mode", "stop html", "chat only". A question or a negation, such as "don't go terse", never switches.
  • Step 4: Write skills/autopilot/SKILL.md
---
name: autopilot
description: Turn this session's autopilot grant on or off, or show what it allows. Only a prompt you type can turn it on.
disable-model-invocation: true
argument-hint: "[on|off|status]"
allowed-tools: Read
---

# Autopilot

A per-session grant for unattended runs. kern's prompt hook turns it on only from a command the user typed; this skill reports the result.

## Steps
1. Read the `kern mode:` line the hook injected this turn. No such line: reply "the hook did not run; autopilot is off". Never write the state file and never report "on" without the hook line.
2. `on`, and the line says `autopilot=on`: list what is allowed, what is denied, the floor, and how to stop (`/kern:autopilot off`, "stop autopilot", or `/clear`). Then ask for, or propose, a checkable stop predicate for the run, such as "stop when every task in the plan has a complete ledger line or one is parked".
3. `on`, and the hook reported "autopilot denied by <file>": name the file and the `autopilot: denied` key. Do not suggest changing it unless the user asks.
4. `status`: on or off, since when, the settings source of `autopilot`, and the `rebase.push` value.
5. `off`: confirm, then list the `parked:` lines in `.kern/ledgers/*/ledger.md` written since autopilot turned on.

## What autopilot allows
- Commit when the message passes the convention and attribution checks. `git add -A` stays denied.
- Push the current branch when it is not protected.
- Open a draft PR after the duplicate check.
- Push with `--force-with-lease` only when settings say `rebase.push: autopilot`, and only to your own branches.

Every other action that would ask is denied, because nobody is there to answer. Merge, deploy, review posting and tracker writes always need the user. Judgment calls are parked with the default chosen, and work that does not depend on them continues.

## Who can turn it on
Only the user, by typing `/kern:autopilot on`. Claude, a subagent, a skill, a background report, a scheduled task and another session cannot.

import { test } from 'node:test';

# Technical writing

Task 16: help skill and lint rule 6

tests/skills-help.test.mjs:

// help: frontmatter from skills spec B3 (description quoted for YAML), setup writes only after a yes.
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { parseFrontmatter } from '../scripts/lib/frontmatter.mjs';

const dir = join(import.meta.dirname, '..', 'skills', 'help');
const src = readFileSync(join(dir, 'SKILL.md'), 'utf8');
const { data, body } = parseFrontmatter(src);

test('help frontmatter matches spec B3, description quoted', () => {
  assert.match(src, /^description: "Map of kern: which skill fits/m);
  assert.equal(data.description, "Map of kern: which skill fits, fixes for a run that went wrong, and help setup to write this project's settings.");
  assert.equal(data['disable-model-invocation'], 'true');
  assert.equal(data['argument-hint'], '[setup|check|<question>]');
  assert.match(data['allowed-tools'], /Bash\(bash \$\{CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT\}\/scripts\/kern-detect \*\)/);
});

test('setup writes only after an explicit yes and never touches tracked files', () => {
  const setup = body.split('## Setup')[1].split('\n## ')[0];
  assert.match(setup, /Write it only after an explicit yes/);
  assert.match(setup, /Never edit `\.gitignore` or any tracked file/);
  assert.match(body, /Under autopilot, setup refuses/);
});

test('the template parses as kern settings and every key in it is documented', () => {
  const table = readFileSync(join(dir, '..', '..', 'kernel', 'settings.md'), 'utf8');
  const tpl = readFileSync(join(dir, 'templates', 'kern.local.yml'), 'utf8');
  const keys = [...tpl.matchAll(/^#? ?([a-z_][a-z0-9_.-]*): /gm)].map((m) => m[1]).filter((k) => !['One', 'Every'].includes(k));
  assert.ok(keys.length > 20);
  for (const k of keys) {
    const family = k.startsWith('overrides.') ? 'overrides.<job>' : k;
    assert.ok(table.includes(`| \`${family}\` |`), `${k} is in kernel/settings.md`);
  }
});

Append to tests/lint-rules.test.mjs (it reuses the file's repo, skill, lint and rules helpers and needs no new imports):

// M1 rules: 6 (routes and help map), 7 (kernel size), 13 (settings keys), 15 (hook-shape fixtures).
const kernel = (rows) => `# kern {VERSION}\n\n## Routing\n| When | Route |\n|---|---|\n${rows.join('\n')}\n`;

test('rule 6: a route must exist and open with ## Steps', () => {
  const ok = lint({
    'kernel/kernel.md': kernel(['| Prose | Read {KERN}/tw/SKILL.md |', '| Phase ends | Read {ROOT}/kernel/context.md |']),
    'skills/tw/SKILL.md': skill('tw', 'description: d\ndisable-model-invocation: true'),
    'kernel/context.md': '# Context\n\n## Steps\n1. a\n',
  });
  assert.deepEqual(rules(ok), []);
  const missing = lint({ 'kernel/kernel.md': kernel(['| Prose | Read {KERN}/nope/SKILL.md |']) });
  assert.deepEqual(rules(missing), [6]);
  const noSteps = lint({
    'kernel/kernel.md': kernel(['| Prose | Read {KERN}/tw/SKILL.md |']),
    'skills/tw/SKILL.md': skill('tw', 'description: d\ndisable-model-invocation: true', '\n# tw\n\n## Rules\n\n1. a\n'),
  });
  assert.deepEqual(rules(noSteps), [6]);
});

test('rule 6: every user-reachable skill is in the help map', () => {
  const map = '| Skill | Tier | Reached by | Job |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `help` | slash | /kern:help | map |\n';
  const files = {
    'skills/help/SKILL.md': skill('help', 'description: d\ndisable-model-invocation: true'),
    'skills/help/references/map.md': map,
    'skills/mode/SKILL.md': skill('mode', 'description: d\ndisable-model-invocation: true'),
  };
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint(files)), [6]);
  files['skills/help/references/map.md'] = map + '| `mode` | slash | /kern:mode | voice |\n';
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint(files)), []);
});

  • Step 3: Write skills/help/SKILL.md
---
name: help
description: "Map of kern: which skill fits, fixes for a run that went wrong, and help setup to write this project's settings."
disable-model-invocation: true
argument-hint: "[setup|check|<question>]"
allowed-tools: Read, Bash(git remote *), Bash(git symbolic-ref *), Bash(git log *), Bash(git for-each-ref *), Bash(git worktree list *), Bash(bash ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/kern-get *), Bash(bash ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/kern-detect *)
---

# Help

The human-facing map of kern and the project setup. A message that asks for work is not a help question: hand it back to the kernel's routing.

## Steps
1. Pick the mode from the argument: `setup`, `check`, or a question. No argument and an unclear need: ask one multiple-choice question: set up this project, pick a skill, fix a run, or change kern itself.
2. Question mode: check the state that changes the answer and mention it only when it does: the kernel was injected this session and its version; which settings files exist; superpowers' SessionStart hook is still enabled (two routers); a second voice plugin injects rules; the mode and autopilot line.
3. Read the target skill before quoting it.
4. Answer from one section of `references/map.md` or `references/fix-a-run.md`. Give at most one example prompt and do not start the work.
5. `setup` and `check` follow their sections below.

## Setup
1. Explore read-only: `git remote -v`; `bash ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/kern-detect base_branch`; the same script for `branch.pattern`, `commit.convention`, `test.full`, `test.focused`, `typecheck`, `lint`, `build`, `tracker` and `pr.template`; CLAUDE.md and `.claude/rules/**`; `.claude/skills/*` and `.claude/commands/*` (jobs a project skill owns); connected tracker MCP tools; existing kern settings.
2. Present two lists: settled by detection (written only where detection guessed wrong) and needs a decision.
3. Ask one key group at a time, recommended answer first, skipping groups detection settled: tracker; conventions; check commands; review; risk (`autopilot`, `protected_targets`, `deploy.envs`, `never_commit`, `scope_threshold`, `shared_services`); `worktree.setup`.
4. Map project skills to kern jobs and show the map, for example `overrides.commit: commit`. Say that kern's guards still apply to project skills.
5. Show the full commented draft of `.claude/kern.local.yml`, built from `templates/kern.local.yml`. Let the user edit it. Write it only after an explicit yes, as one file at the main worktree root; a re-run updates it in place.
6. On the same yes, append to `$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)/info/exclude` the lines `/.claude/kern.local.yml`, `/.kern/`, and `worktree.dir` when it is inside the repo and `git check-ignore -q` fails for it. Never edit `.gitignore` or any tracked file.
7. Offer `.claude/kern.yml` (shared, committed) only if the user wants team settings; committing it follows the normal commit rules. Offer user defaults (`mode.voice`, `mode.surface`, `kern_repo`) for `~/.claude/kern.yml` as a separate yes.
8. Print the status-line snippet from `references/fix-a-run.md`. Never write `settings.json`.

Under autopilot, setup refuses: nobody can confirm the draft. Setup makes no tracker writes.

## Check
Validate without writing anything: unknown keys and floor-action keys (the kernel's Project settings block lists both); `overrides.<job>` values that name a missing project skill; settings files that exist in a linked worktree but not at the main worktree root (`git worktree list`); missing exclude lines for `.claude/kern.local.yml` and `.kern/`. The key table is `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/kernel/settings.md`.
  • Step 4: Write skills/help/references/map.md
# kern map

Installed skills only. Tier: `auto` skills load by description; `path` skills load through a kernel route or a slash command; `slash` skills load only when typed.

| Skill | Tier | Reached by | Job |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | slash | `/kern:mode`, or "go terse", "html mode", "back to normal" | Switch voice and surface for this session |
| `autopilot` | slash | `/kern:autopilot on`, typed only | Grant this session commits, branch pushes and draft PRs without asking |
| `help` | slash | `/kern:help`, `/kern:help setup`, `/kern:help check` | This map, fixes for a run, and project settings |
| `technical-writing` | path | Kernel row "Prose others read", or `/kern:technical-writing` | Docs, PR bodies, issues and commit messages |
| `unslop` | slash | The kernel's Reply rule, technical-writing step 5, or `/kern:unslop` | Rules for prose others read |

## Close calls
- Writing a PR body: `technical-writing` (it applies `unslop`). Editing a reply's tone only: `unslop`.
- Terse chat but a full PR body: terse governs chat only; persisted text always follows `unslop`.
  • Step 5: Write skills/help/references/fix-a-run.md
# Fix a run

| Symptom | Check | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| No routing, no `kern mode:` line | The first context block of the session starts with `# kern <version>` | Run `claude --debug` and look for the kern SessionStart hook; bash must be on PATH; `kern: off` disables kern per project |
| Two routers answer | superpowers is enabled in this project | Set `"superpowers@claude-plugins-official": false` under `enabledPlugins` in `.claude/settings.local.json` |
| Two voices | Another plugin injects a terse voice at session start | Disable that plugin; kern's `terse` mode replaces it |
| `/kern:mode terse` did nothing | `/kern:mode status` shows the hook line | No line: hooks did not run (see the first row). A line with the old value: the word was not recognized |
| A guard asked or denied unexpectedly | The reason starts with `kern G<n>:` | Each guard's rule is in kern's design spec, section 8.3. A policy key (`commit.policy`, `push.policy`, `pr.policy`, `tracker.policy`, `review.post_policy`) changes ask to auto or deny; floor actions have no key |
| Settings ignored in a worktree | Settings live in the main checkout | Put `.claude/kern.local.yml` at the main worktree root (`bash ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/kern-root`) |
| Autopilot refused | "autopilot denied by <file>" | That file sets `autopilot: denied`; only the user changes it |
| kern ignored a project rule | The rule tightens a default | Project rules tighten; only settings keys loosen. Add the key, or report the gap |

## Status line
A plugin cannot add a status line. Add this to your own `statusLine` command to show `[kern terse]`, `[kern html]` or `[kern autopilot]`:

```bash
sid=$(sed -n 's/.*"session_id":"\([A-Za-z0-9_-]*\)".*/\1/p'); f="${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA:-$HOME/.claude/plugins/data/kern-kern}/sessions/$sid.state"
[ -f "$f" ] && awk -F': ' '$1=="autopilot"&&$2=="on"{a=1} $1=="voice"&&$2=="terse"{t=1} $1=="surface"&&$2=="html"{h=1} END{if(a)printf "[kern autopilot] "; if(t)printf "[kern terse] "; if(h)printf "[kern html] "}' "$f"
```

  • Step 7: Add rule 6 to scripts/lib/lint-rules.mjs

Insert this entry into RULES, after the rule 11 entry and before the ]; that closes the array:

  { id: 6, check(model) {
    const out = [];
    const rows = [...model.kernelText.matchAll(/^\|[^|\n]*\|\s*Read \{(KERN|ROOT)\}\/([^\s|]+)\s*\|\s*$/gm)];
    for (const m of rows) {
      const target = m[1] === 'KERN' ? join('skills', m[2]) : m[2];
      const file = join(model.root, target);
      if (!existsSync(file)) { out.push(err(6, 'kernel/kernel.md', `route ${target} does not exist`)); continue; }
      const body = parseFrontmatter(readFileSync(file, 'utf8')).body.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n');
      const firstH2 = body.split('\n').find((l) => l.startsWith('## '));
      if (firstH2 !== '## Steps') out.push(err(6, target, 'a route file must open with ## Steps'));
      else if (!/^## Steps\n+1\. /m.test(body)) out.push(err(6, target, '## Steps must open with a numbered list'));
    }
    const mapFile = join(model.root, 'skills', 'help', 'references', 'map.md');
    if (existsSync(mapFile)) {
      const listed = new Set([...readFileSync(mapFile, 'utf8').matchAll(/^\| `([a-z0-9-]+)` \|/gm)].map((m) => m[1]));
      for (const s of model.skills) {
        if (s.data['user-invocable'] === 'false') continue;
        if (!listed.has(s.name)) out.push(err(6, rel(model, s.file), `${s.name} is missing from skills/help/references/map.md`));
      }
    }
    return out;
  } },

  • Step 2: Write tests/hook-shape.sh
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Runs every hook over the fixture projects in tests/fixtures/projects (lint rule 15).
# Per case: SessionStart output holds each line of expect.txt (or is empty for <empty>);
# SubagentStart prints valid JSON within 1,200 characters; UserPromptSubmit and PreToolUse
# exit 0 and print nothing or valid JSON.
set -euo pipefail
ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)"
TMP=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$TMP"' EXIT
fail=0
cases=0

json_ok() {
  node -e 'let s="";process.stdin.on("data",d=>s+=d).on("end",()=>{if(!s.trim())process.exit(0);JSON.parse(s)})'
}

for dir in "$ROOT"/tests/fixtures/projects/*/; do
  name=$(basename "$dir")
  cases=$((cases + 1))
  repo="$TMP/$name/repo"
  mkdir -p "$repo/.claude" "$TMP/$name/home/.claude"
  git init -q "$repo"
  for f in kern.local.yml kern.yml; do
    [ -f "$dir/$f" ] && cp "$dir/$f" "$repo/.claude/$f"
  done
  export HOME="$TMP/$name/home" CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA="$TMP/$name/data"
  base="\"session_id\":\"shape-$name\",\"cwd\":\"$repo\""
  out=$(printf '{%s,"hook_event_name":"SessionStart","source":"startup"}' "$base" | bash "$ROOT/hooks/session-start")
  while IFS= read -r want; do
    [ -n "$want" ] || continue
    if [ "$want" = "<empty>" ]; then
      [ -z "$out" ] || { echo "FAIL $name: expected no output"; fail=1; }
    elif [[ "$out" != *"$want"* ]]; then
      echo "FAIL $name: missing: $want"
      fail=1
    fi
  done <"$dir/expect.txt"
  sub=$(printf '{%s,"hook_event_name":"SubagentStart","agent_id":"a","agent_type":"Explore"}' "$base" | bash "$ROOT/hooks/subagent-start")
  printf '%s' "$sub" | json_ok || { echo "FAIL $name: SubagentStart output is not JSON"; fail=1; }
  [ "${#sub}" -le 1400 ] || { echo "FAIL $name: SubagentStart output ${#sub} chars"; fail=1; }
  printf '{%s,"hook_event_name":"UserPromptSubmit","prompt":"hello"}' "$base" | bash "$ROOT/hooks/prompt-submit" >/dev/null ||
    { echo "FAIL $name: prompt-submit exited non-zero"; fail=1; }
  pre=$(printf '{%s,"hook_event_name":"PreToolUse","tool_name":"Bash","tool_input":{"command":"git status"}}' "$base" | bash "$ROOT/hooks/pre-tool-use")
  printf '%s' "$pre" | json_ok || { echo "FAIL $name: PreToolUse output is not JSON"; fail=1; }
done
[ "$fail" = 0 ] || exit 1
echo "hook-shape: $cases cases passed"
  • Step 3: Write the failing lint tests

Append to tests/lint-rules.test.mjs:

test('rule 7: an oversized rendered kernel is an error; the real kernel passes', () => {
  const big = '#!/usr/bin/env bash\nhead -c 9500 /dev/zero | tr "\\0" x\n';
  const sub = '#!/usr/bin/env bash\nprintf \'{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"SubagentStart","additionalContext":"k"}}\'\n';
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'hooks/session-start': big, 'hooks/subagent-start': sub })).filter((r) => r === 7), [7]);
  const real = runLint(loadModel(join(import.meta.dirname, '..'))).filter((f) => f.rule === 7 && f.level === 'error');
  assert.deepEqual(real, []);
});

test('rule 13: hooks and the settings table agree', () => {
  const table = '| Key | Default | Values | Read by |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `push.policy` | `ask` | x | pre-tool-use (G1) |\n| `overrides.<job>` | none | x | session-start |\n';
  const hook = '#!/usr/bin/env bash\npolicy_for push\nsettings_get "overrides.$job" ""\n';
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'kernel/settings.md': table, 'hooks/x': hook })), []);
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'kernel/settings.md': table, 'hooks/x': hook + 'settings_get banana.key\n' })), [13]);
  assert.deepEqual(rules(lint({ 'kernel/settings.md': table + '| `orphan.key` | x | x | prompt-submit |\n', 'hooks/x': hook })), [13]);
});

test('rule 15: hook-shape fixtures and runner exist once hooks exist', () => {
  assert.ok(rules(lint({ 'hooks/session-start': '#!/usr/bin/env bash\n' })).includes(15));
  const real = runLint(loadModel(join(import.meta.dirname, '..'))).filter((f) => f.rule === 15);
  assert.deepEqual(real, []);
});

  • Step 5: Add the rules

Replace the first two import lines of scripts/lib/lint-rules.mjs with:

import { readFileSync, readdirSync, existsSync, statSync, mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, copyFileSync, rmSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, relative } from 'node:path';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';

Then insert these entries into RULES, after rule 6 and before the closing ];:

  { id: 7, check(model) {
    if (!existsSync(join(model.root, 'hooks', 'session-start'))) return [];
    const out = [];
    const tmp = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'kern-rule7-'));
    const repo = join(tmp, 'repo');
    mkdirSync(join(repo, '.claude'), { recursive: true });
    spawnSync('git', ['init', '-q', repo]);
    const fixture = join(model.root, 'tests', 'fixtures', 'projects', 'long-settings');
    for (const f of ['kern.local.yml', 'kern.yml']) if (existsSync(join(fixture, f))) copyFileSync(join(fixture, f), join(repo, '.claude', f));
    const env = { ...process.env, HOME: join(tmp, 'home'), CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA: join(tmp, 'data') };
    const hook = (name, payload) => spawnSync('bash', [join(model.root, 'hooks', name)], { input: JSON.stringify(payload), env, encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout || '';
    const norm = (s) => s.split(model.root).join('/Users/someone/.claude/plugins/cache/kern/kern/0.1.0');
    const kernel = norm(hook('session-start', { session_id: 'lint', source: 'startup', cwd: repo, hook_event_name: 'SessionStart' }));
    const terse = existsSync(join(model.root, 'modes', 'terse.md')) ? readFileSync(join(model.root, 'modes', 'terse.md'), 'utf8').length : 0;
    if (kernel.length > 4000) out.push(warn(7, 'kernel/kernel.md', `rendered kernel ${kernel.length} chars with the longest fixture settings, target 4,000`));
    if (kernel.length + terse > 9000) out.push(err(7, 'kernel/kernel.md', `rendered kernel plus terse rules ${kernel.length + terse} chars, limit 9,000`));
    const sub = hook('subagent-start', { session_id: 'lint', cwd: repo, hook_event_name: 'SubagentStart', agent_id: 'a', agent_type: 'Explore' });
    let ctx = '';
    try { ctx = norm(JSON.parse(sub).hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext); } catch { out.push(err(7, 'hooks/subagent-start', 'output is not SubagentStart JSON')); }
    const terseSub = existsSync(join(model.root, 'modes', 'terse-subagent.md')) ? readFileSync(join(model.root, 'modes', 'terse-subagent.md'), 'utf8').length : 0;
    if (ctx.length + terseSub > 1200) out.push(err(7, 'kernel/kernel-subagent.md', `subagent kernel with terse voice ${ctx.length + terseSub} chars, limit 1,200`));
    rmSync(tmp, { recursive: true, force: true });
    return out;
  } },
  { id: 13, check(model) {
    const table = join(model.root, 'kernel', 'settings.md');
    if (!existsSync(table)) return [];
    const out = [];
    const rows = readFileSync(table, 'utf8').split('\n').filter((l) => /^\| `/.test(l)).map((l) => {
      const cells = l.split('|').slice(1, -1).map((c) => c.trim());
      return { key: cells[0].replace(/`/g, ''), readBy: cells[cells.length - 1] };
    });
    const prefix = (k) => (k.includes('<') ? k.slice(0, k.indexOf('<')) : null);
    const known = (k) => rows.some((r) => r.key === k || (prefix(r.key) && k.startsWith(prefix(r.key))));
    const code = [...walk(join(model.root, 'hooks')), ...walk(join(model.root, 'scripts')).filter((f) => /\/kern-[a-z]+$/.test(f))]
      .map((f) => ({ f, text: readFileSync(f, 'utf8') }));
    const read = new Map();
    const add = (k, f) => { if (!read.has(k)) read.set(k, f); };
    for (const { f, text } of code) {
      for (const m of text.matchAll(/settings_(?:get|source)\s+"?([a-z_][a-z0-9_.]*)/g)) add(m[1], f);
      for (const m of text.matchAll(/policy_for\s+"?([a-z][a-z.]*)/g)) if (m[1] !== 'ACTION') add(m[1] === 'review.post' ? 'review.post_policy' : `${m[1]}.policy`, f);
      if (f.endsWith('/kern-detect')) for (const m of text.matchAll(/^ {2}([a-z_.]+)\)$/gm)) add(m[1], f);
    }
    for (const [k, f] of read) if (!known(k)) out.push(err(13, rel(model, f), `reads settings key ${k}, which kernel/settings.md does not list`));
    const hookReader = /session-start|subagent-start|prompt-submit|pre-tool-use|kern-detect|kern-dir|kern-get/;
    const allCode = code.map((c) => c.text).join('\n');
    for (const r of rows) {
      if (!hookReader.test(r.readBy)) continue;
      const ok = prefix(r.key) ? allCode.includes(prefix(r.key)) : read.has(r.key);
      if (!ok) out.push(err(13, 'kernel/settings.md', `${r.key} names a hook or kern script as reader, but none reads it`));
    }
    return out;
  } },
  { id: 15, check(model) {
    if (!existsSync(join(model.root, 'hooks', 'session-start'))) return [];
    const out = [];
    for (const c of ['no-settings', 'shared-only', 'local-only', 'both', 'kern-off', 'floor-policy-key', 'unknown-key', 'long-settings']) {
      if (!existsSync(join(model.root, 'tests', 'fixtures', 'projects', c, 'expect.txt'))) out.push(err(15, `tests/fixtures/projects/${c}`, 'hook-shape fixture has no expect.txt'));
    }
    if (!existsSync(join(model.root, 'tests', 'hook-shape.sh'))) out.push(err(15, 'tests/hook-shape.sh', 'hook-shape runner missing'));
    return out;
  } },

kern M2 Implementation Plan

For agentic workers: REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (- [ ]) syntax for tracking.

Goal: Ship kern's first working workflow: worktree and snapshot scripts, the check scripts behind G7, G9 and G10, guard G18, the three M2 agents, the eleven M2 skills with their kernel routes and evals, a kernel-adherence grader, and a pilot on two real projects.

Architecture: Work happens in kern-made git worktrees off a freshly fetched origin/<base>; per-task review needs no commits because wt-snap turns a worktree's state into a tree id through a temporary index and wt-diff builds review packages between two tree ids. Skills are markdown files reached through kernel rows (path tier), slash commands, or descriptions (auto tier), and they call bash scripts in scripts/ by absolute path. Agents are read-only except the implementer, which works in one worktree and never commits.

Tech Stack: bash 3.2+ and POSIX tools, git 2.31+ (rev-parse --path-format, worktree add --no-track), bats-core, shellcheck, Node 22 (node:test, no npm dependencies), Claude Code 2.1.296 (claude -p --plugin-dir, --output-format stream-json).

Spec: docs/specs/2026-10-12-kern-design.md (sections 3, 4.3, 5, 8, 9, 13, 14) and docs/specs/2026-10-12-kern-m1-m2-skills.md (sections A, C, D, E). Shared names, signatures and exit codes: docs/plans/contract.md, including its sections "Additions from the M2 plan" and "M0 as written (binding)". Executors read the spec, the contract and this plan.

Global Constraints

  • Plugin name kern; skills invoked as /kern:<skill>; plugin agents appear as kern:<name>.
  • Description limits: auto skills at most 300 chars with a trigger phrase; path and slash skills at most 120 chars; agents at most 150 chars; auto skills plus agents at most 2,500 chars. M2 total: prove-it 257 + recall 265 + implementer 149 + task-reviewer 144 + investigator 130 = 945.
  • A frontmatter description that contains : is double-quoted (or single-quoted when it holds double quotes), because Claude Code parses frontmatter as YAML; the parsed text is the spec's text.
  • Path-tier bodies use no $ARGUMENTS placeholders and no ! command injection. Skill files write ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/... for every path into kern; the kernel header prints the absolute root for skills reached by Read.
  • A repo-level script is always written as ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/<name> and run as bash ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/<name>, so a guard sees the script call and lint rule 4 resolves it. A backticked scripts/..., references/... or templates/... path inside a skill means that skill's own directory.
  • MAIN is the main worktree root (first worktree line of git worktree list --porcelain); WORK is MAIN/<work_dir>, default .kern; settings and working files resolve from MAIN, never from cwd.
  • Defaults that skills state when they apply: D1 no commits unless asked; D2 no push, PR, tracker write, deploy or external write without approval; D3 never checkout, switch, reset --hard, stash, bisect or clean in the main checkout, and no two writers share a worktree at once; D4 tasks sharing files or a local service run one at a time; D5 judgment calls go to the user; D6 no silent fallbacks, a missing setting is asked for; D7 stored shapes change by expand and contract; D8 production and declared read-only systems stay read-only; D9 touching a file outside the approved plan pauses; D10 fix loops stop after 3 rounds with a split proposal; D11 new behaviour ships with tests; D12 secrets are never printed.
  • Floor actions never loosen: merge, production deploy, data deletion, force-push to a shared branch, messaging people.
  • Approval: only an explicit yes. "Sounds good", "whatever you think" and silence are not yes. Approval is recorded as status: approved with the date and the user's words in the spec or plan file.
  • Step 0 of every M2 skill, verbatim: 0. Defer: if the kern settings block names an override for this job, follow that project skill instead, say so in one line, and stop.
  • Todo idiom: every route file's first H2 is ## Steps; the kernel copies the steps verbatim into the todo list; a step not done stays as skip: <reason>.
  • Autopilot as skills see it: on only when no settings layer says autopilot: denied and the hook-injected line reads autopilot=on; anything that would ask is denied; judgment calls are parked as parked: <question>; default: <choice>; why: <one line> ledger lines; merge, deploy, review posting and tracker writes stay human.
  • Visual pages ship in M4. A step that says "page when installed" checks ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/visual/SKILL.md; absent, it stays in chat and says once "visual pages not installed".
  • Model table: implementer haiku for batched mechanical edits, sonnet by default, opus for cross-module work or a round-3 fix; task reviewer sonnet; final review opus; investigator sonnet; transcript miners haiku; model.<role> overrides; every dispatch names its model.
  • Port-task convention: a prose file ported from an upstream source names the source file and line range under .upstream/<source>/, lists the required headings in order, states the adaptation rules (no source tags, no personal names or machine paths, kern vocabulary such as WORK, MAIN, kern:investigator and settings keys, unslop rules, no long dashes or curly quotes), gives the full text of anything that is a contract, and ends with a check command: lint, a grep per required heading, and a size bound with wc -c.
  • Upstream layout: .upstream/superpowers/skills/..., .upstream/pstack/pstack/skills/..., .upstream/pocock/skills/engineering/..., .upstream/addy/skills/..., .upstream/local/recall/SKILL.md (filled by scripts/fetch-upstream, M0).
  • Every task that changes skills/, agents/, kernel/ or hooks/ adds one line under ## [Unreleased] in CHANGELOG.md (lint rule 14).
  • Commits: subject M2: <imperative summary>, 72 chars or fewer; body says why; no attribution trailers; stage named paths only (git add -- <paths>), never -A. Pushes happen only after the user says yes.
  • Tests: bats-core 1.5+ (run --separate-stderr), run through tests/run-bats, which on macOS puts /bin/bash 3.2 first on PATH so scripts are tested on the bash they meet there. bash 3.2 ignores a false [[ ]] in the middle of a test, so every [[ ]] assertion ends with || false; tests/run-bats refuses to run while any .bats file has a bare one. Scripts stay bash 3.2 compatible.
  • Prose in every file follows unslop: answer first, no filler, no long dashes, no curly quotes.

Review Focus

  1. A repo whose path contains a space (~/Code/my project). Every script quotes its paths; a missing quote breaks worktree creation, snapshots and diffs only on such machines. Pinned in Task 1 (wt-new works when the repo path has a space) and Task 2 (wt-snap and wt-diff work under a path with a space).
  2. A task that deletes a tracked file and adds a binary file. Snapshots, forks and patches must carry deletions and binary content, or integration silently drops them. Pinned in Task 2 (snapshot drops a deleted tracked file) and Task 3 (fork carries a deletion and a binary file, wt-apply applies a binary patch).
  3. A plan whose task text holds a fenced block with a ### Task 9 line. task-brief must not start or stop a task inside a fence. Pinned in Task 4 (a Task heading inside a fence is ignored).
  4. Investigator commands with pipes, quotes and 2>/dev/null. G18 must split simple commands correctly, allow a stderr redirect to /dev/null, and still deny a real redirect. Pinned in Task 7 (pipes and stderr to /dev/null stay allowed, a file redirect is denied).
  5. A project with no origin remote. wt-new must stop with a clear message instead of branching off a stale local commit. Pinned in Task 1 (no origin remote exits 2 with a message).

Task 1: Worktree creation and the entry check

Files:

  • Create: scripts/lib/wt-common.sh, scripts/wt-new, scripts/wt-check
  • Test: tests/helpers/wt.bash, tests/scripts/wt-new.bats

Interfaces:

  • Consumes: hooks/lib/settings.sh: kern_main_root [dir], settings_get KEY [DEFAULT] (reads $MAIN/.claude/kern.local.yml, $MAIN/.claude/kern.yml, $HOME/.claude/kern.yml; exit 3 when unset with no default). scripts/kern-detect base_branch prints value<TAB>evidence.
  • Produces:
    • wt-new SLUG [--detach] [--base REF] [--branch NAME]: prints the physical worktree path; exit 0, or 2 on any error.
    • wt-check WT BASE_REF: prints ok (exit 0) or the first mismatch (exit 1); usage error exit 2.
    • scripts/lib/wt-common.sh functions wt_dir, wt_exclude_dir DIR, wt_include DST, wt_setup WT, wt_path_free PATH; marker file <worktree git dir>/kern-setup-done.
    • tests/helpers/wt.bash: KERN_ROOT, wt_test_env, make_fixture DIR.
  • Step 1: Write the test helper
# tests/helpers/wt.bash
# Fixture repos for the worktree and check-script tests. Each test gets its own HOME,
# so no user setting or global git config leaks in.
KERN_ROOT=$(cd "$BATS_TEST_DIRNAME/../.." && pwd -P)

wt_test_env() {
  TMP=$(cd "$BATS_TEST_TMPDIR" && pwd -P)
  export HOME="$TMP/home"
  mkdir -p "$HOME"
  export GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1
  export CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA="$TMP/plugin-data"
}

# make_fixture DIR: a repo on main with one commit, a bare origin at DIR.origin,
# main pushed and refs/remotes/origin/HEAD set. .gitignore ignores node_modules/ and *.log.
make_fixture() {
  local dir=$1
  git init --quiet --bare --initial-branch=main "$dir.origin"
  git init --quiet --initial-branch=main "$dir"
  git -C "$dir" config user.email test@example.com
  git -C "$dir" config user.name Test
  git -C "$dir" config commit.gpgsign false
  printf 'node_modules/\n*.log\n' > "$dir/.gitignore"
  printf 'one\ntwo\nthree\n' > "$dir/a.txt"
  git -C "$dir" add .gitignore a.txt
  git -C "$dir" commit --quiet -m init
  git -C "$dir" remote add origin "$dir.origin"
  git -C "$dir" push --quiet origin main
  git -C "$dir" remote set-head origin main
}

  • Step 4: Write scripts/lib/wt-common.sh
# shellcheck shell=bash
# shellcheck disable=SC2154  # MAIN is set by the sourcing script
# Shared by wt-new and wt-fork. Sourced, never run. Needs hooks/lib/settings.sh and MAIN.

# wt_dir: the directory kern worktrees live in (worktree.dir, default MAIN/.claude/worktrees).
wt_dir() {
  local d
  d=$(settings_get worktree.dir .claude/worktrees)
  case "$d" in /*) ;; *) d="$MAIN/$d" ;; esac
  printf '%s\n' "$d"
}

# wt_path_free PATH: exit 2 when PATH already exists.
wt_path_free() {
  if [ -e "$1" ]; then
    echo "wt: path exists: $1" >&2
    return 2
  fi
}

# wt_exclude_dir DIR: keep a worktree dir inside MAIN out of the main checkout's status.
# DIR must exist. Appends to the common git dir's info/exclude, which applies to every worktree.
wt_exclude_dir() {
  local dir=$1 rel common
  case "$dir" in "$MAIN"/*) rel=${dir#"$MAIN"/} ;; *) return 0 ;; esac
  if git -C "$MAIN" check-ignore -q "$rel" 2>/dev/null; then return 0; fi
  common=$(git -C "$MAIN" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir)
  mkdir -p "$common/info"
  if ! grep -qxF "/$rel/" "$common/info/exclude" 2>/dev/null; then
    printf '/%s/\n' "$rel" >> "$common/info/exclude"
    echo "kern: added /$rel/ to .git/info/exclude" >&2
  fi
}

# wt_include DST: copy untracked files that MAIN/.worktreeinclude names (gitignore syntax) into DST.
wt_include() {
  local dst=$1 f wtrel
  [ -f "$MAIN/.worktreeinclude" ] || return 0
  wtrel=$(wt_dir)
  wtrel=${wtrel#"$MAIN"/}
  git -C "$MAIN" ls-files --others --ignored --directory --exclude-from="$MAIN/.worktreeinclude" |
    while IFS= read -r f; do
      f=${f%/}
      case "$f" in "$wtrel"|"$wtrel"/*|.kern|.kern/*) continue ;; esac
      [ -e "$dst/$f" ] && continue
      mkdir -p "$dst/$(dirname "$f")"
      cp -R "$MAIN/$f" "$dst/$f"
    done
}

# wt_setup WT: run worktree.setup in WT and write the marker wt-check looks for.
wt_setup() {
  local wt=$1 setup gitdir
  gitdir=$(git -C "$wt" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-dir)
  if setup=$(settings_get worktree.setup); then
    if ! (cd "$wt" && bash -c "$setup") > "$gitdir/kern-setup.log" 2>&1; then
      echo "wt: worktree.setup failed in $wt (log: $gitdir/kern-setup.log)" >&2
      return 2
    fi
    printf 'setup: %s\n' "$setup" > "$gitdir/kern-setup-done"
  else
    printf 'setup: none (worktree.setup unset)\n' > "$gitdir/kern-setup-done"
    echo "wt: worktree.setup is unset; no setup ran in $wt" >&2
  fi
}
  • Step 5: Write scripts/wt-new
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Create a kern worktree off a freshly fetched origin/<base>, copy .worktreeinclude files, run worktree.setup.
# Usage: wt-new SLUG [--detach] [--base REF] [--branch NAME]
# A SHA-shaped REF is used as a commit with no fetch (bisection); any other base must fetch from origin.
set -euo pipefail
here=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd -P)
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
. "$here/../hooks/lib/settings.sh"
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
. "$here/lib/wt-common.sh"

usage() { echo "usage: wt-new SLUG [--detach] [--base REF] [--branch NAME]" >&2; exit 2; }
[ $# -ge 1 ] || usage
slug=$1
shift
case "$slug" in ''|.*|*[!A-Za-z0-9._-]*) echo "wt-new: bad slug: $slug" >&2; exit 2 ;; esac
detach=0 base='' branch=''
while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
  case "$1" in
    --detach) detach=1; shift ;;
    --base) [ $# -ge 2 ] || usage; base=$2; shift 2 ;;
    --branch) [ $# -ge 2 ] || usage; branch=$2; shift 2 ;;
    *) usage ;;
  esac
done

MAIN=$(kern_main_root "$PWD") || { echo "wt-new: not a git repository" >&2; exit 2; }
if [ -z "$base" ]; then
  if ! base=$(settings_get base_branch); then
    base=$(bash "$here/kern-detect" base_branch | cut -f1) ||
      { echo "wt-new: base_branch is not set and not detectable; set it in .claude/kern.local.yml" >&2; exit 2; }
  fi
fi

if printf '%s' "$base" | grep -Eq '^[0-9a-f]{7,64}$'; then
  git -C "$MAIN" rev-parse --verify --quiet "$base^{commit}" > /dev/null ||
    { echo "wt-new: unknown commit $base" >&2; exit 2; }
  start=$base
else
  name=${base#origin/}
  git -C "$MAIN" fetch --quiet origin "$name" 2> /dev/null ||
    { echo "wt-new: git fetch origin $name failed; kern branches only off a fresh origin/$name" >&2; exit 2; }
  start="refs/remotes/origin/$name"
  git -C "$MAIN" rev-parse --verify --quiet "$start" > /dev/null ||
    { echo "wt-new: $start is missing after the fetch" >&2; exit 2; }
fi

dir=$(wt_dir)
path="$dir/$slug"
wt_path_free "$path" || exit 2
mkdir -p "$dir"
wt_exclude_dir "$dir"

if [ "$detach" = 1 ]; then
  git -C "$MAIN" worktree add --quiet --detach "$path" "$start" >&2
else
  if [ -z "$branch" ]; then
    pattern=$(settings_get branch.pattern 'kern/{slug}')
    branch=${pattern//\{slug\}/$slug}
    case "$branch" in
      *'{'*) echo "wt-new: branch.pattern has placeholders wt-new cannot fill ($branch); pass --branch NAME" >&2; exit 2 ;;
    esac
  fi
  git -C "$MAIN" check-ref-format --branch "$branch" > /dev/null 2>&1 ||
    { echo "wt-new: bad branch name: $branch" >&2; exit 2; }
  if git -C "$MAIN" rev-parse --verify --quiet "refs/heads/$branch" > /dev/null; then
    echo "wt-new: branch exists: $branch" >&2
    exit 2
  fi
  git -C "$MAIN" worktree add --quiet --no-track -b "$branch" "$path" "$start" >&2
fi

path=$(cd "$path" && pwd -P)
wt_include "$path"
wt_setup "$path" || exit 2
printf '%s\n' "$path"
  • Step 6: Write scripts/wt-check
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Entry check for a worker: a linked kern worktree, not the main checkout, based on BASE_REF, setup done.
# Usage: wt-check WT BASE_REF    Prints ok (exit 0) or the first mismatch (exit 1).
set -euo pipefail
here=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd -P)
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
. "$here/../hooks/lib/settings.sh"

[ $# -eq 2 ] || { echo "usage: wt-check WT BASE_REF" >&2; exit 2; }
wt=$1 base=$2
fail() { echo "$1"; exit 1; }

[ -d "$wt" ] || fail "no such directory: $wt"
wt=$(cd "$wt" && pwd -P)
top=$(git -C "$wt" rev-parse --show-toplevel 2> /dev/null) || fail "not a git worktree: $wt"
gitdir=$(git -C "$wt" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-dir)
common=$(git -C "$wt" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir)
[ "$gitdir" != "$common" ] || fail "not a linked worktree (this is the main checkout): $wt"
MAIN=$(kern_main_root "$wt") || fail "cannot resolve the main worktree for $wt"
[ "$(cd "$top" && pwd -P)" != "$(cd "$MAIN" && pwd -P)" ] || fail "worktree is the main checkout: $wt"
git -C "$wt" rev-parse --verify --quiet "$base^{commit}" > /dev/null || fail "unknown base ref: $base"
git -C "$wt" merge-base --is-ancestor "$base" HEAD || fail "base $base is not an ancestor of HEAD in $wt"
[ -f "$gitdir/kern-setup-done" ] || fail "setup marker missing in $wt: create worktrees with wt-new or wt-fork"
echo ok

Task 2: Snapshot trees and review packages

Files:

  • Create: scripts/wt-snap, scripts/wt-diff
  • Test: tests/scripts/wt-snap-diff.bats

Interfaces:

  • Consumes: tests/helpers/wt.bash (Task 1); scripts/kern-dir SUB (M1) for the default package location.
  • Produces:
    • wt-snap WT: prints the tree id of WT's working state (tracked and untracked files, ignore rules applied); exit 0, 2 on error. Touches no ref and not the real index.
    • wt-diff WT FROM_TREE [TO_TREE] [--out FILE]: writes the package and <package>.patch; prints the package path, then TO_TREE, then one changed path per line; exit 0, 3 on an empty diff, 2 on error. Default package path: WORK/reviews/<from7>..<to7>.diff.

# tests/scripts/wt-snap-diff.bats

index_sum() { cksum < "$(git -C "$WT" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-dir)/index"; }

@test "an unchanged worktree snapshots to HEAD's tree" {
  run bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$WT"

@test "snapshot includes untracked files and skips ignored ones" {

  mkdir -p "$WT/node_modules/x" && printf 'dep\n' > "$WT/node_modules/x/i.js"
  printf 'noise\n' > "$WT/debug.log"
  tree=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$WT")
  run git -C "$WT" ls-tree -r --name-only "$tree"
  [[ "$output" == *"b.txt"* ]] || false
  [[ "$output" != *"node_modules"* ]] || false
  [[ "$output" != *"debug.log"* ]] || false
}

  rm "$WT/a.txt"
  tree=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$WT")
  run git -C "$WT" ls-tree -r --name-only "$tree"
  [[ "$output" != *"a.txt"* ]] || false
}

@test "the real index and status are untouched" {
  printf 'new\n' > "$WT/b.txt"
  printf 'changed\n' >> "$WT/a.txt"
  before_sum=$(index_sum)
  before_status=$(git -C "$WT" status --porcelain)
  bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$WT" > /dev/null
  [ "$(index_sum)" = "$before_sum" ]
  [ "$(git -C "$WT" status --porcelain)" = "$before_status" ]
  [ -z "$(git -C "$WT" diff --cached --name-only)" ]
}

@test "same state gives the same tree; a change gives a new one" {
  printf 'x\n' > "$WT/b.txt"
  t1=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$WT")
  t2=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$WT")
  [ "$t1" = "$t2" ]
  printf 'y\n' > "$WT/b.txt"
  t3=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$WT")
  [ "$t1" != "$t3" ]
}

  printf 'four\n' >> "$WT/a.txt"
  run --separate-stderr bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-diff" "$WT" "$t0" --out "$TMP/pkg.diff"
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]

  grep -q '^## Files changed$' "$TMP/pkg.diff"
  grep -q '^## Diff$' "$TMP/pkg.diff"
  grep -q '^+new$' "$TMP/pkg.diff"
  grep -q '^diff --git a/b.txt b/b.txt$' "$TMP/pkg.diff.patch"
}

@test "wt-diff defaults the package into WORK/reviews" {

  • Step 3: Write scripts/wt-snap
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Print the tree id of a worktree's whole working state: tracked and untracked files, ignore rules applied.
# A temporary index does the work, so no ref and not the real index change.
# Usage: wt-snap WT
set -euo pipefail
[ $# -eq 1 ] || { echo "usage: wt-snap WT" >&2; exit 2; }
wt=$1
git -C "$wt" rev-parse --git-dir > /dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "wt-snap: not a git worktree: $wt" >&2; exit 2; }
tmp=$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/kern-snap.XXXXXX")
trap 'rm -rf "$tmp"' EXIT
export GIT_INDEX_FILE="$tmp/index"
if git -C "$wt" rev-parse --verify --quiet HEAD > /dev/null; then
  git -C "$wt" read-tree HEAD
else
  git -C "$wt" read-tree --empty
fi
git -C "$wt" add -A
git -C "$wt" write-tree
  • Step 4: Write scripts/wt-diff
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Review package between two snapshot trees of a worktree. Also writes <package>.patch (binary-safe) for wt-apply.
# Usage: wt-diff WT FROM_TREE [TO_TREE] [--out FILE]
# Prints: package path, TO_TREE, then one changed path per line. Exit 3 when the trees are identical.
# Adapted from superpowers subagent-driven-development/scripts/review-package (MIT).
set -euo pipefail
here=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd -P)
usage() { echo "usage: wt-diff WT FROM_TREE [TO_TREE] [--out FILE]" >&2; exit 2; }
[ $# -ge 2 ] || usage
wt=$1 from=$2
shift 2
to='' out=''
while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
  case "$1" in
    --out) [ $# -ge 2 ] || usage; out=$2; shift 2 ;;
    -*) usage ;;
    *) [ -z "$to" ] || usage; to=$1; shift ;;
  esac
done
git -C "$wt" rev-parse --git-dir > /dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "wt-diff: not a git worktree: $wt" >&2; exit 2; }
[ -n "$to" ] || to=$(bash "$here/wt-snap" "$wt")
fromt=$(git -C "$wt" rev-parse --verify --quiet "$from^{tree}") || { echo "wt-diff: not a tree: $from" >&2; exit 2; }
tot=$(git -C "$wt" rev-parse --verify --quiet "$to^{tree}") || { echo "wt-diff: not a tree: $to" >&2; exit 2; }
if git -C "$wt" diff --quiet "$fromt" "$tot"; then
  echo "wt-diff: empty diff ${fromt:0:7}..${tot:0:7}" >&2
  exit 3
fi
if [ -z "$out" ]; then
  rdir=$(cd "$wt" && bash "$here/kern-dir" reviews)
  out="$rdir/${fromt:0:7}..${tot:0:7}.diff"
fi
mkdir -p "$(dirname "$out")"
{
  echo "# Review package: ${fromt:0:12}..${tot:0:12}"
  echo
  echo "## Files changed"
  git -C "$wt" diff --name-status "$fromt" "$tot"
  echo
  echo "## Stat"
  git -C "$wt" diff --stat "$fromt" "$tot"
  echo
  echo "## Diff"
  git -C "$wt" diff -U10 "$fromt" "$tot"
} > "$out"
git -C "$wt" diff --binary "$fromt" "$tot" > "$out.patch"
printf '%s\n%s\n' "$out" "$tot"
git -C "$wt" diff --name-only "$fromt" "$tot"
  • Step 5: Run the tests to verify they pass

Run: chmod +x scripts/wt-snap scripts/wt-diff && tests/run-bats tests/scripts/wt-snap-diff.bats && shellcheck scripts/wt-snap scripts/wt-diff Expected: 10 tests, 0 failures; shellcheck prints nothing.

Task 3: Forks and serial integration

  • Test: tests/scripts/wt-fork-apply.bats

Interfaces:

  • Consumes: wt-snap, wt-common.sh (Tasks 1 and 2).
  • Produces:
    • wt-fork SRC_WT SLUG: detached worktree at SRC_WT's HEAD with SRC_WT's uncommitted state applied; prints the fork path; exit 0, 3 when the state cannot be applied (the fork is removed), 2 on other errors. Invariant: wt-snap <fork> equals wt-snap <src> at fork time.
    • wt-apply PATCH DST_WT: git apply --check, then git apply; prints applied; exit 0, 3 on a conflict (DST unchanged), 2 on a usage error.
  • Step 1: Write the failing tests
#!/usr/bin/env bats
# tests/scripts/wt-fork-apply.bats

bats_require_minimum_version 1.5.0
load ../helpers/wt

setup() {
  wt_test_env
  REPO="$TMP/repo"
  make_fixture "$REPO"
  cd "$REPO"
  SRC=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-new" plan)
}

@test "fork carries modified and untracked files and snapshots to the same tree" {
  printf 'edited\n' >> "$SRC/a.txt"
  printf 'new\n' > "$SRC/b.txt"
  src_tree=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$SRC")
  run --separate-stderr bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-fork" "$SRC" task-1
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]
  fork=$output
  [ "$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$fork")" = "$src_tree" ]
  [ "$(git -C "$fork" rev-parse HEAD)" = "$(git -C "$SRC" rev-parse HEAD)" ]
  [ "$(git -C "$fork" rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD)" = "HEAD" ]
  [ "$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$SRC")" = "$src_tree" ]
}

@test "fork carries a deletion and a binary file" {
  rm "$SRC/a.txt"
  printf '\000\001\002binary' > "$SRC/blob.bin"
  run --separate-stderr bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-fork" "$SRC" task-2
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]
  fork=$output
  [ ! -e "$fork/a.txt" ]
  cmp -s "$SRC/blob.bin" "$fork/blob.bin"
}

@test "a fork passes wt-check" {
  fork=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-fork" "$SRC" task-3 2> /dev/null)
  run bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-check" "$fork" origin/main
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]
}

@test "an existing fork path exits 2" {
  bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-fork" "$SRC" task-4 > /dev/null 2>&1
  run --separate-stderr bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-fork" "$SRC" task-4
  [ "$status" -eq 2 ]
}

@test "wt-apply applies a worker's patch into the plan worktree" {

  t0=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$fork")

  run bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-apply" "$pkg.patch" "$SRC"
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]
  [ "$output" = "applied" ]
  [ "$(cat "$SRC/w.txt")" = "from worker" ]
}

@test "wt-apply applies a binary patch" {
  fork=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-fork" "$SRC" task-6 2> /dev/null)

@test "a conflicting patch exits 3 and leaves the destination unchanged" {

  t0=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$fork")

  printf 'one\nTWO-other\nthree\n' > "$SRC/a.txt"

  [[ "$output" == *"a.txt"* ]] || false
  [ "$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$SRC")" = "$before" ]
}

@test "a missing patch file exits 2" {
  run bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-apply" "$TMP/none.patch" "$SRC"
  [ "$status" -eq 2 ]
}

  • Step 3: Write scripts/wt-fork
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Detached worktree at SRC_WT's HEAD with SRC_WT's uncommitted state (tracked and untracked) applied.
# Usage: wt-fork SRC_WT SLUG    Prints the fork path. Exit 3 when the state cannot be applied.
set -euo pipefail
here=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd -P)
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
. "$here/../hooks/lib/settings.sh"
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
. "$here/lib/wt-common.sh"

[ $# -eq 2 ] || { echo "usage: wt-fork SRC_WT SLUG" >&2; exit 2; }
src=$(cd "$1" 2> /dev/null && pwd -P) || { echo "wt-fork: no such directory: $1" >&2; exit 2; }
slug=$2
case "$slug" in ''|.*|*[!A-Za-z0-9._-]*) echo "wt-fork: bad slug: $slug" >&2; exit 2 ;; esac
MAIN=$(kern_main_root "$src") || { echo "wt-fork: not a git repository: $src" >&2; exit 2; }
head=$(git -C "$src" rev-parse --verify --quiet HEAD) || { echo "wt-fork: $src has no commits" >&2; exit 2; }
tree=$(bash "$here/wt-snap" "$src")

dir=$(wt_dir)
path="$dir/$slug"
wt_path_free "$path" || exit 2
mkdir -p "$dir"
wt_exclude_dir "$dir"

patch=$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/kern-fork.XXXXXX")
trap 'rm -f "$patch"' EXIT
git -C "$src" diff --binary "$head" "$tree" > "$patch"
git -C "$MAIN" worktree add --quiet --detach "$path" "$head" >&2
path=$(cd "$path" && pwd -P)
if [ -s "$patch" ] && ! git -C "$path" apply --binary "$patch" >&2; then
  git -C "$MAIN" worktree remove --force "$path" > /dev/null 2>&1 || true
  echo "wt-fork: could not apply the uncommitted state of $src; fork removed" >&2
  exit 3
fi
wt_include "$path"
wt_setup "$path" || exit 2
printf '%s\n' "$path"

The worktree remove --force here only removes the fork this run just created; it holds no other work.

  • Step 4: Write scripts/wt-apply
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Apply a binary-safe patch (wt-diff's <package>.patch) into a worktree, all or nothing.
# Usage: wt-apply PATCH DST_WT    Prints "applied". Exit 3 on conflict, with DST_WT unchanged.
set -euo pipefail
[ $# -eq 2 ] || { echo "usage: wt-apply PATCH DST_WT" >&2; exit 2; }
patch=$1 dst=$2
[ -f "$patch" ] || { echo "wt-apply: no such patch: $patch" >&2; exit 2; }
git -C "$dst" rev-parse --git-dir > /dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "wt-apply: not a git worktree: $dst" >&2; exit 2; }
patch=$(cd "$(dirname "$patch")" && pwd -P)/$(basename "$patch")
if ! out=$(git -C "$dst" apply --check --binary "$patch" 2>&1); then
  echo "wt-apply: conflict, nothing applied:"
  printf '%s\n' "$out"
  exit 3
fi
git -C "$dst" apply --binary "$patch"
echo applied

Task 4: Briefs and the ledger

Files:

  • Create: scripts/task-brief, scripts/task-start, scripts/task-done
  • Test: tests/scripts/task.bats

Interfaces:

  • Produces:
    • task-brief PLAN N LEDGER_DIR: writes LEDGER_DIR/briefs/task-N.md holding a header line, the plan's ## Global Constraints section and the task's section; prints that path; exit 0, 2 when the task is not found or on a usage error.
    • task-start LEDGER N TREE: appends and prints Task N: start tree=<id> at=<iso>; exit 0, 2 when the ledger is missing or an argument is malformed.
    • task-done LEDGER N TREE -- CMD...: runs CMD in the current directory, keeps its output in <ledger dir>/logs/task-N.log, prints the last 5 lines; on exit 0 appends Task N: complete tree=<id> test=<cmd> exit=0 review=approved; exits with CMD's code.
    • Ledger line formats (also in skills/execute-plan/references/ledger-format.md, Task 16).
  • Step 1: Write the failing tests
#!/usr/bin/env bats
# tests/scripts/task.bats

load ../helpers/wt

setup() {
  wt_test_env
  PLAN="$TMP/plan.md"
  # One printf argument per line keeps the fixture's headings and fences out of column 0 of this plan,
  # so task extractors reading the plan itself are not confused.
  printf '%s\n' \
    '# Example plan' \
    '' \
    '## Global Constraints' \
    '' \
    '- Node 22.' \
    '' \
    '## Review Focus' \
    '' \
    '1. Empty input.' \
    '' \
    '### Task 1: First' \
    '' \
    'Do the first thing.' \
    '' \
    '```markdown' \
    '### Task 9: not a real task' \
    '```' \
    '' \
    'Still task 1.' \
    '' \
    '### Task 2: Second' \
    '' \
    'Do the second thing.' \
    '' \
    '## Self-review' \
    '' \
    'Not part of any task.' > "$PLAN"
  LEDGER_DIR="$TMP/ledger"
  mkdir -p "$LEDGER_DIR"
  LEDGER="$LEDGER_DIR/ledger.md"
  printf '# kern ledger: %s\n' "$PLAN" > "$LEDGER"
  TREE=$(printf '%040d' 0 | tr 0 a)
}

  • Step 3: Write scripts/task-brief
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Extract one task, plus the plan's Global Constraints, into LEDGER_DIR/briefs/task-N.md.
# Usage: task-brief PLAN N LEDGER_DIR    Prints the brief path. Exit 2 when the task is not found.
# Adapted from superpowers subagent-driven-development/scripts/task-brief (MIT).
set -euo pipefail
[ $# -eq 3 ] || { echo "usage: task-brief PLAN N LEDGER_DIR" >&2; exit 2; }
plan=$1 n=$2 ledger_dir=$3
[ -f "$plan" ] || { echo "task-brief: no such plan: $plan" >&2; exit 2; }
case "$n" in ''|*[!0-9]*) echo "task-brief: N must be a number: $n" >&2; exit 2 ;; esac

task=$(awk -v n="$n" '
  /^[`][`][`]|^~~~/ { fence = !fence; if (intask) print; next }
  fence { if (intask) print; next }
  /^#+[ \t]/ {
    match($0, /^#+/); lvl = RLENGTH
    if ($0 ~ /^#+[ \t]+Task[ \t]+[0-9]+/) {
      intask = ($0 ~ ("^#+[ \t]+Task[ \t]+" n "([^0-9]|$)")); tlvl = lvl
    } else if (intask && lvl <= tlvl) {
      intask = 0
    }
  }
  intask { print }
' "$plan")
[ -n "$task" ] || { echo "task-brief: no heading 'Task $n' in $plan" >&2; exit 2; }

constraints=$(awk '
  /^[`][`][`]|^~~~/ { fence = !fence }
  !fence && /^## / { gc = ($0 ~ /^## Global Constraints/) }
  gc { print }
' "$plan")

mkdir -p "$ledger_dir/briefs"
out="$ledger_dir/briefs/task-$n.md"
plan_abs=$(cd "$(dirname "$plan")" && pwd -P)/$(basename "$plan")
{
  printf '# Brief: Task %s\n\nPlan: %s\n\n' "$n" "$plan_abs"
  if [ -n "$constraints" ]; then printf '%s\n\n' "$constraints"; fi
  printf '%s\n' "$task"
} > "$out"
printf '%s\n' "$out"
  • Step 4: Write scripts/task-start
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Record the start of a task in an execute-plan ledger.
# Usage: task-start LEDGER N TREE    Appends and prints: Task N: start tree=<id> at=<iso>
set -euo pipefail
[ $# -eq 3 ] || { echo "usage: task-start LEDGER N TREE" >&2; exit 2; }
ledger=$1 n=$2 tree=$3
[ -f "$ledger" ] || { echo "task-start: no ledger at $ledger; write its header line first" >&2; exit 2; }
case "$n" in ''|*[!0-9]*) echo "task-start: N must be a number: $n" >&2; exit 2 ;; esac
printf '%s' "$tree" | grep -Eq '^[0-9a-f]{40}([0-9a-f]{24})?$' ||
  { echo "task-start: TREE must be a full object id: $tree" >&2; exit 2; }
line="Task $n: start tree=$tree at=$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)"
printf '%s\n' "$line" >> "$ledger"
printf '%s\n' "$line"
  • Step 5: Write scripts/task-done
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run a task's test command; only on exit 0 append the completion line to the ledger.
# Usage: task-done LEDGER N TREE -- CMD...    Exits with CMD's code.
# Adapted from superpowers executing-plans/scripts/task-done (MIT).
set -euo pipefail
if [ $# -lt 5 ] || [ "$4" != "--" ]; then echo "usage: task-done LEDGER N TREE -- CMD..." >&2; exit 2; fi
ledger=$1 n=$2 tree=$3
shift 4
[ -f "$ledger" ] || { echo "task-done: no ledger at $ledger" >&2; exit 2; }
case "$n" in ''|*[!0-9]*) echo "task-done: N must be a number: $n" >&2; exit 2 ;; esac
printf '%s' "$tree" | grep -Eq '^[0-9a-f]{40}([0-9a-f]{24})?$' ||
  { echo "task-done: TREE must be a full object id: $tree" >&2; exit 2; }

logdir="$(dirname "$ledger")/logs"
mkdir -p "$logdir"
log="$logdir/task-$n.log"
cmd=''
for a in "$@"; do
  case "$a" in
    *[[:space:]\"\;\|\&\']*) cmd="$cmd '$a'" ;;
    *) cmd="$cmd $a" ;;
  esac
done
cmd=${cmd# }

rc=0
"$@" > "$log" 2>&1 || rc=$?
tail -n 5 "$log"
if [ "$rc" -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "task-done: command exited $rc; Task $n not recorded (log: $log)" >&2
  exit "$rc"
fi
line="Task $n: complete tree=$tree test=$cmd exit=0 review=approved"
printf '%s\n' "$line" >> "$ledger"
echo "ledger: $line"

Task 5: Floor scan (G7 as a script)

Files:

  • Create: scripts/floor-scan
  • Test: tests/scripts/floor-scan.bats

Interfaces:

  • Consumes: a unified diff file, for example a wt-diff package or .patch (Task 2).
  • Produces: floor-scan DIFF_FILE prints one line per hit, path:line<TAB>class<TAB>text, with class one of silenced-checker, test-made-easier, test-deleted, assertion-removed, unfinished-work, debug-print; exit 0 clean, 1 hits, 2 unreadable input. Line numbers are new-file lines, except assertion-removed (old-file line) and test-deleted (0). unfinished-work and debug-print skip .md, .mdx, .txt, .rst and .adoc files. Consumed by self-review (Task 17) and, in M3, by the G7 Stop hook.
  • Step 1: Write the failing tests
#!/usr/bin/env bats
# tests/scripts/floor-scan.bats

bats_require_minimum_version 1.5.0
load ../helpers/wt

setup() {
  wt_test_env
  REPO="$TMP/repo"
  make_fixture "$REPO"
  cd "$REPO"
  mkdir -p test src
  printf "test('sum', () => {\n  expect(sum(1, 2)).toBe(3);\n  expect(sum(0, 0)).toBe(0);\n});\n" > test/sum.test.js
  printf "test('old', () => {\n  expect(1).toBe(1);\n});\n" > test/old.test.js
  printf "export function run() {\n  return 1;\n}\n" > src/a.js
  printf '# Readme\n' > README.md
  git add . && git commit --quiet -m base
}

scan() {
  git add -A
  git diff --cached > "$TMP/d.diff"
  run --separate-stderr bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/floor-scan" "$TMP/d.diff"
}

has() { printf '%s\n' "$output" | grep -qE "$1"; }

@test "each floor move is reported with its class" {
  printf "test('sum', () => {\n  expect(sum(1, 2)).toBe(3);\n});\ntest.skip('later', () => {});\n" > test/sum.test.js
  rm test/old.test.js
  printf "export function run() {\n  // eslint-disable-next-line no-console\n  console.log('debug');\n  try { go(); } catch (e) {}\n  // TODO: handle errors\n  return 1;\n}\n" > src/a.js
  printf '# Readme\nTODO: write docs\n' > README.md
  scan
  [ "$status" -eq 1 ]
  has $'^src/a\\.js:2\tsilenced-checker\t'
  has $'^src/a\\.js:3\tdebug-print\t'
  has $'^src/a\\.js:4\tunfinished-work\t'
  has $'^src/a\\.js:5\tunfinished-work\t'
  has $'^test/sum\\.test\\.js:4\ttest-made-easier\t'
  has $'^test/sum\\.test\\.js:3\tassertion-removed\t'
  has $'^test/old\\.test\\.js:0\ttest-deleted\t'
  [ "$(printf '%s\n' "$output" | grep -c '^README\.md:')" -eq 0 ]
}

@test "a clean diff exits 0 with no output" {
  printf "export function run() {\n  return 2;\n}\n" > src/a.js
  scan
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]
  [ -z "$output" ]
}

@test "focused tests count as test-made-easier" {
  printf "test.only('sum', () => {\n  expect(sum(1, 2)).toBe(3);\n  expect(sum(0, 0)).toBe(0);\n});\n" > test/sum.test.js
  scan
  [ "$status" -eq 1 ]
  has $'^test/sum\\.test\\.js:1\ttest-made-easier\t'
}

@test "an unreadable input exits 2" {
  run --separate-stderr bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/floor-scan" "$TMP/missing.diff"
  [ "$status" -eq 2 ]
}
