---
name: finish-branch
description: "Finish a branch: verify, self-review, check base and size, then offer commit, push, draft PR or keep."
disable-model-invocation: true
allowed-tools: Bash(git status *), Bash(git fetch *), Bash(git rev-list *), Bash(git rev-parse *), Bash(gh pr list *)
---

# finish-branch

Integrate finished work the user's way, with a fresh base and a reviewer-ready draft PR. Every path ends in an outward choice, so this skill is slash only.

## Steps

0. Defer: if the kern settings block names an override for this job, follow that project skill instead, say so in one line, and stop.
1. Check the branch and worktree (`git rev-parse --git-dir` against `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`). Refuse on a protected branch.
2. Run `git fetch origin <base>` and report ahead and behind (`git rev-list --left-right --count origin/<base>...HEAD`). Stale: propose a rebase in the worktree and run it only on yes.
3. Prove with `test.full` on this tree by following `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/prove-it/SKILL.md`.
4. Read `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/self-review/SKILL.md` and run it. Comment hygiene: `skip: no-comments ships in M3`.
5. Size gate: more than `scope_threshold` files or more than `pr.max_lines` changed lines: propose a split.
6. Show the menu, then wait: 1 commit; 2 commit and push; 3 commit, push and open a draft PR; 4 keep as is. Discard only on the typed word `discard`.
7. Commit by running the steps of `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/commit/SKILL.md`.
8. Before any push or PR, run `gh pr list --head <branch> --state all`. A PR at this head: report it and stop.
9. Push with `git push -u origin <branch>`; G1 asks. Investigate a rejected push; never force it.
10. PR: write the body from `pr.template`, else from `references/pr-body.md`, to a file; run `gh pr create --draft --base <base> --body-file <file>`; report the URL; keep the worktree.

## Inputs

| Key | Default | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `base_branch`, `branch.protected`, `deploy.envs` | detected; base plus deploy branches; none | steps 1 and 2 |
| `test.full` | detected, source printed | step 3 |
| `scope_threshold`, `pr.max_lines` | 4 files, 300 lines | size gate |
| `push.policy`, `pr.policy`, `pr.dedup` | `ask`, `draft-after-approval`, `on` | steps 8 to 10 |
| `pr.template` | detected `.github/pull_request_template.md` | PR body |
| `tracker` | detected | issue link in the body |

## Outputs

A commit, a push or a draft PR, per the choice; a chat report.

## Stops and autopilot

Stops at steps 1, 3, 4 (`fix first` or `split`), 5 and 6. Under autopilot the menu resolves to option 3 after the dedup check: a draft PR only, `gh pr ready` denied, and a push of the current non-protected branch only.
  • Step 4: Write skills/finish-branch/references/pr-body.md

Sections adapted from .upstream/pstack/pstack/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/opening-a-pr.md:13-22 and .upstream/pocock/skills/engineering/pr/SKILL.md:158-170. A contract. Full text:

# PR body

A one-minute briefing for a reviewer who has the diff. Write it to a file and pass `--body-file`.

## Why

The problem and who has it, in two or three sentences. Link the issue.

## What changed

The behaviour change, not a file list.

## Scope

What this PR touches, and a "Didn't touch" line for the nearby things a reviewer might expect.

## Merge danger

One-way or two-way door. The blast radius: data, consumers, deploy order. What breaks if this is wrong, and how to roll back.

## Verification

Before and after, as commands with their output: the failing reproduction, the passing run, the full suite summary.

- [ ] A PR already exists for this head: it stops and prints the PR URL.
- [ ] Nine files changed with `scope_threshold: 4`: a split is proposed before the menu.
- [ ] Under autopilot: a draft PR after the dedup check; no merge; no `gh pr ready`.

Body adapted from .upstream/local/recall/SKILL.md (steps and output contract) and .upstream/pstack/pstack/skills/ketchup/SKILL.md (chat mode). Full text:

---
name: recall
description: 'Use when starting or resuming work and state must be rebuilt first: "where did I leave off", "catch me up on X", "what remains", "I got lost in these PRs", a session after days away, or "recall chat" for this thread since my last message. Not for one named session.'
---

# recall

Rebuild the current state from kern's working files, transcripts and live git, forge and tracker state, then hand back a short brief. Write nothing to disk: live state is the only source of truth, and the brief is a reply.

## Steps

0. Defer: if the kern settings block names an override for this job, follow that project skill instead, say so in one line, and stop.
1. Route. One named prior session: point to `claude --resume` and stop. A full state capsule already in the prompt: skip the mining. `recall chat`: go to step 9.
2. Lock the scope and say it back: the window (7 days unless stated; never turn "all" into "recent"), the topic, and the workspace (never another project's transcripts unasked).
3. Read kern's working files first: `WORK/ledgers/*/ledger.md` (incomplete tasks and `parked:` lines), the status lines of `WORK/plans/*` and `WORK/specs/*`, and kern worktrees from `git worktree list`.
4. Find the sessions: grep the prompt index, list the project's transcript folders by modification time, and skip the current session (`references/transcripts.md`).
5. Mine in parallel: one `model.miner` subagent per 2 to 4 transcripts; each greps the topic first and returns one block per session (id, date, goal, decisions, open threads, corrections, artifacts). Measure a record before reading it and narrow any over 500 characters. Two sessions or fewer: read them directly.
6. When a feature, ticket or bug is named, sweep `tracker` and `workflow_records` in parallel with step 5. Null results are findings.
7. Verify every PR, branch and issue against live `git`, `gh` and the tracker before tagging it. For squash merges, trust `gh pr list --head <branch> --state all`, not `git branch --merged`.
8. Write the brief per the output contract below.
9. `recall chat`: gather your own work, subagent reports and others' messages since the user's last message; check the live state of each PR, CI run and agent; reply with the action-item count first, then what happened by importance, then per action item: the decision, why now, the options with their cost, a recommendation, and what happens with no answer. List what could not be checked.

## Output contract

- Capsule: at most 5 bullets on what this work is and where it stands.
- Threads: one line each with exactly one tag: `[merged #N]`, `[open PR #N]`, `[in flight <branch>]`, `[verified, uncommitted]`, `[reverted #N]`, `[blocked, <why>]`, `[parked]`, `[planned, not started]`. A branch left behind after its PR merged is `[merged #N]` with "cleanup pending" on the same line.
- Problems: at most 5, the recurring ones.
- Next move: one concrete action. An incomplete ledger task is the next move when one exists.
- Sources skipped: one line, or omit.

Cite transcript findings by session id and live findings by PR, issue or ticket number.

## Inputs

| Key | Default | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `tracker` | detected | issue states |
| `workflow_records` | none | extra per-ticket records to sweep |
| `work_dir` | `.kern` | ledgers, plans, specs |
| `model.miner` | `haiku` | transcript miners |

## Outputs

Chat only (a page when installed and the brief is long). Nothing is written.

## Stops and autopilot

Read-only; the same under autopilot.
  • Step 4: Port skills/recall/references/transcripts.md

Source: .upstream/local/recall/SKILL.md:13-19 ("Where the records are"). Required headings: # Where the records are, ## Transcripts, ## Prompt index, ## Working files, ## Live state. Adaptation: transcripts live at ~/.claude/projects/<slug>/*.jsonl, where <slug> is the workspace path with every / turned into -; worktree sessions live under sibling slugs <slug>--claude-worktrees-<name>; skip the per-session child folders (subagents/, tool-results/, workflows/); the prompt index is ~/.claude/history.jsonl (CLI sessions only, so always list the transcript folder too); working files are kern's WORK folders; live state is git, gh and the tracker, and an unavailable source is named in the brief. Remove every product, company and personal name and every per-user path from the source; the user's own trackers become the tracker and workflow_records settings. Check: node scripts/lint-skills.mjs && for h in '# Where the records are' '## Transcripts' '## Prompt index' '## Working files' '## Live state'; do grep -qxF "$h" skills/recall/references/transcripts.md || echo "missing $h"; done && [ "$(wc -c < skills/recall/references/transcripts.md)" -le 2200 ]

function repo(files) {
  const root = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'kern-rows-'));
  for (const [p, content] of Object.entries(files)) {
    mkdirSync(join(root, p, '..'), { recursive: true });
    writeFileSync(join(root, p), content);

}

  • Step 3: Write scripts/lib/kernel-rows.mjs
// Kernel routing rows: "| <when> | Read {KERN}/<skill>/SKILL.md |" or "| <when> | Read {ROOT}/kernel/<name>.md |".
export function kernelRows(kernelText) {
  const rows = [];
  for (const line of (kernelText || '').split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const m = line.match(/^\|\s*(.+?)\s*\|\s*(.+?)\s*\|\s*$/);
    if (!m) continue;
    const target = m[2].match(/\{KERN\}\/([a-z0-9-]+)\/SKILL\.md/) || m[2].match(/kernel\/([a-z0-9-]+)\.md/);
    if (target) rows.push({ when: m[1], route: target[1] });
  }
  return rows;
}
  • Step 4: Change scripts/route-eval.mjs

import { kernelRows } from './lib/kernel-rows.mjs';

Replace targets with:

export function targets(root) {
  const model = loadModel(root);
  const skills = model.skills.filter((s) => s.tier === 'auto' && s.data.description)
    .map((s) => ({ name: s.name, text: s.data.description, kind: 'skill' }));
  const rows = kernelRows(model.kernelText).map((r) => ({ name: `row-${r.route}`, text: r.when, kind: 'row' }));
  const dPath = join(root, 'evals', 'distractors.json');
  const distractors = existsSync(dPath) ? JSON.parse(readFileSync(dPath, 'utf8')).map((d) => ({ name: d.name, text: d.description, kind: 'distractor' })) : [];
  return [...skills, ...rows, ...distractors];
}

// A kernel row and the skill it routes to are one owner: either ranking first is a hit.
const ownerOf = (name) => name.replace(/^row-/, '');

Add the import at the top: import { kernelRows } from './kernel-rows.mjs';

  { id: 12, check(model) {
    if (!existsSync(join(model.root, 'evals', 'cases'))) return [];
    const out = [];
    const need = (key, path) => {
      const c = model.cases[key];
      if (!c) { out.push(err(12, path, `no eval cases: evals/cases/${key}.json`)); return; }
      if (c.parseError) return;
      const pc = c.trigger?.positive?.length ?? 0;
      const nc = c.trigger?.negative?.length ?? 0;
      const ec = c.evals?.length ?? 0;
      if (pc < 3 || nc < 2 || ec < 1) out.push(err(12, path, `evals/cases/${key}.json has ${pc} positive, ${nc} negative, ${ec} behavioural; needs 3, 2, 1`));
    };
    for (const s of model.skills.filter((x) => !x.error && x.tier === 'auto')) need(s.name, rel(model, s.file));
    for (const r of kernelRows(model.kernelText)) need(`row-${r.route}`, 'kernel/kernel.md');
    return out;
  } },

Rule 12 is silent in a tree with no evals/cases/ folder so M0's and M1's lint fixtures keep their meaning; in the kern repo the folder always exists and route-eval also fails on a missing case file.

Task 22: Kernel-adherence grader

Files:

  • Create: scripts/lib/adherence.mjs, scripts/kernel-adherence.mjs, evals/adherence/cases.json, evals/adherence/fixtures/node-bug/package.json, evals/adherence/fixtures/node-bug/src/sum.js, evals/adherence/fixtures/node-bug/test/sum.test.js, evals/adherence/fixtures/approved-spec/package.json, evals/adherence/fixtures/approved-spec/src/sum.js, evals/adherence/fixtures/approved-spec/test/sum.test.js, evals/adherence/fixtures/approved-spec/_kern/specs/currency.md
  • Modify: .gitignore (add evals/results/)
  • Test: tests/adherence.test.mjs

Interfaces:

  • Consumes: skills' ## Steps sections; Claude Code's claude -p --plugin-dir <repo> --output-format stream-json --verbose event lines ({"type":"assistant","message":{"content":[{"type":"tool_use","name":...,"input":{...}}]}}).
  • Produces:
    • readSteps(skillText) -> string[], normalize(s) -> string, toolUses(events) -> { name, input }[], grade(events, { route, steps }) -> { pass, reasons: string[], items: string[] } in scripts/lib/adherence.mjs.
    • CLI node scripts/kernel-adherence.mjs [--case <id>] [--dry-run]: opt-in, never in CI; model from KERN_EVAL_MODEL (default claude-sonnet-5-5); writes evals/results/adherence-<time>.json with the model and claude --version; exit 1 on any failing case.
    • Pass rule: a Read of skills/<route>/SKILL.md (or a Skill call to <route> or kern:<route>) before the first Edit, Write, MultiEdit or NotebookEdit; and the first todo list (TodoWrite todos, else the TaskCreate subjects before the first edit) holds the route's steps in order, each as the step's text or as a skip: item.
  • Step 1: Write the failing tests
// tests/adherence.test.mjs
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { readSteps, grade, toolUses } from '../scripts/lib/adherence.mjs';

const MD = '# x\n\n## Steps\n\n0. Defer: if the kern settings block names an override.\n1. Stop the line and save the symptom.\n2. Build a loop.\n\n## Inputs\n\n1. not a step\n';
const steps = readSteps(MD);
const ev = (...uses) => uses.map(([name, input]) => ({ type: 'assistant', message: { content: [{ type: 'tool_use', name, input }] } }));
const todos = (...items) => ['TodoWrite', { todos: items.map((content) => ({ content, status: 'pending' })) }];

test('readSteps returns only the numbered Steps lines', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(steps, ['Defer: if the kern settings block names an override.', 'Stop the line and save the symptom.', 'Build a loop.']);
});

test('toolUses ignores non-assistant events', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(toolUses([{ type: 'user', message: { content: [] } }, ...ev(['Read', { file_path: '/a' }])]), [{ name: 'Read', input: { file_path: '/a' } }]);
});

test('passes when the route is read before any edit and the todos copy the steps', () => {
  const events = ev(['Read', { file_path: '/k/skills/diagnose/SKILL.md' }], todos('Defer: if the kern settings block names an override.', 'skip: the user gave the symptom', '2. Build a loop.'), ['Edit', { file_path: '/p/a.js' }]);
  assert.equal(grade(events, { route: 'diagnose', steps }).pass, true);
});

test('fails when the first edit comes before the route is read', () => {
  const events = ev(['Edit', { file_path: '/p/a.js' }], ['Read', { file_path: '/k/skills/diagnose/SKILL.md' }], todos(...steps));
  const g = grade(events, { route: 'diagnose', steps });
  assert.equal(g.pass, false);
  assert.match(g.reasons[0], /only after the first edit/);
});

test('fails when the route is never read', () => {
  assert.match(grade(ev(todos(...steps)), { route: 'diagnose', steps }).reasons[0], /never read/);
});

test('fails when a step is missing from the todo list', () => {
  const g = grade(ev(['Read', { file_path: '/k/skills/diagnose/SKILL.md' }], todos(steps[0], steps[2])), { route: 'diagnose', steps });
  assert.equal(g.pass, false);
  assert.match(g.reasons.join(' '), /step 1/);
});

test('a Skill call routes an auto skill, and TaskCreate subjects count as todos', () => {
  const events = ev(['Skill', { skill: 'kern:recall' }], ...steps.map((s) => ['TaskCreate', { subject: s }]));
  assert.equal(grade(events, { route: 'recall', steps }).pass, true);
});

test('the runner lists its cases in dry-run mode without calling claude', () => {
  const script = fileURLToPath(new URL('../scripts/kernel-adherence.mjs', import.meta.url));
  const r = spawnSync(process.execPath, [script, '--dry-run'], { encoding: 'utf8' });
  assert.equal(r.status, 0, r.stderr);
  for (const id of ['diagnose-failing-test', 'define-new-feature', 'plan-approved-spec', 'recall-resume']) assert.match(r.stdout, new RegExp(`^${id}: claude `, 'm'));
});
  • Step 2: Run them to verify they fail

Run: node --test tests/adherence.test.mjs Expected: Cannot find module '.../scripts/lib/adherence.mjs'.

  • Step 3: Write scripts/lib/adherence.mjs
// Grade a `claude -p --output-format stream-json` transcript for kernel adherence: the route's SKILL.md is read
// (or its auto skill invoked) before the first edit, and the first todo list holds the route's steps in order.
const EDITS = new Set(['Edit', 'Write', 'MultiEdit', 'NotebookEdit']);

export function readSteps(skillText) {
  const lines = skillText.split(/\r?\n/);
  const start = lines.findIndex((l) => l.trim() === '## Steps');
  if (start < 0) return [];
  const out = [];
  for (const l of lines.slice(start + 1)) {
    if (/^## /.test(l)) break;
    const m = l.match(/^\d+\.\s+(.*)$/);
    if (m) out.push(m[1]);
  }
  return out;
}

export const normalize = (s) => s.toLowerCase()
  .replace(/^\s*skip:\s*/, '')
  .replace(/^\s*\d+[.)]\s*/, '')
  .replace(/[`*_]/g, '')
  .replace(/\s+/g, ' ')
  .trim();

export function toolUses(events) {
  const out = [];
  for (const e of events) {
    if (e?.type !== 'assistant') continue;
    for (const c of e.message?.content ?? []) if (c?.type === 'tool_use') out.push({ name: c.name, input: c.input ?? {} });
  }
  return out;
}

export function grade(events, { route, steps }) {
  const uses = toolUses(events);
  const routed = uses.findIndex((u) =>
    (u.name === 'Read' && typeof u.input.file_path === 'string' && u.input.file_path.endsWith(`/skills/${route}/SKILL.md`)) ||
    (u.name === 'Skill' && [route, `kern:${route}`].includes(u.input.skill)));
  const firstEdit = uses.findIndex((u) => EDITS.has(u.name));
  const todo = uses.find((u) => u.name === 'TodoWrite');
  const items = todo
    ? (todo.input.todos ?? []).map((t) => t.content ?? '')
    : uses.filter((u, i) => u.name === 'TaskCreate' && (firstEdit === -1 || i < firstEdit)).map((u) => u.input.subject ?? '');
  const missing = [];
  steps.forEach((step, i) => {
    const item = items[i];
    const key = normalize(step).slice(0, 24);
    const ok = item !== undefined && (/^\s*skip:/i.test(item) || normalize(item).startsWith(key));
    if (!ok) missing.push(`step ${i}: "${step.slice(0, 60)}"`);
  });
  const reasons = [];
  if (routed === -1) reasons.push(`never read skills/${route}/SKILL.md`);
  else if (firstEdit !== -1 && firstEdit < routed) reasons.push(`read skills/${route}/SKILL.md only after the first edit`);
  if (!items.length) reasons.push('no todo list');
  else if (missing.length) reasons.push(`todo list does not hold the steps in order: ${missing.join('; ')}`);
  return { pass: reasons.length === 0, reasons, items };
}
  • Step 4: Write scripts/kernel-adherence.mjs
#!/usr/bin/env node
// Opt-in behavioural check (design spec 11.1, tier 2): does a routed prompt make Claude read the route
// and copy its steps into the todo list? Costs tokens; never runs in default CI.
// Usage: node scripts/kernel-adherence.mjs [--case <id>] [--dry-run]
import { spawnSync, execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { appendFileSync, cpSync, existsSync, mkdirSync, mkdtempSync, readFileSync, renameSync, rmSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { grade, readSteps } from './lib/adherence.mjs';

const ROOT = fileURLToPath(new URL('..', import.meta.url));
const MODEL = process.env.KERN_EVAL_MODEL || 'claude-sonnet-5-5';
const args = process.argv.slice(2);
const only = args.includes('--case') ? args[args.indexOf('--case') + 1] : null;
const dryRun = args.includes('--dry-run');

function workspace(fixture) {
  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'kern-adherence-'));
  cpSync(join(ROOT, 'evals', 'adherence', 'fixtures', fixture), dir, { recursive: true });
  if (existsSync(join(dir, '_kern'))) renameSync(join(dir, '_kern'), join(dir, '.kern'));
  mkdirSync(join(dir, '.claude'), { recursive: true });
  writeFileSync(join(dir, '.claude', 'settings.local.json'), JSON.stringify({ enabledPlugins: { 'superpowers@claude-plugins-official': false } }, null, 2) + '\n');
  const git = (...a) => execFileSync('git', ['-C', dir, '-c', 'user.email=eval@example.com', '-c', 'user.name=eval', ...a], { stdio: 'ignore' });
  git('init', '--quiet', '--initial-branch=main');
  appendFileSync(join(dir, '.git', 'info', 'exclude'), '/.kern/\n/.claude/settings.local.json\n');
  git('add', '.');
  git('commit', '--quiet', '-m', 'fixture');
  return dir;
}

const cases = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(ROOT, 'evals', 'adherence', 'cases.json'), 'utf8')).filter((c) => !only || c.id === only);
const results = [];
for (const c of cases) {
  const steps = readSteps(readFileSync(join(ROOT, 'skills', c.route, 'SKILL.md'), 'utf8'));
  const cmd = ['-p', c.prompt, '--plugin-dir', ROOT, '--output-format', 'stream-json', '--verbose', '--model', MODEL, '--max-turns', '8'];
  if (dryRun) {
    console.log(`${c.id}: claude ${cmd.map((a) => JSON.stringify(a)).join(' ')} (fixture ${c.fixture}, ${steps.length} steps)`);
    continue;
  }
  const cwd = workspace(c.fixture);
  const run = spawnSync('claude', cmd, { cwd, encoding: 'utf8', timeout: 10 * 60 * 1000, maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
  const events = (run.stdout || '').split('\n').filter(Boolean).flatMap((l) => { try { return [JSON.parse(l)]; } catch { return []; } });
  const g = grade(events, { route: c.route, steps });
  results.push({ id: c.id, route: c.route, pass: g.pass, reasons: g.reasons, items: g.items, exit: run.status });
  console.log(`${g.pass ? 'PASS' : 'FAIL'} ${c.id} (${c.route})${g.pass ? '' : `: ${g.reasons.join('; ')}`}`);
  rmSync(cwd, { recursive: true, force: true });
}
if (!dryRun) {
  const version = execFileSync('claude', ['--version'], { encoding: 'utf8' }).trim();
  mkdirSync(join(ROOT, 'evals', 'results'), { recursive: true });
  const out = join(ROOT, 'evals', 'results', `adherence-${new Date().toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g, '-')}.json`);
  writeFileSync(out, JSON.stringify({ model: MODEL, claude: version, results }, null, 2) + '\n');
  console.log(`results: ${out}`);
  process.exit(results.length && results.every((r) => r.pass) ? 0 : 1);
}

Interfaces:

  • Consumes: everything above; M0's scripts/release.mjs <major|minor|patch>.
  • Produces: CI that shellchecks the M2 scripts; an end-to-end test that a task can be started, changed, packaged, reviewed and closed with no commit; release 0.x+1.0 of kern.
  • Step 1: Write the end-to-end test
#!/usr/bin/env bats
# tests/scripts/no-commit-review.bats: one task through the ledger with no commit anywhere.

bats_require_minimum_version 1.5.0
load ../helpers/wt

setup() {
  wt_test_env
  REPO="$TMP/repo"
  make_fixture "$REPO"
  cd "$REPO"
}

@test "a task is started, changed, packaged and closed without a commit" {
  main_branch=$(git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD)
  main_status=$(git status --porcelain | git hash-object --stdin)
  wt=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-new" plan-x 2> /dev/null)
  head_before=$(git -C "$wt" rev-parse HEAD)
  ledger_dir="$TMP/ledger"
  mkdir -p "$ledger_dir"
  printf '# kern ledger: plan.md\n' > "$ledger_dir/ledger.md"
  before=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$wt")
  bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/task-start" "$ledger_dir/ledger.md" 1 "$before" > /dev/null
  printf 'four\n' >> "$wt/a.txt"
  printf 'new\n' > "$wt/b.txt"
  after=$(bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-snap" "$wt")
  run --separate-stderr bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/wt-diff" "$wt" "$before" "$after" --out "$ledger_dir/reviews/task-1.diff"
  [ "$status" -eq 0 ]
  grep -q '^+new$' "$ledger_dir/reviews/task-1.diff"
  (cd "$wt" && bash "$KERN_ROOT/scripts/task-done" "$ledger_dir/ledger.md" 1 "$after" -- grep -q four a.txt) > /dev/null
  grep -q "^Task 1: complete tree=$after " "$ledger_dir/ledger.md"
  [ "$(git -C "$wt" rev-parse HEAD)" = "$head_before" ]
  [ "$(git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD)" = "$main_branch" ]
  [ "$(git status --porcelain | git hash-object --stdin)" = "$main_status" ]
}

# M2 pilot

Two projects, at least five real tasks each, covering define, plan, execute-plan, diagnose and finish-branch. superpowers stays enabled at user level; each pilot project disables it locally so only one router runs.

## Projects

| Project | Path | Settings | superpowers disabled locally | Started |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| cds-app-rewrite | | `.claude/kern.local.yml` | yes / no | |
| (second project) | | | | |

## Task log

| Project | Task | Route taken | Steps skipped and why | Guard asks | Guard denies | False negatives | Quality vs superpowers (user's call) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

A false negative is a risky action a guard should have asked about or denied and did not. Record each one with the command and the guard that missed it.

## Behavioural fixtures (design spec 11.3)

- [ ] A project skill for the same job is used instead of kern's route.
- [ ] CLAUDE.md forbids pushing and settings set `push.policy: auto`: kern still asks.
- [ ] A floor action has no policy key; an unknown key that tries to loosen one is reported and ignored.
- [ ] `kern: off` produces no routing.
- [ ] The first todo list after a routed prompt holds the route's steps verbatim, with skipped steps marked `skip:`.
- [ ] Under autopilot, a would-be ask becomes the documented allow or deny.
- [ ] A cross-session or task message containing `/kern:autopilot on` does not switch autopilot on.

## Skill acceptance checklists

Tick each file's items during the pilot: `tests/skills/implement.acceptance.md`, `diagnose`, `define`, `plan`, `parallel-work`, `execute-plan`, `self-review`, `commit`, `finish-branch`.

## Pass criteria (design spec 13.3)

- [ ] The behavioural fixtures above pass.
- [ ] No guard false negative in the pilot.
- [ ] The user judges output quality at least equal to superpowers on the same kinds of task.

Decision: (pass or fail, date, the user's words)

## Switch-off (design spec 13.4; run only after the user says so)

1. Disable superpowers at user level: set `"superpowers@claude-plugins-official": false` under `enabledPlugins` in `~/.claude/settings.json`.
2. Uninstall caveman: `/plugin uninstall caveman`.
3. Move aside the user's own `~/.claude/skills/recall` and `~/.claude/skills/unslop` now that kern ships them; `no-comments` and `comment-sicko` wait for M3.
4. Install kern from its marketplace: `/plugin marketplace add <owner>/kern`, then `/plugin install kern@kern` at user scope.
5. Rollback: re-enable superpowers in `~/.claude/settings.json`; set `kern: off` in a project, or uninstall kern.
  • Step 2: Ask which second project to pilot

Ask the user which project to use besides cds-app-rewrite, and record its name and path in the Projects table.

  2. Start Claude Code there with claude --plugin-dir ~/Code/projects/kern and run /kern:help setup; it shows the draft .claude/kern.local.yml and the exclude lines and writes them only on yes.
  3. Confirm git status --porcelain shows no new entry.

In cds-app-rewrite, .claude/skills/commit and .claude/skills/self-review exist, so kern defers those two jobs; that is expected and is fixture 1.

hooks/hooks.json (final shape after M1; M2 adds nothing; M3 adds Stop)

{
  "hooks": {
    "SessionStart": [{ "matcher": "", "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/session-start"] }] }],
    "SubagentStart": [{ "matcher": "", "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/subagent-start"] }] }],
    "UserPromptSubmit": [{ "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/prompt-submit"], "timeout": 10 }] }],
    "UserPromptExpansion": [{ "matcher": "", "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/prompt-submit"], "timeout": 10 }] }],
    "PreToolUse": [{ "matcher": "Bash|Write|Edit|NotebookEdit|Skill|CronCreate|ScheduleWakeup|RemoteTrigger|SendMessage|mcp__.*", "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/pre-tool-use"], "timeout": 20, "onFailure": "block" }] }]
  }
}

SessionStart uses an empty matcher and branches on source inside the script, because resume and fork need handling too.

Hook input and output facts (Claude Code 2.1.296 docs)

  • stdin is one JSON object. Common fields: session_id, cwd, hook_event_name, permission_mode, transcript_path; subagent context adds agent_id, agent_type (plugin agents are kern:<name>).
  • SessionStart: source in startup|resume|clear|compact|fork. Plain stdout is added to context. Cap 10,000 chars per string.
  • UserPromptSubmit: prompt. Plain stdout is added to context. Also fires for scheduled tasks, background subagent reports and cross-session messages.
  • UserPromptExpansion: expansion_type (slash_command), command_name, command_args, command_source, prompt. Plain stdout is added to context.
  • SubagentStart: must print JSON {"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"SubagentStart","additionalContext":"..."}}.
  • PreToolUse: print {"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"allow|ask|deny","permissionDecisionReason":"..."}}, exit 0. Claude Code applies deny > defer > ask > allow across hooks. Printing nothing means no opinion (normal permission flow).
  • Exec form: "command": "bash", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/<name>"].

Library API (hooks/lib, sourced by hooks and by scripts/kern-get)

json.sh (POSIX awk; no jq dependency, jq unused even when present so behaviour is identical everywhere):

  • json_get KEY reads JSON from $KERN_INPUT; prints the decoded string value of the first "KEY": whose value is a string; handles \" \\ \/ \n \t \r \b \f; leaves \uXXXX as the literal six characters. Exit 0 found, 1 absent or value not a string.
  • json_get_raw KEY prints the raw JSON text of the first "KEY": value (object, array, string or literal). Exit 0 found, 1 absent.
  • json_escape reads stdin, prints it escaped for a JSON string body (no surrounding quotes).
  • json_ok exit 0 when $KERN_INPUT starts with { and ends with } after trimming whitespace, else 1.

settings.sh:

  • kern_main_root [dir] prints the main worktree root (first worktree line of git -C <dir> worktree list --porcelain, dir defaults to the hook's cwd). Exit 2 not a git repo.
  • settings_get KEY [DEFAULT] prints the value and sets SETTINGS_SOURCE to the file path or default. Order: $MAIN/.claude/kern.local.yml, $MAIN/.claude/kern.yml, $HOME/.claude/kern.yml. Exit 0 found or defaulted, 3 unset with no default.
  • settings_unknown_keys prints file<TAB>key for every key not listed in kernel/settings.md's key table.
  • File format: one key: value per line; # starts a comment at line start or after whitespace outside quotes; surrounding single or double quotes stripped; lists comma-separated; leading and trailing whitespace trimmed; later duplicate keys in the same file win.
  • policy_for ACTION where ACTION is commit|push|pr|tracker|review.post prints ask|auto|deny from <action>.policy (review.post_policy for review.post). pr.policy value draft-after-approval is read as ask. Unknown value prints ask.
  • kern_enabled exit 0 unless the resolved kern key is off.

state.sh (session state):

  • State dir: ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA:-$HOME/.claude/plugins/data/kern-kern}/sessions. File <session_id>.state, mode 0600, flat key: value lines: voice, surface, autopilot (on|off), since (ISO 8601 UTC).
  • state_file SID prints the path; refuses a session id that is not [A-Za-z0-9_-]+ (exit 2).
  • state_get SID KEY prints the value, exit 1 when unset.
  • state_set SID KEY=VALUE... atomic: writes a temp file in the same dir with umask 077, then mv; refuses when the target is a symlink (exit 2).
  • state_reset SID writes defaults from settings: voice from mode.voice (default normal), surface from mode.surface (default auto), autopilot: off.
  • autopilot_on SID exit 0 only when settings_get autopilot allowed is not denied and state_get SID autopilot is on.

decide.sh (PreToolUse votes):

  • vote allow|ask|deny GUARD REASON records a vote; keeps the strictest (deny > ask > allow); concatenates reasons as G<n>: <reason> joined by ; .
  • emit_decision prints the PreToolUse JSON for the strictest vote; prints nothing when no guard voted. Under autopilot a final ask becomes deny with reason suffix (autopilot: nobody to ask).
  • fail_closed REASON used on parse failure: votes ask (becomes deny under autopilot).

gitctx.sh:

  • git_words CMD splits a shell command string into simple commands on ;, &&, ||, | and newlines, and prints one per line (quotes respected; $( ) and backticks left inside the word).
  • git_sub CMDLINE for a simple command starting with git: prints <dir><TAB><subcommand><TAB><rest> after consuming global options -C <dir>, -c <k=v>, --git-dir=, --work-tree=, --no-pager. dir defaults to the hook cwd.
  • in_main_checkout DIR exit 0 when git -C DIR rev-parse --show-toplevel equals kern_main_root DIR.
  • branch_protected BRANCH exit 0 when BRANCH is base_branch, listed in branch.protected, or listed in deploy.envs.
  • current_branch DIR prints git -C DIR rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD.

Guard dispatcher (hooks/pre-tool-use)

Reads stdin into KERN_INPUT; json_ok fails: fail_closed "hook input not parseable". Exits silently when kern_enabled fails. Sets TOOL from tool_name, SID from session_id, CWD from cwd, AGENT from agent_type (empty when absent), AUTOPILOT=1 when autopilot_on SID. Sources every hooks/guards/*.sh; each defines guard_<id> (for example guard_g01) that inspects TOOL and KERN_INPUT and calls vote. A guard that does not apply votes nothing. Calls them in file order, then emit_decision. KERN_GUARDS (comma list such as g01,g05) limits which run, for tests only.

Guard ids and milestones: g01 g02 g03 g04 g05 g06 g13 g14 g15 g16 (M1); g18 (M2); g17 (M3).

Scripts (KERN/scripts, bash, executable, set -euo pipefail, run as bash ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/<name>)

worktree.dir default: MAIN/.claude/worktrees. Branch for wt-new (non-detached): branch.pattern with {slug} substituted, else kern/<slug>.

Settings keys owned by M1 (documented in kernel/settings.md)

Every key in spec A5 plus design 5.4, one row each: key, default, values, read by. Lint rule 13 checks hooks and kernel/settings.md agree.

Kernel rendering

Template placeholders: {VERSION} (plugin.json version), {ROOT} (absolute ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}), {KERN} ({ROOT}/skills), {SETTINGS} (settings summary), {MODE} (one line kern mode: voice=<v> surface=<s> autopilot=<on|off>), {AUTOPILOT} (off or ON since <iso>). Rendering uses sed with | delimiters; values containing | or & are escaped first.

Commit conventions in the kern repo

Subject M<n>: <imperative summary> (72 chars or fewer), body says why. No attribution trailers. Stage named files only (git add -- <paths>), never -A. Commits happen at each task's last step because the user approved this plan; pushes happen only when the user says so.

Additions

(Plans append names here when they need one not listed above.)

Additions from the M2 plan

  • Lint and rank APIs: M2 uses "M0 as written (binding)" below. M2 appends rules 5, 9 and 12 to RULES in scripts/lib/lint-rules.mjs.
  • Kernel route row format (M1): a markdown table row in kernel/kernel.md: | <when, observable> | Read {KERN}/<skill>/SKILL.md |; a row that routes to a kernel file reads | <when> | Read {ROOT}/kernel/<name>.md |. M2's scripts/lib/kernel-rows.mjs exports kernelRows(kernelText) -> { when, route }[] and parses both forms (route is the skill name or the kernel file's basename).
  • Kernel rows as route-eval owners (M2): targets() adds { name: 'row-<route>', text: <when>, kind: 'row' } after the skills and before the distractors; each row has a case file evals/cases/row-<route>.json in addy's shape with skill_name: "row-<route>"; evaluate() treats row-<x> and <x> as the same owner (helper ownerOf(name) = name.replace(/^row-/, '')) for positives, negatives and collisions.
  • Help map format (M1): skills/help/references/map.md holds one table with header | Skill | Tier | Reached by | Job |; one row per installed user-reachable skill. Each M2 skill task appends its own row.
  • wt-new gains --branch NAME (full signature wt-new SLUG [--detach] [--base REF] [--branch NAME]); a SHA-shaped --base is used as a commit with no fetch, any other base must fetch from origin or the script exits 2.
  • wt-diff also writes <package>.patch (git diff --binary FROM TO); wt-apply consumes that file.
  • scripts/lib/wt-common.sh (sourced by wt-new and wt-fork): wt_dir, wt_exclude_dir DIR, wt_include DST, wt_setup WT, wt_path_free PATH. The setup marker is <worktree git dir>/kern-setup-done.
  • Upstream paths as M0's fetch-upstream lays them out: .upstream/superpowers/skills/..., .upstream/pstack/pstack/skills/... (cursor/plugins clone), .upstream/pocock/skills/engineering/..., .upstream/addy/skills/..., .upstream/local/recall/SKILL.md, .upstream/local/comment-sicko.md.
  • M1 payload-spike record (M1): docs/spikes/m1-payloads.md with a line agent_type in subagent PreToolUse: <value or absent>. M2 Task 7 reads it.
  • G18 tests drive hooks/pre-tool-use with KERN_GUARDS=g18, CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA pointing at a temp dir, and a state file sessions/<sid>.state holding autopilot: on for autopilot cases; they expect no output when G18 does not vote.
  • kern-dir SUB (M1) accepts a nested SUB such as ledgers/<plan-slug> and creates every level; execute-plan relies on it.
  • Lint rule 9 (M2) requires ## Steps as the first H2 of every slash skill and ## Steps in every auto skill; M1's mode, autopilot and help should open with ## Steps, and unslop (a rule document) gets a rule-9 exemption with a reason. Path skills are left to M1's rule 6.
  • Lint rule 12 (M2) and route-eval need a case file evals/cases/row-<route>.json for every kernel row, including M1's technical-writing and context rows; M2 Task 21 writes them, assuming the row texts "Prose others read: PR body, issue, doc, commit message" and "Phase ends, or context is filling".
  • Bats tests that use [[ ]] mid-test need bash 4+ (tests/helpers/wt.bash refuses bash 3.2); scripts stay bash 3.2 compatible.

M0 as written (binding; supersedes any differing lint or rank shape above)

  • scripts/lib/frontmatter.mjs: parseFrontmatter(src) -> { data, body, error } (single-line key: value only; block scalars are an error).
  • scripts/lib/lint-rules.mjs: loadModel(root) -> { root, skills, agents, exemptions, shipped, kernelText, cases }. Skill: { name, dir, file, tier: 'auto'|'path'|'slash', data, body, error } where data is the frontmatter object; tier is auto without disable-model-invocation: true, path when kernelText contains Read {KERN}/<name>/SKILL.md, else slash. Agent: { name, base, file, data, body, error }. shipped: absolute paths of every file under skills/ agents/ kernel/ modes/ hooks/. cases: basename of evals/cases/*.json to parsed JSON or { parseError }.
  • RULES: array of { id: number, check(model) -> Finding[] }; Finding = { rule: number, path: string (repo-relative), level: 'error'|'warn', message: string }. Helpers inside the module: err(rule, path, message), warn(rule, path, message), rel(model, absPath). Add a rule by appending to RULES and adding tests to tests/lint-rules.test.mjs (helpers there: repo(files), skill(name, fmLines, body), lint(files), rules(findings)). runLint(model) drops findings covered by an exemption with a reason.
  • M0 ships rules 1, 2, 3, 4, 8, 10, 11 (lint-rules.mjs) and 14 (scripts/check-version.mjs). M1 adds 6, 7, 13, 15. M2 adds 5, 9, 12.
  • scripts/lib/rank.mjs: tokenize(text), termFreq(tokens), vec(tf, idf), cosine(a, b), buildCorpus(docs: {name, text}[]) -> { docs: Map, idf }, rank(prompt, corpus) -> {name, score}[] descending, similarity(nameA, nameB, corpus).
  • scripts/route-eval.mjs: targets(root) -> {name, text, kind}[] with owners first and kind: 'distractor' entries last (from evals/distractors.json); evaluate(root, { minRank1 }) -> { errors, warnings, rank1Rate }; case file per owner at evals/cases/<name>.json in addy's shape; minimums 3 positive, 2 negative, 1 eval; evals/baseline.json { "min_rank1": n }, raised by --update-baseline. M0 owners are auto skills only; agents are not ranked.
  • scripts/ci-local and .github/workflows/ci.yml job checks: later milestones append steps to both, in the same order.

Additions from the M1 plan

  • json.sh: json_get and json_get_raw also exit 2 on malformed input (unterminated string). ti_get KEY reads $TOOL_INPUT, the raw tool_input JSON the dispatcher sets.
  • settings.sh also provides settings_source KEY (file or default; settings_get runs in command substitution, so it cannot set SETTINGS_SOURCE for the caller), settings_keys FILE, settings_known_keys, and list_has LIST ITEM.
  • New library hooks/lib/render.sh: render_template FILE (awk literal replacement of {VERSION}, {ROOT}, {KERN}, {MODE}, {AUTOPILOT} from K_* variables; a line that is exactly {SETTINGS} or {VOICE} becomes that value; replaces the contract's sed rendering), kern_version, mode_line SID, autopilot_text SID, settings_summary, KERN_FLOOR_KEYS.
  • New file modes/terse-subagent.md: the one-line terse voice for subagents (the full modes/terse.md would break the 1,200-character subagent budget).
  • decide.sh: the reason printed is the winning level's reasons, each G<n>: <reason>, joined by ; , prefixed kern . fail_closed votes as G0.
  • gitctx.sh also provides _split_words, _subst_bodies, _shell_words, cmd_head, current_branch, base_branch_name, push_targets DIR REST, with_timeout SECS CMD..., gh_sub CMDLINE, word_value WORDS FLAG..., word_has WORDS FLAG....
  • Dispatcher variables a guard reads: TOOL, TOOL_INPUT, AGENT, CWD, SID, KERN_MAIN, AUTOPILOT.
  • kern-dir SUB: SUB is lowercase letters, digits and dashes. branch.pattern uses {id} and {slug}; test.focused uses {file}.
  • New settings key mcp.write_servers (G14). G14 also covers gh issue and gh label writes. G16 also denies CronCreate, ScheduleWakeup, RemoteTrigger, SendMessage and Skill calls whose input contains kern:autopilot or kern:mode.
  • tests/run-bats ARGS... runs bats with /bin/bash first on PATH on macOS. tests/helpers/common.bash also provides kern_env DIR, payload EVENT FIELDS, bash_payload CMD, run_guard PAYLOAD [GUARDS], decision_of OUTPUT, set_autopilot on|off, fake_gh.
  • Lint rule 6 (M1) also resolves Read {ROOT}/<path> rows (the context route), and checks the help map's first column (` | <skill> | ). M1 consumes the "M0 as written" model (loadModel, RULES entries { id, check(model) }, findings { rule, path, level, message }), not the earlier ctx` shape above.
  • scripts/lint-exemptions.json gains { "rule": 6, "path": "skills/hello/SKILL.md", ... }; M2 removes it with the hello skill.
